     leads further. Before leaving, the code collects the choice: it takes from the array
     of values those whose caption has ✅, and joins them with commas.

The ready masters-exchange bot collects a master's working days, reception hours and list
of services this way. The same trick fits "choose several categories",
"tick the options you need", "which days suit you".

BUTTONS OF PAST MESSAGES STAY WORKING. In a messenger buttons do not disappear from the chat: the subscriber can scroll up and press a button of a message sent half an hour ago. The value of such a button comes to THE block where the subscriber stands now, not to the one where the button was shown.
Hence an error visible only in a live bot: the bot has two choice screens with value buttons — say, classes and tariffs. The client chooses a class, the bot answers "a pass is needed first" and moves them to the tariffs screen. The client again presses a class in the message above — the value goes to the tariff parsing, it is not there, and on every press they get "no such tariff".
A BUTTON VALUE IS NOT ALWAYS A COMMAND. A "Yes/No" button in a settings form is a choice, not a transition: its value is read later as an ANSWER with the template [AnswerID(blockid)] or [Answer] in the next step and put into a list. Such a value need not be parsed by transition conditions. Parsing is needed where a press must lead somewhere or launch something.

RULE: if there is more than one screen with value buttons, each parsing scenario must recognise OTHER screens' values. This is done with one check: look up the incoming value in the list of the neighbouring screen and, if found, lead the subscriber to its step — where the person was aiming. Answering "not found" to a button of your own bot is forbidden.
The same logic applies to returning from a mini app: the answer "webapp:calendar" is caught by a condition on EVERY block, because the subscriber may be standing anywhere at that moment.

AN AI MODEL THAT DOES NOT ANSWER BUT ASSEMBLES AN ORDER. When the client writes a free phrase ("a hearty lunch for three under 2000 without onion, to 45 Lenina St. by 13:00"), the AI model must return NOT TEXT BUT STRICT JSON, which is then parsed by JS code. This is the only way to keep inventions out of the deal:
  1. Three things go into the question: the owner's instruction from a list, THE CATALOG AS ONE STRING (name|price|weight|ingredients, items separated by §) and the client's message. Plus what has already been collected — the client gives the address and phone not in the same message as the dishes.
  2. Ask for an answer as one JSON object without explanations or markdown. Set the format right in the question: items (name and quantity), address, time, phone, comment, a short answer to the client.
  3. The code strips the markdown wrapper, parses the JSON and CHECKS EACH ITEM AGAINST THE CATALOG: only what was found goes into the cart, and at the catalog price. What the AI model invented is set aside separately, so the owner sees what people are missing.
  4. THE SUM AND THE WEIGHT ARE COUNTED BY CODE, not by the AI model: it makes silent mistakes in arithmetic.
  5. ORDER READINESS IS DECIDED BY THE BOT, not by the AI model: no items, the sum below the minimum, no address or phone — the order is not ready, whatever it wrote. Otherwise one day you get "all ready" without an address and a request with empty coordinates.
  6. The cart lives AS A ROW IN A LIST with a status (draft → awaiting payment → in delivery → closed), not in variables: the client writes in three goes, and the owner must see the order.

COURIER DELIVERY: WHAT ANY SUCH BOT MUST HAVE. Yandex Delivery accepts COORDINATES, not an address, so between "the client named a street" and "the request is created" there is always a geocoder — both for the client's address and for the warehouse address. The order of steps: the price (check-price) before payment, creating the request (claims/create) after payment, CONFIRMATION (claims/accept with the version from claims/info when the status became ready_for_approval) — without it the courier will not leave — and the status (claims/info) in a tracking round. The geocoder and Delivery keys are issued to the client's organisation: they lie in the settings list and are substituted by a variable, and when something is missing the bot honestly says what exactly does not work.

YANDEX DELIVERY. Coordinates
are mandatory and go as [longitude, latitude] in route_points[].address.coordinates.
In items — pickup_point and dropoff_point (droppof_point is obsolete), cost_value
as a string, size in metres, weight in kg. The header "Accept-Language: ru" is needed
for all methods. After claims/create the request is estimated by itself (new →
estimating → ready_for_approval): confirm with claims/accept with the body
{"version": N} using the version from claims/info when the status became
ready_for_approval — not later than 10 minutes, otherwise failed. request_id: a repeat
with the same code after a break or a 5xx does not create a second request; after a
4xx refusal a new one is needed. The "where is the courier" link — GET claims/tracking-links,
sharing_link of the destination point (409 — no links yet). Cancellation —
claims/cancel-info (free, paid, unavailable), then claims/cancel with the body
{"version", "cancel_state"}. Final statuses: delivered_finish,
returned_finish, cancelled*, failed, estimating_failed, performer_not_found —
on them the tracking round ends. A ready sample — the catalog bot "Delivery
by Yandex Delivery: price, courier, status".

TRACKING A REQUEST IS A ROUND OF TWO BLOCKS: a pass-through block with a "transition without waiting" and a block with a one-minute delay whose scenario asks for the status. A delay from a minute goes to the schedule and does not hold the messenger request. The client is told ONLY A CHANGE of status, and in human words ("the order is with the courier, on the way to you"), not with an API code. The round must end: final statuses plus a counter of checks with a limit from the settings.

SEARCH BY WORD STEM: HOW TO ANSWER LIVE SPEECH WITH TEMPLATES. The list row filter has two checkboxes that together give a simple "search by meaning" without an AI model:
- "SEARCH BY WORD STEM" reduces the words of both sides to stems and compares the sets of stems. "price", "prices", "at the price", "to the prices" — one stem, they match.
- "REVERSE COMPARISON" swaps the sides. WITHOUT IT the condition reads "all words of the client's MESSAGE are in the short phrase of the field" — and almost never matches. With it — "all words of the FIELD are in the client's message", which is what is needed.
Together: a list row matches when each word of its key phrase is in the subscriber's message, in any form and any order. The comparison sign does not matter here — the engine builds a special condition and does not read the sign.

WHAT THE STEM DOES NOT CATCH (checked on the engine, functions SeekBasis and stemmer):
- OTHER PARTS OF SPEECH: "стоимость" and "стоит" (cost / costs) are different stems, "запись" and "записаться" (booking / to book) too, "доставка" and "доставляете" (delivery / you deliver) too. A synonym and a verb form need THEIR OWN ROW with the same answer.
- So in a template list they write ONE WORD per row, not a topic separated by spaces: several words in the field mean "all at once", and "price cost pricelist" will not fire on a single live question.
Choose the keywords for a topic the way the client writes them: "price", "cost", "costs", "pricelist" — four rows with the same answer. This is not duplication but exactly how the search works.

CASHBACK AND POINTS: THE WHOLE REFERRAL SYSTEM. There is no need to create your own lists for points — the engine keeps the balance, payouts and levels itself; the work goes through the "Referral system in Telegram" action:
  1. THE LINK. The "Get referral link" operation puts a ready link into a variable. Inside it is a start parameter built by the engine, so whoever comes by the link is bound to the inviter by themselves — the link need not be recorded anywhere. The balance and the spent amount are shown by the templates [Balance] and [Paid], the number of levels — [Level], the inviter's ClientID — [RefererClientID].
  2. CREDITING THE INVITER. After payment calculate the percentage ({{ #{sum} * 0.05 }}, round with [Round(…)]) and run "Add/Subtract points", FILLING IN THE LEVEL FIELDS "from" and "to". This is the main place of mistakes: "from" and "to" are the NUMBERS OF REFERRAL LEVELS, not a sum and not dates. From 1 to 1 — credit whoever brought them; from 1 to 2 — also whoever brought that person. EMPTY level fields mean "change the balance of the subscriber themselves".
  3. PAYING WITH POINTS. Check "[Balance] >= #{sum}". Enough — "Add/Subtract points" with a NEGATIVE value and without levels (deducting from the buyer), and next to it "Make a payout" for the same sum: it increases [Paid], that is "points used". Not enough — a screen with a choice: pay with money, invite a friend, to the menu.
The ready food-delivery bot with 5% cashback is built this way.

AGENTS AND PARTNERS WITH A REWARD PER DEAL — YOUR OWN LISTS, NOT THE REFERRAL SYSTEM. The referral system is good when money comes as a payment in the bot. When the deal is closed by a manager or a CRM (the sum is known only after negotiations), and the agent is owed a percentage after the return period and a payout on request, build it on the lists "Agents", "Deals", "Payouts":
  1. THE AGENT'S LINK — the start parameter "?start=ag-CODE" (a code without a dash — "A1001", not "A-1001": then the first dash unambiguously separates the prefix from the code, and parsing is a simple pattern comparison). The start block remembers the code in a subscriber variable; a request made under it is the agent's deal. An agent does not credit themselves by their own link: compare the agent's contact with the applicant's.
  2. BINDING BY PHONE. A client stays with whoever brought them first for N days. Compare the phone by the LAST 10 DIGITS ("8 900…" and "+7 900…" are one person) and keep this key in a separate column: then the client's deals are read by a filter on the key, not the whole list. To the second agent — "the client is already in work until such a date" without others' details; an agent does not intercept a client who came by themselves.
  3. CREDITING ON A WIN: the sum × the rate (personal from the agent's row or general from the settings), into the deal row — "Reward", "Accrual: credited", "Available from" = now + hold.
  4. DERIVE THE STATE OF THE MONEY, DO NOT STORE IT. A payout request writes ONE row into "Payouts" with the sum and the deal numbers separated by commas. "In payout" and "paid" are counted in JS over this list: a number in a requested payout — in payout, in a paid one — paid, in a rejected one — available again. There is no loop "mark each deal" in a scenario, and it is not needed this way: a rejection returns the money by itself. One open payout per agent — do not create a second request.
  5. A REFUND before the payout cancels the accrual; after the payout the accrual stays, and the manager gets a warning to withhold manually.
The ready bot "Agent account" is built this way.

A MESSAGE LINE BY A CONDITION — WITHOUT AN EXTRA BRANCH. The expression {{ }} in a text supports the ternary operator, and this saves copies of a screen: {{ ('#{type}' == 'Delivery') ? 'Delivery cost: #{price}' : '' }} — with pickup the line simply disappears. Inside {{ }} jexl calculates, so a double == is written there and both sides are in quotes; in transition conditions the sign is still single.

DELIVERY ZONES WITHOUT A GEOCODER: A POINT ON THE MAP AND POLYGONS IN A LIST. It is asked often ("the delivery price depends on the district"), and it is solved without a single external service:
  1. The client sends a point: "Paperclip 📎 → Location 📍". The engine puts the coordinates into #{client_latitude} and #{client_longitude} — there is no need to ask for an address as text and decode it with a geocoder.
  2. The zones lie in an ordinary "Zones" list: the fields Zone | Price | Coordinates, ONE ROW PER VERTEX. Several rows with the same zone name are a polygon drawn on the map.
  3. A "JS code" action builds polygons from the rows (grouping by name), closes each — the first point is added at the end — and checks whether the point is inside with a ray: an odd number of crossings with the edges means "inside".
  4. It fell into none — the zone "Outside zones" and the default delivery price from the settings. The free-delivery threshold is checked separately: the order sum is above the threshold — the delivery price is set to zero.
The ready zone-delivery bot is built this way. The same trick fits "master's visit", "service area", "we work only in these districts".

AN INCOMING STREAM IS ACCEPTED BY A SCENARIO WITHOUT A BLOCK. The "Waiting for data" action (getdata, type: waiting) makes a scenario a webhook one: the engine finds this action and launches the scenario FROM IT when a request comes to the webhook address (bcontrolup.js with opt.webhook). Neither a block nor a subscriber is needed on this end — this is how a stream of ads, purchases, vacancies, requests from another system is brought into the bot. The client takes the webhook address in the account, at the scenario; the request fields are laid out into variables by the save parameter.
Then such a scenario usually does three things in a row: discards garbage and duplicates (a filter by number with filtervar), writes a row to a list and sends it to the matching subscribers with one "Send to bot" action with array variables.

"DOES NOT MATTER" IN A FILTER IS TWO PASSES, NOT ONE CONDITION. A subscriber whose filter field is empty ("any region", "any category") does not get into a selection with equality to a specific value: empty and non-empty do not match. This cannot be expressed with one condition, so the read and the mailing are done TWICE — first to those whose value matched, then to those whose field is empty. The ready bot of purchases by filter is built this way.

SORTING A FEED — BY A NUMBER, NOT BY A DATE. List sorting compares values as STRINGS, and by a date field dd.mm.yyyy the feed is ordered wrongly: "01.02.2027" turns out smaller than "31.01.2026". Create a separate numeric weight field and put a timestamp in milliseconds into it (Date.now() in a JS action): for all dates of our century it is 13 digits, and strings of the same length compare like numbers. The same field does "bump to the top": write a new mark — the row becomes first. The ready flea-market bot is built this way.

THE SUM OF A FIELD OVER ALL ROWS. The filter counter answers only "found / not found", the array length gives the number of rows, and the sum is taken like this:
  1. in the list read operation the mode "Build a string" is turned on, and the parts are the needed field and an arbitrary string "+";
  2. the output is "100+250+80+";
  3. in the next step variable = {{ #{string} }} — the engine evaluates the expression and cuts the extra plus at the end itself.
This is how the day's revenue, the stock balance, an employee's earnings are counted.

AUTO-REPLIES IN A PERSONAL CHAT (TELEGRAM BUSINESS). The owner connects the bot to their account, and the bot answers clients ON THEIR BEHALF — the client does not know they are writing to a bot. Such a bot is built differently from an ordinary one:
  1. THE PERSONAL-CHAT CHAIN IS ONE BLOCK looped on itself, with the marks "dialog" and "not processed". The client writes free text as many times as they like, and each message must get into the same parsing.
  2. NO BUTTONS AND NO GREETINGS. A bot keyboard in a personal chat gives the bot away at once. The answer text is written in the first person.
  3. TWO WAYS OF ANSWERING, and both are mandatory:
     • the AI model with an INSTRUCTION FROM A LIST — a description of the business and answer rules that the owner edits from the account without opening the constructor;
     • templates by the words of the question (search by word stem + reverse comparison) — for when there is no AI model: the balance ran out, the key was revoked, the gateway is silent.
     Check the refusal by resultvar: "#{result} = 0" — go to the templates. And teach the AI model to refuse by itself: "if the answer is not in the description — return exactly NOANSWER", and catch this marker by a condition and also lead to the templates.
  4. SILENCE IS A LEGITIMATE OUTCOME. Found neither by the AI model nor in the templates — an empty answer (the engine does not send it) plus a message to the owner with the client's question. A brush-off "I did not understand you" in a personal chat is worse than silence.
  5. THE LOG IS WRITTEN ON ALL OUTCOMES, including silence: unanswered questions are a ready list of what is worth adding to the templates.
  6. THE OWNER'S ACCOUNT is a separate chain in the chat of the bot itself, and its first scenario must check '#{_business_chat}' = '1' and leave silently: a client who wrote to the owner must not see the settings.
  7. THE CONNECTION is caught by '#{_business}' = '1' — a convenient moment to send the owner a memo.

AN INVITATION BY LINK: HOW TO ADD AN EMPLOYEE WITHOUT A CODE WORD. Telegram can pass a parameter to the bot right from a link: https://t.me/#{_botuser}?start=CODE. What stands after "start=" the engine puts into the variable #{start} — it exists in the start block's scenario, and only there. Hence the recipe for any "invite an operator, partner, student":
  1. The administrator fills in the employee form (name, topics, rights).
  2. The bot generates a code with the template [GenStr8] and creates a row in the employees list
     IN ADVANCE: the name and settings are filled, ClientID = 0, the active flag
     = 0. This is the place for the future person.
  3. The administrator gets a link like
     "https://t.me/#{_botuser}?start=#{code}" — and forwards it to the employee.
  4. The employee opens the link, the start scenario sees a non-empty #{start},
     finds the row by the "Code" field and writes the real [ClientID],
     [Messenger], [BotID] into it, sets active = 1.
So a person becomes an employee with one tap: no need to dictate a code
or type it by hand. #{start} must be checked in quotes
on both sides: '#{start}' != ''.
A LINK FROM A PLACE: A STAND, AN EXHIBITION, A POINT OF SALE. The same start parameter carries where the person came from: "?start=ev-CODE". The start block recognises the prefix, finds the event in the list and leads straight to the form, and the lead gets a source. Once you have read #{start}, erase it in the same JS (varstore['start'] = ''): otherwise the next "menu" takes the old parameter for a new entry by the link. Give the QR picture for printing as a link button (action: "link") to https://api.qrserver.com/v1/create-qr-code/?size=600x600&data= + encodeURIComponent(link) — no file needs to be kept. Build the link from #{_bottype} and #{_botuser}: in MAX it is https://max.ru/…, in Telegram https://t.me/….
A PROMISE "WE WILL ANSWER WITHIN AN HOUR" IS COUNTED IN WORKING HOURS. A lead at 21:00 must hear "by 10:00 tomorrow", not "by 22:00": otherwise the promise is broken from the start, and the duty robot wakes the manager at night. Write the deadline into the lead's row on creation — answer the person by it and hurry the manager by it.

THE KEY AND THE ACCOUNT ADDRESS LIVE IN THE SETTINGS LIST, NOT IN THE ACTION. A ready bot travels as an export: the client downloads it, it is forwarded, it lies in an archive. Everything written right into an action travels with it — so an API key, a bot token and a password are substituted by a variable from the settings list, not written into the header or the request body. The same with the address of a personal account: "https://myaccount.getcourse.ru" in seven actions means the buyer of the bot will have to edit seven places by hand. The address is a setting just like the key.
Checked on the ready bots of the catalog: four of them carried working keys, one — a whole Telegram bot token. The keys were duplicated: the working request went with a substitution, and next to it the body held a debug copy with the real key. Remove debug copies and messages like "json: #{json}" from a ready bot.

AN EXCHANGE OF PERFORMERS (masters, couriers, tutors, helpers). It is asked often, and it is built from the same parts — here they are, from the ready masters-exchange bot:
- THREE ROLES IN ONE CHAIN: client, performer, administrator. The role is recognised at the start by a filter over the "Masters" and "Administrators" lists, and each has their own start screen. Separate chains for roles are not needed.
- THE PERFORMER'S FORM goes to the administrator IN THE BOT with the buttons "Activate" / "Reject", not into a list for manual editing. Approval is a press: the button carries the form code, the scenario finds the row by it and sets the activation flag.
- PAID PLACEMENT: prices lie in the settings list, payment — with a payment-acceptance block, the term is written in the "Paid until" field. The performers shown to the client are filtered by this field: "Paid until" >= [CurrentDate]. One boundary is enough for a date — the engine converts a date to milliseconds, and the comparison is correct (one boundary is not enough for numbers; they need both).
- CHOOSING SEVERAL VALUES — WITH TWO SCREENS IN A CIRCLE. When the customer needs not one item but several (a host AND a DJ, roof repair AND fence repair, two services in one order), do not make them leave two requests: make a choice screen and a "what else" screen, and let them go round until the "enough" button is pressed. THE BLOCK'S ANSWER IS NEW EACH TIME, so accumulate the choice in a SEPARATE variable with a JS action, and write it to the list, not the answer variable. Do not add a repeat: the same button is pressed twice all the time.
  The filter by the accumulated string works by itself: the value "Host, DJ" with the word stem WITHOUT the reverse comparison looks for the words of the value IN THE performer's field — whoever has ANY of the listed matches. This is that rare case when the dry-run remark about "word stem without reverse comparison" describes what was intended.
  A SCREEN THAT RETURNS TO ITSELF needs "Replace the previous message" (btnsettings.tg.replace and max.replace), otherwise each press leaves a copy of the screen in the chat and the person pokes the one before last. Two different screens in a circle do not have this trouble — each message there is its own. The ready bot of hosts and artists is built this way.
- BUSY DATES — WITH ONE STRING FIELD, NOT A SEPARATE LIST. Where a performer is busy for whole days (a photographer, a host, an instructor, a crew on site), keep in their row the field "Busy dates" with a comma-separated list and filter with the comparison "Does not contain" (notinclude): { pole: "Busy dates", cmp: "notinclude", value: "#{date}" }. In the performer's feed the same comparison is set the OTHER WAY ROUND — with reverse: true, { pole: "Shoot date", cmp: "notinclude", value: "#{my_dates}", reverse: true }. **reverse swaps the sides of ANY comparison, not only of the word stem**: without it the short field would always "not contain" the long list.
  ACCEPT A DATE IN A STRICT FORMAT: the block gets regex: true and regexval: '/^\d{2}\.\d{2}\.\d{4}$/', plus wronganswer. Otherwise "December 25", "25.12.26" and "25.12.2026" land in one field and never compare. Exactly 10 characters also save you from false matches: "05.09.2026" cannot be a piece of "15.09.2026".
  MAKE THE BUSY-DATES SCREEN A TOGGLE: one button "send a date" — a free day becomes busy, a busy one is freed. Two buttons "take" and "free" give twice as many blocks and twice as many errors. Keep empty busyness AS A DASH: the filter "does not contain" on a dash reads unambiguously. (An empty value can be written into a list field and filtered by: a row edit with value: "" clears the field, the filter condition { pole, value: "" } compares with emptiness. But a VARIABLE cannot be cleared by an empty assignment — see below.) The ready photographers bot is built this way.
- TAKE THE VISIT ADDRESS AS A LOCATION, NOT IN WORDS. Where the performer goes to a person on the street (a tow truck, tyre fitting, a courier, a visiting master), an address in words is useless: someone standing on the roadside knows neither the street nor the house. Give the block btnsettings with location: true — and necessarily inline: false, WITHOUT media on this block, otherwise Telegram builds an inline keyboard and the location button is not visible at all. Your own buttons with inline: false go into the same keyboard, but arrive as text — see "CANCEL ON A LOCATION BLOCK" below. The next block must set "Clear the keyboard under the input field" (clearkeyboard: true), otherwise the keyboard stays hanging on all further steps for whoever wrote the address in words.
  THE POINT DOES NOT COME INTO THE ANSWER VARIABLE. The engine puts it into client_latitude and client_longitude — WITHOUT an underscore at the start, unlike the other service variables. The block's answer variable gets nothing from a location, so build a map link from the latitude and longitude with a JS action and keep it as a separate list field:
  varstore['point'] = (lat && lon) ? ('https://yandex.ru/maps/?pt=' + lon + ',' + lat + '&z=17&l=map') : ''
  The order in a Yandex link is LONGITUDE, then latitude. Keep a fallback for whoever writes the address in words: if there is no point, what was written works. The ready mobile car-service bot is built this way.
  TO THE PERFORMER — AS A LINK, NOT AS "A POINT ON THE MAP". Where the address is seen by another person (a driver, a courier, a master), the words "a point on the map" tell them nothing. Show an address with coordinates as a link: <a href="https://yandex.ru/maps/?pt=LONGITUDE,LATITUDE&z=17&l=map">address text</a>, and with two points add a route https://yandex.ru/maps/?rtext=LATITUDE1,LONGITUDE1~LATITUDE2,LONGITUDE2&rtt=auto (in rtext the order is reversed: latitude, then longitude). Build these strings with a JS action once when the order is written and keep them as a list field — in the mailing, the card and the free-orders list substitute the ready field. Remove angle brackets from the address text: they break the message markup.
  CANCEL ON A LOCATION BLOCK. Your own "Cancel" button CAN be put on such a block if it has inline: false: in Telegram it goes into the keyboard under the input field next to the location button. But the press arrives AS THE TEXT of the button name, not as a transition — so add to the block a branch { cond: "'[Answer]' INCLUDE 'Cancel order'", nextmessage: menu } WITHOUT a scenario on the transition, otherwise the text "❌ Cancel order" goes to the address parsing. In MAX the same button works as an ordinary transition.
- ADDRESS COORDINATES BY THE AI MODEL. When a passenger or a customer writes an address in words and there is no geocoder key, the coordinates can be found by the AI model: qb.cfg.ai({ question: "Determine the exact coordinates (latitude and longitude) for the address: #{city}, #{address}. Answer with one line in the format latitude:longitude, decimal numbers with a dot. If there is no such address, answer 0:0. No explanations.", answervar, resultvar }). Rules without which this is dangerous:
  1) ERASE THE PREVIOUS ANSWER BEFORE THE REQUEST (varstore['answer'] = '' in a JS step before it): if the AI model does not answer, the variable keeps the pickup address, the destination lands at the same point, and the price comes out minimal;
  2) parse the answer by code: two numbers, accept a comma in the fractional part as a dot, latitude −90…90, longitude −180…180; "0:0" and numbers without a fractional part of at least three digits ("58:56" is a city, not a house) — there are no coordinates;
  3) substitute the city from the settings: "Lenina, 10" exists in every city;
  4) not found — do not invent: the price is negotiable, offer to send a point on the map;
  5) the AI model may get the house wrong, so show the customer a route link before sending the order, and the performer a note "the point was determined by the AI model, check against the address text".
  Make the choice "AI model / geocoder / negotiable" an owner setting: an AI request without a key is charged to the account balance. The ready bot "Taxi Service AI" is built this way.
- A LIVE POINT ON THE MAP IS LIVE LOCATION SHARING, AND IT IS BUILT DIFFERENTLY. Where a person must see WHERE the car is NOW (a taxi, a car carrier, a courier, a tow truck), a one-time point does not fit: it is outdated in a minute. The subscriber turns on sharing in Telegram (paperclip → Location → Share my live location), and the messenger itself sends coordinate updates for the whole chosen period. The bot CANNOT SEND live sharing — only receive it, and only in Telegram.
  TWO PARTS, AND BOTH ARE MANDATORY: the block has btnsettings.tg.livegeo: true AND an action scenario on THIS SAME block via externalbefore. Without the scenario the points will arrive, but nobody will record them — silently, without an error.
  THE COORDINATES COME INTO client_latitude AND client_longitude (WITHOUT an underscore), and the scenario is launched ON EVERY update, wherever the subscriber is in the bot at that moment. So the branches of such a scenario exit with exittype: 'back' (return to the scenario): a startmes branch would throw the person onto a block dozens of times in a row. Do not send a message on every update either — write the point to a list silently.
  THE SAME SCENARIO ALSO FIRES WHEN THE SUBSCRIBER JUST OPENED THE BLOCK — there are no coordinates yet. As the first step check whether a point came: empty — show instructions on how to turn on sharing and record nothing.
  THE POINT LIVES WHILE THE SHARING GOES ON: the app was closed — the updates ended. Keep the update time ([CurrentDate] [CurrentTime]) next to the point and show it TOGETHER with the point, otherwise the bot passes yesterday's place off as the current one. Show the point itself with the substitution [SendLocation(latitude, longitude)] in the message text. The ready bots "Taxi Mega" (the driver's point) and "Cargo tracking" (the car carrier's point) are built this way.
  THE FIRST POINT OF SHARING COMES AS AN ORDINARY MESSAGE (the text "latitude:longitude", #{_islocation} = 1) — it is parsed by the block the subscriber stands on (the parsing), and the livegeo block's scenario gets only the UPDATES. If you need the first point, parse it in the parsing with the same calculation and answer "point accepted"; do not answer updates.
  COUNT THE DISTANCE TRAVELLED FROM AN ANCHOR — the last accepted point — along the Earth's arc, and do not move the anchor until the person has moved 10 m away: otherwise GPS jitter in place gives kilometres in an evening. A jump faster than a reasonable speed (200 km/h) and a break longer than the auto-stop — without metres, the anchor is moved. Accumulate metres in the person's row, by days — into a separate list on a change of day and on a stop. Take the point time from JS (Date.now()): a sharing message does not carry it. The ready bot "Distance tracker" is built this way.
- A DIRECTORY WITH SEVERAL COLUMNS DRIVES A FORM. When the unit of measurement or the question itself depends on the choice (a well is measured in metres of depth, a fence — in metres of length, a roof — in square metres, a septic tank — in people), do not write one question for all cases and do not breed a block for each kind. Add the columns "Question" and "Unit" next to the name in the directory list: the buttons are built by name, and the "before block" scenario reads the row of the pressed button and puts the question into a variable. THE BLOCK TEXT is this variable (#{volume_question}), and the unit goes to the list together with the answer, so that the cards read as "30 m of depth".
  Such a scenario ends SILENTLY, without a message: the block itself will show it. Be sure to keep a fallback branch — the kind of work could be written in words instead of chosen by a button, and there will be no row in the directory. Adding a kind of work after that = adding a row with three cells; no rule in the bot needs to change. The ready country-works bot is built this way.
- A SHARED DIRECTORY FOR BOTH SIDES INSTEAD OF FREE INPUT. A city, a district, a brand, a category — everything that is later filtered by must be chosen WITH BUTTONS FROM A LIST, and show the same screen to both sides: to the customer in the request form and to the performer in theirs. Then both rows hold exactly one word, and the filter goes by EXACT equality — without a word stem and without "Moscow" versus "msk" and "Moscow, SWAD". Technically this is one scenario assigned by externalbefore to TWO blocks: there is nothing to get out of sync by construction. Leave free input only for what is not filtered by.
- NOT EVERYTHING COLLECTED GOES INTO THE MAILING. Where a person describes something personal — an illness, debts, a divorce, a criminal case — put only the category, the city and the urgency into the mailing to performers. Open the text itself and the documents together with the contact, to the one who responded. This is not excessive caution: the mailing goes to dozens of people and cannot be cancelled. The ready bot of lawyers by field is built this way.
- MATCHING IN TWO PASSES: FIRST EXACT, THEN BROAD. When a performer has both a general specialisation and a narrow one (the type of equipment and brands, the field of law and the courts, the cuisine and the specific occasion), do not put both conditions into one filter: a request for a rare case goes to nobody. Make TWO read actions — the first with both conditions, the second with only the general one — and lead the "nobody found" branch of the first to the second. Both meet on ONE mailing action: only the read must branch, while the message, the buttons and the recipients stay shared, otherwise text edits must be made twice.
  { pole: "Equipment", value: "#{equipment}", basis: true } + { pole: "Brands", value: "#{brand}", basis: true } → if empty → only { pole: "Equipment", value: "#{equipment}", basis: true }
  The dry run gives a remark on EVERY word-stem filter in such matching — this is normal, there are simply more of them. The ready appliance-repair bot is built this way.
- A PERCENTAGE OF A DEAL — THROUGH A DEBT, NOT BY TAKING MONEY FOR THE CLIENT. The constructor can ACCEPT payments but not pay them out, so "take a commission from the client's payment" cannot be built: the money goes past the bot. It is built the other way round: the customer settles with the performer as they like, and the bot ACCRUES a debt to the performer and does not let them to new requests until the debt is paid. The "Debt" field is a number, the response gatekeeper compares it with zero, payment resets it.
  ASK THE CUSTOMER, NOT THE PERFORMER, FOR THE DEAL SUM: the customer pays and has no reason to understate it, while the performer sees the same sum in the commission notification — neither side can lie. Ask where the customer marks the choice anyway: after the rating and the review, in one block.
  MAKE THE FIRST ORDERS COMMISSION-FREE (the freejobs setting): a newcomer needs something to get going on, and the service needs to show that the requests are real. The ready cleaners-exchange bot is built this way.
- A FEE PER REQUEST INSTEAD OF A SUBSCRIPTION — A PACK OF RESPONSES. A subscription is good where the performer works every day. For an emergency worker, a mover, a tow truck it does not pay off: three call-outs a week — and the month did not pay back, the person leaves. Then create a numeric field "Remaining", put free responses into it on registration, sell packs (5 / 15 / 50 / 150), and subtract on a successful response. Responses do not burn — this is the argument for a pack.
  THE PRICE OF A RESPONSE MAY DEPEND ON THE REQUEST (urgent is more expensive than ordinary). Then THE GATEKEEPER STANDS AFTER reading the request, not at the start of the scenario: until the request is read, the price cannot be calculated. The order is: the form → has not responded already → read the request → the gatekeeper (price and balance) → the response limit → write the response → deduct. Deduction — in one edit together with the response counter: two edits of one list row in a row are an extra reason for a race. The ready urgent-plumber-call bot is built this way.
- THE OWNER'S CHECK OF A PERFORMER — WITH A LIST FIELD. Where people are admitted by documents (nannies, carers, tutors for children, couriers with money), a form is not enough: manual admission is needed. Add the field "Verified" to the performers list, write "нет" (no) into it when the form is saved, and put the check in THREE places at once — into the request mailing filter, into the "New requests" feed and into the response gatekeeper. Otherwise an unverified person bypasses the ban by an old link. Editing the form must reset the field back to "нет": the documents may have changed.
  COMPARE WITH "нет", NOT WITH "да": { pole: "Verified", cmp: "!=", value: "нет" } and the branch "'#{my_check}' != 'нет'". The owner will write "Да", "verified" or "medical book until 2027" in the cell — when compared with "да" the performer silently stops receiving requests: no error, no feed, nowhere to look for the reason. When compared with "нет", anything but the original word fires. (In a bot in another language use the same rule with that language's "no".) The ready carers-and-nannies bot is built this way.
- A RATING IS CALCULATED, NOT STORED AS STARS: keep the average rating and the number of completed orders in the list, and on a new rating recalculate the average by code. A rating of oneself does not count — compare the rater's ClientID with the performer's ClientID.
- A REVIEW WITH MODERATION: if approval is on in the settings, the review goes into a separate list and to the administrator with an "Approve" button; it gets into the rating only after the press.
- CONSENT TO PERSONAL DATA PROCESSING — a screen at the start with the consent recorded in a list; without it an exchange with phones and addresses is not built.
- A REQUEST LIVES BY STATUSES in one list: whether a performer is assigned, whether they confirmed, whether it was cancelled. The "Confirm" and "Cancel" buttons go to the performer IN THE BOT and carry the request code.

SELLING ACCESS THROUGH AN EXTERNAL GATEWAY (Lava.top, YooKassa, Prodamus) AND GRANTING IT ON ANOTHER PLATFORM (GetCourse, a closed channel). Built after the ready course-selling bot:
- THE PAYMENT LINK IS OBTAINED BY TWO REQUESTS: first the gateway's product list, to find the offer id by your own product id, then the invoice — it returns the link and the sum. The gateway key — in the request header by substitution from the settings. The subscriber chooses the currency: rubles or EUR/USD from the settings if the card is foreign.
- PAYMENT IS CONFIRMED BY A WEBHOOK, NOT BY A BUTTON. A scenario with the "Waiting for data" action lists the fields the gateway sends (invoice number, sum, status, currency) — the engine lays them out into variables itself, there is no need to parse JSON.
- THERE ARE ALWAYS MORE THAN TWO STATUSES. The payment went through — grant access; the subscription was renewed — keep it; the subscription expired, was cancelled or was not charged — TAKE IT AWAY. A subscription without handling the "bad" statuses is a sale that cannot be stopped.
- GRANTING ACCESS IS A REQUEST TO THE PLATFORM. For GetCourse the parameters are put into JSON, encoded in base64 and sent in the params field together with the key and the add action. Removing access there is done not by "delete" but by "set this set of groups for the user": get the export id by email, read the export, take the user id, collect their active groups without the cancelled one and send the update.
- A CLOSED TELEGRAM CHANNEL: after payment — "Generate an invitation link" plus "Unban" (the person may have been banned after a previous unsubscription), after cancellation — "Ban on the channel". Show the link in a separate block the bot moves to after payment.
- RECORD THE PURCHASE IN A LIST in full: the date, the payment number, the sum, what was bought, the email, the flag "subscription or one-off". "My courses", unsubscription and the access check are built on it later.

A FORM WITH CONFIGURABLE FIELDS. When the owner decides what to ask (first name, last name, country, phone), do not breed versions of the bot: put "yes/no" switches into the settings list, and in each form block list all the possible next steps in order as conditions, "else" — straight to completion. Then a disabled field is simply skipped, and not a single extra branch appears.

A DOCUMENT FROM A TEMPLATE: THE PLACEHOLDER IN THE FILE, THE NAME IN THE ACTION. In a .docx file placeholders are written in curly braces: {number}, {customer}, {sum}. In the action ONLY the placeholder name is given — without braces: the engine puts the value into data[name], and the substitution is done by easy-template-x. Braces written into the name give the placeholder {{number}}, which is not in the document, and the field stays empty.
The syntax of the file itself: {name} — a variable, {#name} … {/name} — a loop (table rows: invoice items, works in an act), {#condition} … {/} — a condition, {name} with sizes given — an image or a QR code, {name} with a link — a hyperlink.
An element with an empty value is skipped entirely by the engine (external.js: if (createdoc[i].name && createdoc[i].value)) — the placeholder stays in the finished document as it is. So each element must have a value: either a variable or a default dash calculated a step earlier.
The template file itself does not get into the export: the bot owner uploads it OR doc.url contains a public DOC/DOCX link from another server, Google Drive or Yandex Disk. If doc.url is already set, do not ask for another upload. If no source is set, the action name must say plainly what needs to be done — for example "UPLOAD THE CONTRACT TEMPLATE OR SET ITS PUBLIC LINK IN THIS ACTION". The same with Google Drive, Yandex Disk and CRM accounts: someone else's account cannot be connected, so the note goes into the action name, not into a comment.
The optional doc.filename sets the finished file name and supports variables/substitution templates. Cyrillic is transliterated; spaces, internal dots and invalid characters become underscores; the engine adds the extension and a unique suffix. An empty name keeps the previous random generation. Do not put an extension into filename. An external link without an extension is valid: the engine detects DOC/DOCX from the server response or uses DOCX for the temporary file.
Where the finished document goes is set right there: docto — to the subscriber in the bot, emailcheck with email — by email (the address is taken from a variable if the bot collected it), messengercheck — to an employee. Save the link to the finished file in doclinkvar: without it the document cannot be written to a list or sent to a disk.
THE DOCUMENT — AS THE LAST STEP. If the owner's tariff does not allow documents, the engine cuts the whole scenario right at the "Document from template" action: everything after it does not run. So put list records, the message to the subscriber ("the act will come as a file") and messages to employees BEFORE the document, and the document itself as the last step of the scenario. But do not end the scenario SILENTLY: after the document — an exit with a short message ("📄 The act has been sent as a file" and a back button). A block whose scenario exits without a message and without buttons does not wait for the person — its transition fires by itself, and if it leads to a dispatcher block that goes back to this block, they call each other without stopping.

A LINE BREAK IS THE BREAK ITSELF, NOT THE TWO CHARACTERS "\n". In an export the value is already parsed from JSON, so the text must contain a real line break. If it is escaped once more during assembly, the value gets a pair of characters — a backslash and n — and the subscriber sees them right in the message: "Request accepted\n\nWe will contact you" on one line. There is no error anywhere, the bot works, the messages look broken.
This happens especially often where the text is built not in a block but in an action: the exit message, sending to the bot, a message to an employee, an email, information for a calendar booking and the reminder text. Check these fields separately — in block texts the breaks are usually right, in them they are not.
The only place where "\n" as two characters is appropriate is JS code: there it is a break inside a string of the code itself, for example lines.join('\n\n').
VARIABLES IN CODE ARE TAKEN FROM varstore, NOT BY SUBSTITUTION. The engine substitutes #{name} into JS code too, but it substitutes it AS TEXT, right into the source. While the variable holds a number, "const r = #{rating};" works. If it turns out empty, you get "const r = ;", a syntax error, and the action does not run AT ALL, silently. Write varstore['rating']: an empty value stays an empty value instead of breaking the whole step. If a substitution is still needed, put it in quotes: var s = '#{name}'. The same in regular expressions and code strings: the quantifier /#{1,6}/ is taken by the engine for the variable "1,6" and cut out — write /#+/ or /[#]{1,6}/, "#" and "{" not next to each other.

IN CODE THE RULE IS THE OPPOSITE, AND THE PRICE OF AN ERROR IS HIGHER. A real line break inside quotes is a JS syntax error, and the engine does not run the action AT ALL: not just the line with the break but the whole code. There is no message or note about it anywhere — the variables it was supposed to fill just stay empty, and the next steps silently run idle. This is how a client's message failed to be appended to a ticket in a tech-support bot:

    varstore['txt'] = varstore['was'] + '
    ' + varstore['new']          ← the action will not run at all

    varstore['txt'] = varstore['was'] + '\n' + varstore['new']   ← correct

A BLOCK THAT ONLY SAVES IS MADE PASS-THROUGH. A block with [Actions] that asks nothing — the scenario saved the request and answered "Request accepted" — must have dontwaitanswer: true and a transition to the next block. Otherwise the bot freezes: the subscriber read the answer, there are no buttons, and until they write something themselves, there is no continuation.

ONE STEP AT A TIME: "do not wait for an answer" OR a scenario that leads away. The mark
dontwaitanswer on a block means "showed it and moved on" — the engine itself
moves the subscriber by the block's transition (conditions, then "else"), and where there is no
transition — to the next block in order. The same when the block was woken by
a delayed launch: the planner of a reminder round can be put anywhere in the scheme,
a transition to it is enough. If the scenario of THIS block also leads away
(the exit "Launch block", exittype startmes), there are TWO steps: the scenario
brings the subscriber to the needed block, and the mark immediately carries them further.

From the outside it looks like this: the subscriber starts the bot and immediately gets two
messages in a row — "What is your name?" and "Which city are you from?". They answer
the second, the first question stays unanswered, and the form is collected with a hole.
In the editor everything looks right; the error is visible only at launch.

The rule: on a block with [Actions] whose scenario leads away with the exit "Launch
block" on EVERY path, dontwaitanswer must be false — the scenario decides.
Put the mark only where the scenario leads nowhere: it calculated, wrote,
returned text into [Actions] — and on in order.

ONE SCENARIO — ONE CALL. If a scenario is assigned both to the transition from a block (externalafter in conditionlist) and to the next block (externalbefore), it runs TWICE in a row: the request is written twice, the counter is increased twice, the client gets two identical messages. No error is visible anywhere — just duplicates in the list. The rule: a scenario that writes or sends something is called ONCE. If the result is needed in a message too, put it only on the externalbefore of a block with [Actions]; the variables of the previous block (answervar) are already filled by that moment.

A SCENARIO ON A BLOCK TRANSITION (externalafter) CANNOT ANSWER WITH ITS OWN MESSAGE. The engine reads "go to block", "end the scenario" and "launch a reminder" from it: its own exit message (exitmes, exitbtn, exitmedia) is NOT sent AT ALL — the block has already been shown, the answer received, there is nowhere to return the text.

But "Launch block" works IN FULL for it: the target block is shown with everything it is entitled to — with its own "before launch" scenario, with the chain's common scenario, with a record in the subscriber's path and in analytics. So such a scenario can answer the subscriber — not by itself, but through the block it leads to: that block has the text [Actions], and the BLOCK's scenario fills it.

A ROUTER ON A TRANSITION IS BUILT EXACTLY LIKE THIS: a parsing scenario stands on the "else"
transition of the menu, parses what the subscriber wrote and leads with "Launch block"
to the needed screen, and the screen finishes the story with its own scenario.

CLEANER — WITH A SEPARATE BLOCK, and for complex parsing it is better this way: one job —
one block, it is visible in the bot scheme and in the subscriber's path, and the scenario is not
doubled if the same parser is needed somewhere else.

  1. Create a technical block "Answer parsing": text = "[Actions]",
     externalbefore = the parsing scenario, no buttons, dontwaitanswer is not needed
     (the scenario leads away by an exit anyway), one transition branch — a fallback,
     to the main menu.
  2. The "else" branch of every screen leads to this block with an ordinary nextmessage.
  3. The parsing scenario stands on the block "before sending": both its own
     exit message and "Launch block" work for it.

The same trick solves everything a "scenario after an answer" does: sending
a template, delivering a message to the other party, parsing a press. One technical
block for each such job.
So externalafter fits silent work (write, calculate, notify an employee, set a tag) and the "Launch block" fork. If you need the scenario's OWN message to the subscriber, put it on the externalbefore of the NEXT block with [Actions]: the answer of the previous block is already available by then ([Answer], answervar), and the exit and the transition of such a scenario work fully.

Hence a simple check: a transition with a scenario (externalafter) MUST have
nextmessage filled in. A transition without a target and with a scenario is a dead end: the scenario
works silently, and the subscriber sees nothing and stays on the previous block.
A typical case — action buttons on a card (block, confirm,
cancel): a press must lead to a block with [Actions], and it must be parsed by
THAT block's scenario, not by the transition's scenario.

And also: a scenario branch that DID something must say so. A branch
without exitmes and without a transition leaves the subscriber with an empty screen — they do not know
whether the button worked, and press it again.

THE FIRST ONE IN BECOMES THE OWNER — AND ONLY WHILE THE LIST IS EMPTY. The bot
gets to the person who set it up: they open it before the clients.
So the first launch with an empty employees list records the subscriber
as the owner: the role 'owner' in a separate list field. After that the list is not empty,
and nobody else gets it.

SHOW THE OWNER WHAT TO DO AT ONCE. The owner's first-launch screen is
not a client greeting but a short briefing: what this bot is, what to press
first, how to invite employees. Without it the person sees "Write your
question" and does not understand where they are: no settings are visible anywhere, there are no buttons.
Then the owner must see their own menu — managing what changes
in the bot: employees, access codes, parameters.

THE OTHER EMPLOYEES — BY AN ACCESS CODE from the parameters list. The owner
sees the code in their menu, can change it, passes it to an employee together
with the bot link; the employee sends the code as an ordinary message, and the branch
"the subscriber's answer equals the code" adds them to the list. Write the condition
in quotes on both sides: '[Answer]' = '#{access_code}'.

The exception — channels, groups and bots whose link has already been distributed:
there a random buyer will write first (see rule 4b). In such bots
the owner is added by an access code, or their ClientID is entered into the list
before launch.

THE EMPLOYEE'S ACCOUNT — IN THE SAME CHAIN. A separate chain launched by keywords does not open if the bot has a chain with nothandled or dialog: the subscriber already stands on its block, and their word goes as an answer to the current block. Make the account blocks part of the same chain, and show the button to them with a scenario — then only an employee sees it. A button from a scenario is set in done: exitbtn (nextMessage — a string with the block id) and is added to the block's buttons; for different roles make two scenario branches: with the button and without. The second working way — launching a chain by a tag (addtagstart) and a button of type action that sets this tag.

A "NOT EMPTY" FILTER IS UNRELIABLE for rows the subscriber's data is written to: a person may have no name in the profile, and the row is written with an empty field — after which it is no longer found. If you need all rows of a list, do not set a filter at all, and check existence with filtervar.

If you need to remember the choice of an answer option by buttons, use buttons of type nextdata; then the value is written to the subscriber's answer. And for the bot to move to another block after a nextdata button is chosen, add the block to move to into the block's nextmessage property.

The values of nextdata buttons must match the values that are later compared in filters and conditions: a "Ready" button with the value "done" finds nothing when searching by the status "ready", and no error is visible anywhere. Keep a fixed set of values (statuses, categories, ratings) in a directory list and take the button values from there too.

A PRESS IS PARSED BY THE BLOCK THE SUBSCRIBER STANDS ON — that is, the screen
they see, not the one the button "should" lead to. So a command
available from several screens (an employee's console, "to menu", "back")
must be in the transitions of EACH such block. If the route is written
only on one block, from the other screens the button silently goes by the "else" branch
and does nothing: the subscriber presses — and nothing.

In code assembly this is done with a shared set of transitions: one list of conditions
that is put into all blocks where the console is shown.

WHAT GETS INTO THE ANSWER WHEN A BUTTON WITH A JUMP TO A BLOCK IS PRESSED. The subscriber
came to a block by pressing a button (nextMessage to this block) — the engine writes
the CAPTION of the pressed button into the block's answervar, [Answer] and _answer, for example
"💬 Reply". Where there is no caption (a jump by a /to… command, the website, VK), the
BLOCK NAME is written. Hence the rule for a screen that waits for text and is itself
opened by buttons (a conversation, entering a comment): its "before
sending" scenario treats as a press, not a message, the captions of all buttons
leading to this block and the name of the block itself. Otherwise the caption of the "Write" button
goes to the other party as a message. Read the answer at the very beginning of the code, before
the common code resets it. The conversation in the ready bot
"Taxi in a group" is built this way.

THE SCENARIO'S BUTTONS ARE ADDED TO THE BLOCK'S BUTTONS, NOT REPLACING THEM. If a "before
sending" scenario exits with exitbtn, and the same buttons stand in the block itself,
the subscriber sees them TWICE: two "🏠 Main menu" in a row, two "Check
status". No error is visible anywhere, but it looks like a breakdown.
THERE IS ONE RULE: the block's buttons are set by ONE THING — either the block itself or its
scenario. A block with [Actions] whose scenario gives buttons must not have its own
buttons: the scenario gives them on EVERY exit branch, and different ones per branch
(one set for an empty list, another for a filled one), including the branches "no rights",
"nothing found" and "the list is empty" — an exit button is mandatory there, otherwise
the subscriber hits a dead end.
A check before handing over: take each block with buttons, find its scenario
(externalbefore and externalafter) and compare WHERE the buttons lead. If the target
of at least one pair matches — it is a duplicate, remove the button from the scenario.
Compare the target, not the caption: "🏠 To menu" and "🏠 Changed my mind" to the same
block are the same two buttons to one place.
The exit "go to block" (exittype startmes) with text shows the buttons
of the TARGET block — there compare with the buttons of the block you lead to.
One scenario can hang on several blocks: removing a button from a scenario
is allowed only if the same one exists on ALL its blocks, otherwise on another screen
the only exit disappears.

A SETTING WITHOUT ITS OWN CHECKBOX IS DEAD. At a scenario exit and at a block branch, next
to each set there is a switch, and the engine reads the set ONLY when it is
on: addtag without addtagcheck, deltag without deltagcheck, messengermes without
messengercheck, webhook without webhookcheck, email without emailcheck. Filling in
the tag list and not setting addtagcheck is a common error: the funnel stages
seem to be laid out in the bot, but not a single tag lands on the subscribers
and the funnel is empty. Filled in a set — turn on its switch.

A LINK BUTTON TO AN UNFILLED ADDRESS BREAKS THE WHOLE MESSAGE. A placeholder like
"https://" or an empty string is not a link: the messenger answers with a refusal
and does not send the message at all, together with the text and the other buttons.
The subscriber presses "About the company" and gets nothing.
While there are no addresses (the customer will send them later, the links lie in the parameters list),
DO NOT PUT them as buttons. Print them as a line in the text the scenario builds:
it checks each address and outputs only the filled ones. The sign of a real
address is simple — after "://" there is something other than emptiness.

A BLOCK WITH BUTTONS MUST HAVE btnsettings FOR THE BOT'S CHANNELS. Without them the buttons
go out one per row in a column — a menu of eight items stretches over the whole screen,
and there is nowhere to set "Replace the previous message". Give the block the set
of settings of the messenger the bot is for: inrow — two or three short
buttons per row, long ones one per row; replace — on screens that redraw
themselves.

WRONGANSWER IS A REFERENCE TO A BLOCK, NOT TEXT. The field holds { label, value }:
the name and id of the block the bot shows if the answer failed the check
(number, phone, email, regex). The engine DOES NOT SHOW a string in this field
AT ALL: the subscriber sent "tomorrow" instead of a phone and got nothing,
wrote again — silence again, and left. The error is quiet: the bot installs,
in the account everything looks right, and the conversation breaks off at the first typo.
Checked on the real engine; in the catalog of ready bots 36 bots (118 blocks) were silent this way,
and it was fixed by a separate change.

THE RETURN ON A WRONG ANSWER MUST NOT LEAD TO THE SAME BLOCK EITHER. wronganswer
pointing to itself is a trap: the phone-check block has no buttons of its own
(they cancel the number request), and a person whose number fails the check
stays there forever — neither "Back" nor "Main menu".

CORRECT — a separate short "not recognised" block for each check:
  • text — an explanation with an example of a correct answer ("A date like 25.12.2026 is needed");
  • a "Enter again" button → the question block;
  • a "Main menu" button → the menu;
  • conditionlist[0].nextmessage → the question block (text returns to it);
  • if the question asked for a phone or a location with its own keyboard, put
    clearkeyboard on this block: otherwise the keyboard keeps hanging.
It is also needed for a date, an email and any other answer check.

In JSON it looks like this:
  { "id": "zpphon00", "text": "Leave your phone", "phone": true,
    "wronganswer": { "label": "Phone: not recognised", "value": "zpphonw0" }, … }
  { "id": "zpphonw0", "name": "Phone: not recognised",
    "text": "This does not look like a phone. For example: +7 900 111-22-33",
    "btn": [ the "Enter again" button → zpphon00, the "Main menu" button → menu ], … }

SEVERAL FILES IN A ROW ARE SEVERAL MESSAGES. The engine assembles an album
itself, but three photos sent one by one are three firings of the block.
If you promise "several files are allowed", the intake screen must return
TO ITSELF (a transition with the condition #{_ismedia} = 1 leads to this same block),
and the "Done" button leads the person on. Then also give it replace,
otherwise after five photos the chat has five copies of "send the files".
Add up the counter of what was sent, do not overwrite it: #{media_count}
is separate for each pass.

SOMEONE ELSE'S DATA BY A GUESSED NUMBER. When the subscriber enters an order, reservation or booking number, the row filter MUST go by two conditions at once: by the number AND by the subscriber's ClientID ([ClientID]). A filter by the number alone shows someone else's order to anyone who picks the number: the phone, the address, the sum. The exception is an employee's account: there a ClientID restriction is, on the contrary, unacceptable, otherwise they cannot handle others' orders. Check the rights in such an account separately — by looking the subscriber up in the employees list.

A NUMBER ENTERED BY HAND is normalised before the search: people write "R-1001", "r 1001", "№1001", "1001". The most reliable way is to extract the digits from the input and rebuild the number by your own pattern — then any spelling fits.

If the bot returns an informational message to the subscriber without requiring any action, and further in the scenario there are blocks the subscriber must go through, add a "Continue" button with a transition to the next block.

EVERY BLOCK HAS A BUTTON. In channels with buttons (Telegram, MAX, VK, Viber) any block must have at least one button: the next step, "To menu" or "Cancel". Otherwise a subscriber who changed their mind halfway through a form has to scroll the message history looking for where to press — and more often simply leaves. Even where free text is awaited (a name, a problem description, a comment to a review), put an exit button: "Cancel" with a transition to the menu, or "Send without a comment" of type nextdata with a dash value, so the step can be skipped without losing what was already collected.
Three legitimate exceptions: the block asks for a phone or a location via btnsettings (own buttons cancel this request); the block shows nothing (the whole text is [Actions], and the scenario exits without a message); a bot for a channel without buttons (Avito, marketplaces, WhatsApp) — buttons turn into text there.
A button not everyone needs (an employee's account, repeating an order) is given from a scenario via done.exitbtn: it is added to the block's buttons, and only whoever is entitled to it sees it.

In the buttons located in action scenarios — exitbtn, sendtobtn, docxtobtn, doctobtn, notifybtn — with action equal to 'nextmessage' the nextMessage property is a string; put the block's ordinal number in it (block numbering starts from 1), or the ID of the block to go to (preferable). In the block buttons btn, the nextMessage property holds an object like { label: "Button name", value: "ID" }. Do not confuse them!

Why so: scenario buttons go through variable substitution, and it turns an object into a JSON string — the transition breaks and the button leads nowhere. Block buttons do not go through the transition substitution, an object is needed there. Check the form separately for each place you take a button from.

THE SECOND HALF OF THE SAME RULE — WHAT A BUTTON CARRIES. A button cannot have both a transition and an answer at once: the engine looks at action. With action = 'nextmessage' a transition (nextMessage) goes out and the data field — on the other side it is read with the template [Data1]. With action = 'nextdata' only nextData goes out: it becomes the subscriber's answer ([Answer]), but leads nowhere — the subscriber stays on their block.

Hence the rule for notifications to another subscriber (sendtobtn): for the recipient to press a button and get straight into the right block with the right record, put action = 'nextmessage', nextMessage = the block id as a string, and put the record number into data and read it in the scenario as [Data1]. Putting the record number into nextData is useless: the recipient will not go anywhere. Leave action = 'nextdata' only for choice buttons on a screen where the answer is awaited right now, and only if the bot has no crossing flows (see below).
The exception is a website visitor: for a transition the widget drops data. If the bot works on a website, make buttons with data value buttons and parse them in the first block where the answers of ALL blocks lead (a console bot): then the value is parsed whatever block the person stands on.

The block a notification button leads to must collect everything needed itself: read its subscriber from the list by [ClientID] and the record by [Data1]. It can no longer rely on the data having been collected on the way through the menu — from a notification people come into it directly.

CROSSING FLOWS — PLAN THEM BEFORE THE FIRST SCREEN. A bot where subscribers have shared records (an order, a request, a reservation, a conversation) is several message flows that cross: notifications from other subscribers, conversations between them, timed blocks. The errors of such a bot are not visible in the scheme — they appear when the flows meet. Before building screens, write out for EACH role: which messages come to it from outside, what buttons they have and on which block the person may stand at the moment of the press.

A VALUE BUTTON IS PARSED BY THE CURRENT BLOCK, NOT BY THE SCREEN WHERE IT IS SHOWN. A nextdata press comes as an answer to the block the subscriber stands on now. The current block shifts by itself: the person pressed "💬 Reply" in the other party's notification, a timed block came, they opened a card from a mailing. After that the value button of the old message goes into someone else's block. This happened in the ready taxi bot: the driver wrote to the passenger, pressed "🚗 Arriving" — the value went into the conversation, the passenger got nothing, the driver got the start message.
THE RULE: an ACTION button on a record (accept, mark, cancel, rate, approve, pay, take) — action = 'nextmessage' to a handler block, and what was pressed goes into data: "kind~code", additions separated by "|" ([Data2], [Data3]). A value button is allowed only for a choice on a form screen that the person presses right away, and only in a bot without crossing flows. In a notification to another subscriber (sendtobtn) — never.
A HANDLER BLOCK: text [Actions], externalbefore — a scenario that reads [Data1] and does the job silently, dontwaitanswer: true and one branch to the screen the person must see. If an answer in words is needed, the handler waits for the answer and answers itself with an exit message with buttons.
A TOGGLE ON ITS OWN SCREEN (options, classes, a checklist): the button leads by a transition to THIS SAME block, the data — "kind~key~display number", the screen's scenario first toggles, then draws. Replace on such a screen deletes the previous copy; through a "no waiting" handler the copies would pile up.
CHOOSING SEVERAL ITEMS — WITH A MASK IN THE BUTTON DATA, WITHOUT STORAGE. When there are many ticks (wrap zones, extra services, order options), do not write the choice to a list on every press: the whole choice travels in the button data as a mask number — bit N means directory row N in order, the number is written in base 36: "zg~B~0~75". An item button carries the mask WITH THE BIT TOGGLED, the group tabs and "Done" — the current mask. Then an old copy of the screen does not lie either: its buttons show their own state. Count the bits by division — Math.floor(mask / Math.pow(2, i)) % 2, toggle by adding and subtracting Math.pow(2, i). The operators >>, & and | in JavaScript are 32-bit and silently break the mask after the 31st item. Exact — up to 50 items (2^53); take no more directory rows than that. Write the result to the list once, on "Done", and as item codes, not the mask: the owner will reorder the directory rows, and a saved estimate will not change. The ready bot "Detailing and protective coatings" is built this way.
MANY LIST ROWS IN ONE WRITE — WITH ARRAYS IN THE FIELDS. A person sent a list as lines (employees, order items, results per group) — do not create a separate "Write to list" action for each line. The code puts JSON arrays OF ONE LENGTH into variables, one per field: varstore['fullname'] = JSON.stringify(lines.map(function (l) { return l.fullname })), likewise numbers, deadlines and the rest; an empty value — '-', not an empty string. One qb.cfg.listAdd with the fields "#{fullname}", "#{number}"… adds as many rows as there are elements in the array; a field with an ordinary value ("works", "#{company}") is repeated in every row. The array lengths must match — the engine takes the shortest. Do not pass an empty array: with zero rows do not reach the write — use a branch. Calculate row numbers in code in advance (the maximum + 1, + 2…).
THE ENGINE DOES NOT CLEAR THE DATA FIELD. [Data1] lives until the next press WITH DATA: came to a screen by a button without data — [Data1] holds the previous value. [Data1] is overwritten by any value in the button's data field: where it matters what is pressed now, give data to EVERY button, even "m" for the "Menu" button. And where the previous value is useful — for example, the "Back" button to a card must remember which card it is — leave it without data: [Data1] keeps the context of the previous press. A handler only buttons with data lead to is not afraid of this. A screen scenario that parses [Data1] on every display is: remember the parsed value in a variable and do not perform the same thing a second time, and put a display number into the toggle's data (for example String(Date.now()).slice(-5)), so that a repeated press differs. A "Launch block" transition from parsing typed text is not a press and has no data field: a screen reached BOTH by a button AND by such a transition cannot tell them apart by [Data1] — it holds the previous button's data. Before such a transition put a one-time marker (varstore['jump'] = 'sum'), and the screen reads it first thing and erases it at once — the marker is there, so they came from text, and the old button data is not touched (the "Gift certificates" bot works this way).
A LIST READ WRITES INTO ITS OWN VARIABLES — KEEP YOUR OWN VALUES UNDER OTHER NAMES. qb.cfg.listRead(list, { "From": "shift_from", "Status": "shift_status" }, …) puts the row fields into these variables on every run. If the code of the same scenario writes its own value into a variable with the same name (for example varstore['shift_from'] = the start day of the window for the next filter), it overwrites the field that was read — and the step that parses the row further gets someone else's value: the shift "started" at minute 20261006, and the "Start" button silently did not work. Neither the dry run nor checking the steps one by one sees this — only a live launch. The rule: the read has its own prefix (shift_…), the scenario variables another (ss_…), and the code never assigns names the read saves into (clearing them with an empty string is allowed).
PARSE RAW LIST FIELDS ONLY ONCE AFTER listRead. If a JS step converts several rows into objects and then clears the raw variables, a later JS step MUST use the saved objects or JSON. Calling the same parser again without a new listRead silently returns an empty set. If the raw fields are genuinely needed again, put another qb.cfg.listRead before that step.
ONE VARIABLE — ONE ROLE: WHAT TO BRANCH ON AND WHAT TO WRITE ARE DIFFERENT NAMES. The code puts varstore['kind'] = 'photo' (what to write into the "Kind" field), and at the end of the same step varstore['kind'] = 'write' (where to lead the branch) — and "write" goes into the list: the marks lose their kind, the filter "Kind != mark" lets everything through. Do not use any variable that stands in a branch condition ('#{kind}' = 'write') as a value in qb.cfg.listAdd / listEdit of the same scenario; create a separate name for the data (mark_kind).
A SET OF JUMP BUTTONS WITH DATA. If the set is built by code (the buttons depend on the state), the button's name, nextMessage and data are three JSON arrays of one length: name "#{x_names}", nextMessage "#{x_blocks}", data "#{x_data}". The engine gives each button its own data. A single string in data is shared by the whole set, and then you cannot know which button was pressed: for a jump press [Answer] is empty.
THE LENGTH OF A PRESS. Telegram has 64 bytes for the whole press, and that includes the service part: "nmn+|<block id>|+da+|<data>|+" — about 20 bytes. About 40 bytes remain for the data, Cyrillic — 2 bytes per letter. Put a short Latin code ("st~go|R123456789"), and read names, addresses and sums from a list by the code. Longer — the message with such a button does not go out at all, silently. Put the row time (Date.now(), 13 digits) into the data in base 36: Number(ms).toString(36) — 8 characters, back with parseInt(s, 36). Codes from a directory filled in by the bot owner, if they travel in a button, limit in length when parsing the directory (for example, to 8 characters) — otherwise one long list row silently breaks the whole screen.
TEXT IS PARSED BY A MARKER, NOT BY A BLOCK. Text has the same trouble as a value button: what is written is parsed by the current block. If timed blocks come to the person or they can press someone else's notification while the bot waits for text from them, the text goes the wrong way. The pattern:
  1) the screen that asks for text sets a marker by code: varstore['waiting'] = 'kind~number~' + Date.now();
  2) ALL screens have one answervar ("text_in"), and the "else" branch leads to one block "Text: parse";
  3) its scenario looks at the marker (older than 2–3 hours — not valid), sets varstore['from_router'] = '1' and with the exit "Launch block" goes to saving this kind;
  4) saving trusts the text only if the flag is set, and immediately erases the flag, the text and the marker. Without the flag "text_in" may hold a previous answer;
  5) the menu and the account remove the marker; without a marker the text is a question: "Pass it to the manager?".
A saving step that can also be reached by a button (for example "Not needed" at the phone in MAX) understands by the absence of the flag that there was no text. The ready bot "Review after a visit" is built this way.
THE "SEND FILES" MARKER AFTER THE FIRST FILE — NOT AN ERROR ON A WORD. Files are sent one by one, so the "files" marker is set again after an accepted file. But a word after accepted files ("thanks", "menu" in the client's language, a question) is the end of sending, not a wrong answer: remove the marker and show the menu. Distinguish by the marker code: after the first file that arrived add a sign to the code ("5:PASS:1", for a receipt — the number of the created payment), and parsing a word with the sign leads to the menu, while without the sign (no file yet) it answers "send a file". Otherwise for three hours any word gets "send a file", and the word "menu" in another language does not open the menu — in a multilingual bot add the menu words of each language to the parsing.
"~" IS FORBIDDEN IN THE MARKER CODE. The marker itself is divided by "~" into three parts: the kind, the code and the time. Put a second "~" into the code ("basis~K-1001~refusal~1790…") — the time slides into another part, parsing reads a word instead of it, the marker turns out "stale", and the bot silently answers "I did not expect this". There will be no error anywhere. If you need to pass two things in the code, glue them with another sign: "basis~K-1001:refusal~time", and in parsing take w.code.split(':').
THE JUMP MARKER IS ONE-TIME AND ONLY A MARKER. A screen reached both by a button and by a "Launch block" transition from parsing recognises the transition by a variable that parsing sets. The screen erases it IMMEDIATELY on reading and puts nothing else into it: keep the code needed later for writing in another variable. Otherwise the marker outlives the screen, the next press is taken for a transition, and the button data ("code~c" — "I subscribed") is lost. The marker is set by a separate step RIGHT BEFORE the transition itself, not by a decision step above: if between setting it and the transition the scenario can exit with a message ("already decided", the check failed), the marker stays and intercepts the next press — the wrong card opens.
A SCREEN AFTER A JUMP FROM PARSING DOES NOT EXIT SILENTLY. Its "else" transition leads to the parsing, and an exit without a message returns the same event there — the blocks start calling each other. Decide "whether to do something" (whether the person subscribed to their channel, whether there is something to give out) in the parsing itself, before the transition, and let the screen always answer.
A LOOP OVER DATES — ONLY WITH A LIMITER. "While the day has passed the cut-off — take the next one" is written with while, and if the next-day calculation even once returns the same day, the JS action hangs together with the message processing. Write a for with a ceiling: for (var guard = 0; guard < 60 && day !== null && !canEdit(day); guard++) day = next(day). Sixty steps are enough for any schedule, and the engine is never at risk of hanging. The ready bot "Office supplies" is built this way.
VARIABLE NAMES IN JS — LITERAL. Write varstore['ad_cid'] = …, not varstore[p + '_cid'] = … and not two assignments on one line. The assembly check finds a variable by name: one assembled from pieces looks unfilled, and sending to it ("#{ad_cid}" in the recipients) is counted as a hole. If the same logic is needed for different recipients, make two small functions with literal names. Collect the slots "first, second, third reminder" the same way: an object in a function, and the assignments varstore['tr1_text'] — one per line.
COMPARISON AFTER REPLACING "Ё". If you bring a string to a form without "ё" (toLowerCase().replace(/ё/g, 'е')), compare it without "ё" too: "отключён" after normalisation is "отключен", and the check === 'отключён' NEVER fires, silently.
THE COMPARISON VALUE — IN QUOTES. The engine glues list filter conditions and branch conditions into one string where "|" is OR and "&" is AND.
