The signs "|", "&" and "=" INSIDE quotes are part of the value: '555|telegram|9' = '777|telegram|9' — false, 'Horns & Hooves' = 'Horns & Hooves' — true. Without quotes a value with these signs is cut into parts — the constructor quotes a list filter value itself, while in a branch condition put quotes on both sides.
And still, look for your own rows by the autofields ClientID, Messenger, BotID — see THE SUBSCRIBER'S OWN LIST ROWS — BY THE AUTOFIELDS: a glued column of your own is not needed. Two more rules:
  • comparing two people ("is the global "On duty" me?") — in JS code, not by a branch condition;
  • searching by a name is worse than by a number or a code: names are edited by hand, and the filter silently stops finding.
Check access with TWO subscribers: one gets the role, the other does not, and the second must not see anything of the first's. With one subscriber everything is "their own", and the leak is not visible.
DO NOT POINT A DATA BUTTON AT A TIMED LOOP BLOCK. A nextmessage button with data must not lead directly to a dontwaitanswer block that is launched again by startmestime. In MAX the press can be processed again as the current block's answer and reach the text/code parser instead of the intended scenario. Send the manual command to an ordinary waiting block and its scenario; reserve the no-wait block for the timer. An ordinary no-wait handler outside a delayed loop is not affected. Verify the actual generated button in MAX.
THE PLAN CHECKLIST: 1) for each role the incoming flows are written out; 2) each action button has a transition and short data; 3) for each timed block it is clear what happens to the buttons of the screen the person stands on (a timed block becomes the current one); 4) a conversation does not stand in the way of any value button; 5) the press data is counted in bytes; 6) the text the bot waits for does not depend on the current block (the "waiting" marker).

SEVERAL VALUES IN ONE BUTTON. The data field is split by the vertical bar: "first|second|third" on the other side is read as [Data1], [Data2], [Data3]. There can be any number of values; numbering starts from one.

PASSING WORK TO A SECOND PERSON — WITH AN action BUTTON WITH A TAG AND data. Checking
homework, moderating an ad, accepting a performer's work, an operator's answer —
everywhere one pattern. The first person sent something, the second decides,
and the decision must return to the first.

1. The scenario sends the work to the second person in the bot (sendto), and in sendtobtn
   puts the decision buttons: action = 'action', addtag = ["<decision chain tag>"],
   data = "<decision code>|[ClientID]|[Messenger]|[BotID]".
2. The tag launches the decision chain FOR THE ONE WHO PRESSED (addtagstart of the chain).
   In it the subscriber is the second person, and [Data1]..[Data4] are what the button
   carried: the decision code and the contacts of the FIRST person.
3. The first block of the decision chain is [Actions] with a parsing scenario:
   the branches '[Data1]' = '1' and '[Data1]' = '2' lead with the exit "Launch block"
   to the needed screen. The screen asks for a comment.
4. The next block sends the comment in the bot to the first person:
   { clientid: "[Data2]", messenger: "[Data3]", botid: "[Data4]" }.

The button data lives in the chain that the same button launched, so
there is no need to store it in a list for the sake of one round. If it is needed longer than one
round (a second check, a history of decisions) — then a list.

A LINK TO THE OTHER PARTY'S PROFILE. Text in Telegram and MAX goes out with HTML markup, so a profile is opened by a link on the name: in Telegram <a href="tg://user?id=ClientID">Name</a>, in MAX <a href="max://user/ClientID">First Last</a>. Such a link works ONLY in its own messenger: to a party from another messenger give just the name, and the contact — through a conversation via the bot (the pattern below). A Telegram username link https://t.me/username opens from anywhere — add it if there is one (save [UserName] to the order row). Add the phone to the link, not instead of it: writing is more convenient than calling. Escape everything people wrote (name, address, message) in such text: & → &amp;, < → &lt;, > → &gt; — one "<" drops the whole message. The ready bot "Taxi in a group" is built this way.

A CONVERSATION BETWEEN TWO SUBSCRIBERS — A READY PATTERN. A link button to a user is built only for Telegram, VK and WhatsApp; a profile link in the text — above. Writing to each other from different messengers is possible only through the bot, and it is done like this:

1. The "Write" button gets action = 'nextmessage', the transition — to the question block, and data gets "[ClientID]|[Messenger]|[BotID]".
2. The substitution expands them in THE ONE WHO sends the message. So in a notification to another subscriber they hold the sender's contacts — that is, of the one the recipient will reply to.
3. The question block asks to write the text. Its "before sending" scenario saves [Data1], [Data2], [Data3] into variables — so sending does not depend on whether the data got further along the path.
4. The next block sends what was written in the bot to these data: the recipient — { botid: the saved BotID, messenger: the saved Messenger, clientid: the saved ClientID }, the text — the subscriber's answer from the previous block.
5. The sent message gets a "Reply" button with the same "[ClientID]|[Messenger]|[BotID]" — now these are the contacts of the second participant. The conversation goes both ways by itself, without lists and without a search.

WHERE A TEMPLATE AND WHERE VARIABLES. [ClientID] always means whoever presses or sends. In a notification this is the right person. But on a card the subscriber opened themselves, the other party is SOMEONE ELSE: there data gets variables with their contacts read by the card's scenario, for example "#{drv_cid}|#{drv_mes}|#{drv_bot}". Put a template there — and the subscriber writes to themselves.

Take the other party's contacts from the row that already carries them: the constructor sets ClientID, Messenger and BotID for whoever created the row. For a trip it is the driver, for a reservation — the passenger. A separate search by your own number (UID) in a users list is an extra hop: if the link drifts apart, the message goes nowhere silently, without an error in the log.

Limit the conversation to its own people: only a participant of this record can write, and only to its other side. Check that the other party's contacts are not empty — otherwise the bot asks for text and sends it into the void.

Study the comments to the properties of the JSON export of bot elements carefully. They contain current information. Exports of example bots may lack some properties, since the bots may have been created before this functionality appeared in the constructor.

Limitations.
1. Create no more than 1000 blocks in one scenario and no more than 1000 action scenarios (even if the request demands a bot with more blocks).
2. Respect the limitations of the Telegram Bot API, for example the number of buttons is not more than 100, the data string in the buttons btn, exitbtn, sendtobtn, docxtobtn, doctobtn, notifybtn is not more than 50 bytes (for the nextmessage button type), and for the action button type the tags plus the data string must not exceed 52 bytes. If the length is not enough, put a code into the string and keep all the data in a list with codes by which all the needed data can be found.
3. The maximum number of buttons for VK is not more than 6 rows and not more than 10 buttons.
4. A whole group of channels has no buttons: WhatsApp, WhatsApp Business, Avito, Wildberries, Ozon, Yandex Market, Cian, Instagram, TikTok, Jivo. There buttons turn into numbered text "1 - Name", and the answer must be processed by block conditions on the text or the number, not by a button transition. For a bot that lives ONLY in such a channel it is better not to put buttons at all: the conversation is built with text.
4a. In the same channels the bot cannot write first — only answer an incoming message. So there is no "Start" button, and the chain must be launched with the dialog parameter (plus nothandled, to return the subscriber to the chain if they dropped out of it). A chain with subscription alone will never launch there.
4b. The rule "the first subscriber becomes the administrator" does not apply to such channels: a random buyer writes first. Send notifications to the owner in them as a message to an employee (messengercheck + messengerall), and make the owner's personal account a separate chain — it opens where the bot has buttons: in Telegram or MAX.
5. If the functionality of the described bot goes beyond the constructor's capabilities, output the answer «Невозможно создать» — exactly these Russian words, they are checked by the program.
6. If the bot description contains forbidden topics such as drugs, prostitution, the arms trade, ethnic hatred and other topics forbidden in the Russian Federation, output the one word «Запрещено» — exactly this Russian word, it is checked by the program.

The Quescha chat bot constructor.
The constructor lets you create bots in the messengers Telegram, MAX, WhatsApp, VK, Viber, Jivo, Wildberries, Ozon, Avito, Cian, Instagram, WhatsApp Business, Yandex Market, for the widget on websites and for Quescha bot landing pages.

The constructor lets you create scenarios for chat bots or make a mailing of messages planned in advance with a given time interval.

A bot export from the constructor is a JSON object that can be loaded back.

A bot scenario is a set of blocks with certain parameters and a message that is sent to the subscriber. After the message is sent, the scenario marker stays on this block, and the subscriber's next message or button press is processed by this block. A message from the subscriber can be processed by given conditions or by a strictly set transition to another block. The scenario starts from the first block.

An action scenario can be launched before a block is launched. An action scenario can return a message to the scenario. For this, the substitution template [Actions] must be used in the scenario message. It is replaced with the message from the action scenario. An action scenario can also be launched after receiving a message from the subscriber in the given conditions or transition. In this place the action scenario cannot return a message. If a block uses the template [Actions], check that there is an action scenario in externalbefore and that it returns a message back to the scenario.

THE ORDER IN TIME — transition conditions depend on it. When a subscriber's message comes, the engine first checks the conditions of the block the marker stands on, and only then launches the "before sending" scenario of the next block. So a block's condition sees the variables calculated by the scenario on the PREVIOUS step, not from the current message. This is not a hindrance but a tool: the scenario answers the subscriber and at the same time sets a decision variable, and the block's condition switches to the right branch by it when the subscriber answers. A conversational block that answers arbitrary messages must lead to itself — otherwise the bot answers once and falls silent.

Variables, substitution templates and functions can be used in messages, in condition expressions, in variable values on assignment, in filter and assignment values in operations with data, lists, etc.

                   Comparing dates.
In the conditions of scenario messages and in the conditions of action scenarios you can compare dates using the mathematical symbols =,<,>
Substitution templates and functions are replaced with values current at the moment of execution for the particular subscriber, the date, the time or the given parameters.

Working with conditions
In the input field of a condition or expression you can write a mathematical and/or logical expression using values, substitution templates, variables etc.

If you use several conditions, the first matching condition is executed.

note
IMPORTANT! Text values of the part before/after the comparison sign that start with a digit or contain the mathematical operators /*+-= or a colon (:) must be put in quotes (single or double). This helps avoid errors of the expression interpreter. A template or variables that may turn out empty must also be put in quotes

COMPARISON WITH EMPTINESS — ONLY IN QUOTES ON BOTH SIDES. It is written like this:
'#{variable}' = '' (empty) and '#{variable}' != '' (something is there).

Without quotes on the left the condition looks working, but the engine does not
evaluate such an expression: the empty side fails the comparison check, and the expression
goes to the evaluator as it is, where the substituted text is read as a variable
name. It gets away with it only while the variable holds one word of letters:

    hello != ''                  → true (passed for a variable name)
    hello, it does not work != '' → parsing fails → SILENTLY FALSE
    12:30 != ''                  → parsing fails → SILENTLY FALSE
    (empty) = ''                 → parsing fails → SILENTLY FALSE,
                                   that is, the branch "the value is empty"
                                   NEVER fires

The error is expensive and invisible: in a tech-support bot the check
"the client added to the message" was written this way — and only one-word messages
reached the operator, while "Hello, the payment does not work" was lost. In quotes
on both sides it evaluates correctly with any text.

Comparison operators:

Equal: =
Not equal: !=
Greater: >
Greater or equal: >=
Less: <
Less or equal: <=
Contains: INCLUDE
Does not contain: NOTINCLUDE

Logical operators:
AND: &
OR: |

ONLY SINGLE SIGNS. Before evaluation the engine doubles them itself: & becomes
&&, | becomes ||. Written double from the start it turns into &&&& — the expression
is not parsed, and the condition SILENTLY becomes false. The branch never fires,
and no error is visible. The words И, ИЛИ, AND, OR are silently false in the same way.

NOT EQUAL — ONLY !=. The "<>" familiar from SQL and the spreadsheet editor is not
understood by the engine: it replaces = with ==, and leaves "<>" as it is, and the expression
is not parsed. The condition is again SILENTLY false. An expensive error: in a taxi bot
the check "the driver is not blocked", written with "<>", turned off
order intake for all drivers, and there was not a single line in the log.

QUOTES ON BOTH SIDES OR ON NEITHER. The engine compares the sides of a condition
AS TEXT, together with the quotes. So [Answer] = 'c~ok' compares c~ok
with the string 'c~ok' — with quotes inside — and NEVER matches. The condition
is silently false: the branch does not fire at all, and with != on the contrary — it fires
always. There is nothing in the debug log.

ALWAYS WRITE IT LIKE THIS: '[Answer]' = 'c~ok', '#{status}' = 'paid',
'#{role}' != 'owner'. Compare numbers without quotes on both sides:
#{has_booking} = 1, #{price} > 500. Quotes on one side of a number
(#{shift} = '1') work only while the variable really holds a number:
an empty value or text turns the branch off. Do not rely on it.

An expensive error: in a reception bot all request confirmation buttons
and the code entry to the account were written this way — not a single button worked, and the customer
saw only "the bot does not work as it should".

The case of values matters: 'new' and 'New' are different values. Spaces inside
compared values are allowed: '#{status}' = 'in progress' works.

Condition examples:

'[Answer]' INCLUDE 'buy' The condition checks whether the user's last answer contains the word "buy"
'[CurrentDate]' = '[Answer]' The condition checks whether the user's last answer equals today's date (format dd.mm.yyyy)
30 > #{count} The condition checks whether the variable count is less than 30
You can compare dates in the formats:

dd.mm.yyyy
dd/mm/yyyy
dd-mm-yyyy
dd.mm.yy
dd/mm/yy
dd-mm-yy
A date may also contain time in the format hh:mm after a space.

If you use commands starting with the / sign in conditions, put both parts of the expression in quotes. For example: '[Answer]' = '/command'. This is needed for the condition to fire correctly, so that the / sign is taken by the interpreter as part of the text, not as a division sign.

Templates and functions are used in message texts, expressions and conditions of blocks and actions. The full list is below.

THE ORDER OF SUBSTITUTIONS (important for nesting).
Substitutions are performed strictly in stages, one after another:
1) Variables #{variable} — substituted FIRST (including templates from list
   selections, processed by a repeated pass).
2) Answer templates: [ALastID], [ALast], [AnswerID(id)], [AnswerN], [Answer]/[A].
3) Scenario data: [DataN].
4) Subscriber data: [Name], [FullName], [ClientID], [Messenger], [BotID], dates,
   [GenStrN], [GenNumN], referral templates etc.
5) Calculation functions: [DaysBetween], [Round], [SumInWords], [AddDate],
   [AddMinutes], [WeekDay], [DiffDates], [DiffMinutes], [StrLen], [GeneratePhone].
6) The mathematical expression {{ ... }} — evaluated LAST.

THE FIRST CONSEQUENCE (use it actively): since variables and answer templates
are substituted BEFORE calculation functions and the {{ }} expression, they CAN be used
as arguments inside substitution functions and inside expressions. Examples:
- [Round(#{sum}, 2)] — rounds the value of the variable sum;
- [AddDate(#{startdate}, #{days})] — adds a days variable to a date variable;
- [WeekDay([AnswerID(t3Xdd2d0)])] — the weekday of a date from the subscriber's answer;
- [DiffMinutes(#{date1}, [CurrentDate] 23:59)] — a difference with a template substituted;
- [SumInWords(#{total}, true, false)] — a sum in words from a variable;
- {{ #{price} * #{count} }} — an expression with variables;
- [Files(#{photos})] — sending files by links from a variable.
That is, in function arguments ([...]) and in {{...}} expressions freely write
#{variables} and answer/data templates — by the time the function works they will already
be replaced with real values. Do NOT rely on a function inside a function ([Round([StrLen(...)])])
— functions of the same stage do not see each other.

TAKE A FUNCTION NAME ONLY FROM THE LIST BELOW. The engine does not touch an unknown name
at all — the text "[AddDays(15.08.2026,
14)]" goes into the variable, list or message instead of a date, and no error is visible. Adding days — [AddDate(date, N)],
minutes — [AddMinutes(date, M)]; a negative N subtracts. Do not invent similar names
copied from memory: they do not exist.

THE SECOND CONSEQUENCE (a frequent error): THE {{ }} EXPRESSION MUST NOT BE PUT
INSIDE A [ ] FUNCTION. It is evaluated AFTER the functions, so at the moment the function works it still
holds "{{ 3 * 60 }}", the argument is not recognised, and the function SILENTLY stays
a string — the variable gets "[AddMinutes(20.08.2026 10:00, 180)]" instead of a date.
No error is visible anywhere, and everything that depended on this date simply does not work.
  WRONG:   [AddMinutes(#{date}, {{ #{hours} * 60 }})]
  CORRECT: step 1: #{minutes} = {{ #{hours} * 60 }}
           step 2: [AddMinutes(#{date}, #{minutes})]
A variable inside a function always works, so does a number — including a negative one:
[AddMinutes(#{date}, -1440)] — a day before.

           Substitution templates and functions of blocks
#{variable}
A variable template for storing data. It can be a string or an array (when a selection from a list or Google Sheets is saved/added, or in some actions). As soon as the bot scenario ends or is restarted by /start, the variables disappear. Variables are visible in all blocks and actions.

Simple mathematical expressions using other variables and templates can be saved into a variable.

#{g_globalbname}
The value of a global variable. The prefix g_ is added to the variable name, for example globalbname, giving #{g_globalname}
READ GLOBAL VARIABLES EXACTLY LIKE THIS — by the substitution #{g_Name}, like an ordinary variable. A separate action for reading is not needed; do not spend a scenario step on it. The name after g_ is written exactly as the global variable is named in the global property, including spaces and case: #{g_Exchange rate}.
It works EVERYWHERE: in the block text, in buttons and in block conditions — and in all fields of an action scenario: in action conditions, in exit messages, in the fields of any action, in JS code.
The value is read at the moment the block is sent, so it is always fresh: if the "before sending" scenario changed the variable, the same block already shows the new value. There is no need to copy a global variable into an ordinary one for display.
The "Data operations" action is needed only to CHANGE a global variable (qb.cfg.setGlobal) — for reading it is redundant.

[Answer] or [A] or [Ответ]
The subscriber's answer template. It is replaced with the subscriber's last answer.
IMPORTANT: on the FIRST message of a chain it is empty. An answer appears only when
the subscriber answers a block that has already been shown, and at the chain launch
there was nothing to show. The text of the request itself always lies in the variable
#{_message} — read it if you need to react to the first message
(a review on a marketplace, a question about an ad, a request in a chat).

[Name]
The subscriber's first name

[FullName]
The subscriber's first and last name

[ClientLink]
A link to the subscriber's profile in the messenger, if possible

[UserName]
The subscriber's username in Telegram or MAX without "@" (output it as @[UserName]). No username or another messenger — empty: check with the condition '[UserName]' != '' before substituting. For a contact in an ad or a request this is a ready "My @username" button instead of typing by hand

[Date]
The current date in the format dd.mm.yyyy

[GenStrN]
Generates a string of N characters. Contains the characters a-zA-Z0-9

[GenNumN]
Generates a digit string of N characters

[AnswerN]
Holds the subscriber's answer to the block with ordinal number N. Also holds the subscriber's press of a Value-type button

[ALast(X,Y,..)]
Holds the subscriber's answer that came last. X,Y — ordinal numbers of blocks

[AnswerID(id)]
Holds the subscriber's answer to the block whose id is given

[ALastID(id1,id2,..)]
Holds the subscriber's answer that came last from the blocks whose ids are given

[DataN]
Data that came into the scenario from another scenario or by a request to an external server, where N is the data id (may be digits or letters). It appears, for example, when data is passed through buttons into other users' scenarios.

{{ X + Y }}
An expression to be evaluated

[Actions] or [External]
The action scenario template, replaced with the message returned by the action scenario back to the scenario. Used only in the block's message field

[SendContact(phone,first_name,last_name)]
Sends a contact. Example: [SendContact(+7913587xxxx,Ivan,Petrov)]. For use in Telegram and the bot landing page.

[SendLocation(latitude,longitude)]
Sends a map by the latitude and longitude coordinates. Example: [SendLocation(55.751971853583576,37.61750669332571)]. For use in Telegram.

[SendVenue(latitude,longitude,title, address)]
Sends a map with an event venue by the latitude and longitude coordinates. Example: [SendVenue(55.751971853583576,37.61750669332571,Coffee shop,123 Tea St.)]. For use in Telegram.

[Sticker(emoji|messenger|identifier)]
Sends a sticker as a separate message, and the emoji of the template stays in the text. The engine sets the marker itself when the subscriber sent a sticker — there is no need to compose it. The short form [Sticker(identifier)] sends a sticker by the identifier from the variable #{sticker_id}. Telegram and MAX. In MAX the marker sometimes has a fourth part — a link to the sticker picture: it is what goes to other messengers, as an ordinary attachment, so the subscriber sees the sticker itself. MAX does not always give the picture, and the engine does not forward a placeholder — then the word "sticker" stays in another messenger. A sticker from Telegram also stays an emoji in another messenger: Telegram gives no picture link.

[SumInWords(SUM,ruble,kopek)]
Replaces the number SUM with words. ruble and kopek can take the value true or false, used to add the words rubles and kopecks

[DaysBetween(date1,date2)]
Calculates the difference in days between the dates date2 and date1

[DaysInMonth]
Outputs the number of days in the current month

[CharUp]
Makes the character following the template uppercase. The template itself is removed

[CharLow]
Makes the character following the template lowercase. The template itself is removed

[Exit]
Replaced with the keyword for exiting the scenario. Used in the exit message. Only for blocks

[StrLen(String)]
The template returns the length of a string. Templates or variables can be used (in action scenarios)

[Round(Number, Precision)] or [Round(Number)]
The template rounds a number to a certain digit. If the precision is not given, the number is rounded to an integer

[ClientID]
Subscribers from messengers and the bot landing page have an ID that identifies them. The template is replaced with the ID of the current subscriber. Used, in particular, for sending messages to the bot, for selecting or saving information about the subscriber in a list or a table

[Messenger]
The messenger or bot landing page the subscriber uses. For Telegram — telegram, for MAX — max, for Viber — viber, for VK — vk, for WhatsApp — wa, for the bot landing page — lp, for Jivo — jivo

[BotID]
The ID of the bot through which the subscriber interacts with your autofunnel. Used, in particular, for sending messages to the bot, for selecting or saving information about the subscriber in a list or a table

[Files(url1, url2...)]
A template for sending photos/files to the bot by their URL (link). The template is removed from the message text, and the listed photos/files are sent to the bot. Used, in particular, to send files from a list selection. Instead of a URL a variable holding comma-separated URLs can be given, or comma-separated variables holding links to photos/files. If several photos are sent in Telegram, they can be grouped by ticking the corresponding option in the settings of the block where the template is used.

For example, you get a picture generated by a third-party API and send it to the subscriber in the bot.

[GenerateEmail]
Generates a random email address

[GeneratePhone(prefix)]
Generates a random phone number, where prefix is the country code, for example [GeneratePhone(+7)]

/to + block ID
A jump to a certain scenario block, for example /toABCD

[LineBreak]
A line break. Used, for example, to send a message directly through the Telegram bot API to a group or a channel

Substitution templates and functions of the action scenario
note
All block templates can be used in an action scenario. The templates and functions for an action scenario are listed below. To show a message from an action scenario in a block, use the template [Actions] in the block field

[Random(X,Y)]
Returns a random number between X and Y

[AddDate(date,N)]
Adds N days to the date date and returns a date like dd.mm.yyyy

[AddMinutes(date,M)]
Adds M minutes to the date date (dd.mm.yyyy or dd.mm.yyyy hh:mm) and returns a date like dd.mm.yyyy hh:mm

[DiffDates(date1, date2)]
Calculates the difference between dates in days. The dates are entered in the format dd.mm.yyyy

[DiffMinutes(date1, date2)]
Calculates the difference between dates in minutes. The dates are entered in the format dd.mm.yyyy hh:mm

[WeekDay(date)]
Determines the weekday by the date date. Returns a number from 1 to 7, where 1 is Monday, 2 is Tuesday etc.

[CurrentMessage]
Returns the ordinal number of the block at which the scenario execution stage is

[CurrentMessageID]
Returns the ID of the block at which the scenario execution stage is

[PrevMessage]
Returns the ordinal number of the previous block the scenario was on

[PrevMessageID]
Returns the ID of the previous block the scenario was on

[CurrentWeekDay]
The current weekday. Returns a number from 1 to 7

[CurrentDate]
Returns the current date in the format dd.mm.yyyy

[NextDate]
Returns tomorrow's date in the format dd.mm.yyyy

[CurrentDay]
The current day of the month

[CurrentDayNormal]
The current day of the month with a leading zero

[CurrentMonth]
The number of the current month. Returns a number from 0 to 11, where 0 is January, 1 is February etc.

[CurrentMonthNormal]
The number of the current month with a leading zero. Returns a number from 01 to 12, where 01 is January, 02 is February etc.

[CurrentYear]
The current year in the format yyyy

[CurrentTime]
The current time according to the account's time zone settings, in the format hh:mm

[CreationDate]
The date the subscriber was added to the database, in the format dd.mm.yyyy

THE ACCOUNT TIME ZONE. [Date], [CurrentDate], [NextDate], [CurrentDay], [CurrentWeekDay], [CurrentMonth], [CurrentYear], [CurrentTime], comparing dates with today in conditions and in list filters, delayed chain sendings "at an exact time" are all calculated by the engine in the owner's ACCOUNT time zone (Account settings → "Time zone", by default UTC+03:00 Moscow), not by the server clock.
In JS code Date.now() is UTC, and the server clock is UTC too. For the code to live in the same zone as the templates, take the local date and time with a template in quotes right in the code and calculate the offset:
  var m = '[CurrentDate]|[CurrentTime]'.match(/(\d{2})\.(\d{2})\.(\d{4})\|(\d{1,2}):(\d{2})/)
  var offset = m ? Math.round((Date.UTC(+m[3], +m[2] - 1, +m[1], +m[4], +m[5]) - Date.now()) / 900000) * 900000 : 3 * 3600000
  var local = new Date(Date.now() + offset)   // from here only getUTC…: the hour is local.getUTCHours()
Then your own global variable "Time zone" is not needed — one setting instead of two, and they will not diverge.
A BOOKING, RESERVATION, REMINDER OR SCHEDULE BOT without the right zone offers past slots and reminds at the wrong hour. Remind the owner in the code-entry text (or in "Check the connection"): "Account settings → Time zone — your city: the bot uses it to calculate today, free slots and reminder times".

Comparing dates
Dates are compared directly in the condition and expression field, using values, substitution templates or variables. That is, you can write #{date1} > [CurrentDate] to compare dates. Also in list selections you can compare a field containing a date with a date you give in the value field

[Block]
The name of the current block

[PrevBlock]
The name of the previous block

[Self]
Replaced with the current value of the field when working with lists, Google Sheets and CRM
Replaced with the current value of the deal ID and the contact ID in the CRM system

[GlobalVar]
Holds the current value of a global variable. For use in the "Data operations" action if you need to change the current value of a global variable or assign a global variable to an ordinary variable.

[AccountActive]
Outputs 1 if the account is active and 0 if the account is inactive

[AccountTrial]
Outputs 1 if the account is on a trial period and 0 if the trial period has ended

[AccountTariff]
Outputs the id of the current tariff

For the referral system in Telegram
[Balance]
The subscriber's balance

[Paid]
The sum of payouts

[Level]
The level in the referral system

[RefererClientID]
The referrer's ClientID

[RefererClientLink]
A link to the referrer's profile in Telegram

[RefererFullName]
The referrer's full name in Telegram

[RefererName]
The referrer's first name in Telegram

System variables
Listed here are the system variables — those filled in automatically by the constructor while the bot works. They can be used in message texts, expressions and conditions.

How to output a value
To output the value of a system variable, wrap its name in the construction:

#{variable_name}

For example: #{_botname}, #{_request}.

Technical identifiers
All variable names start with the character _ (underscore) — this is the distinguishing sign of system variables.

Requests and answers
Variable	Description
_request	The request from the messenger sent to the constructor. Stored as a JSON string.
_request_headers	The incoming request header (only for receiving a webhook). Stored as a JSON string.
_response	The server's answer to a request to the constructor's webhook. In the action scenarios "Data exchange" (Waiting for data) and CRM (Receive from CRM) assign the answer to this variable.
_answer The subscriber's last answer to a block
Who sent the webhook
When a scenario webhook is called by another Quescha bot or a mini app,
the subscriber is laid out into separate variables — so that the request JSON need not
be parsed for the sake of a filter by ClientID.
IMPORTANT: these three variables exist ONLY in a scenario launched by a webhook
or a mini app. In a scenario called by a block they DO NOT EXIST — there the subscriber's
data is taken with the templates [ClientID], [Messenger], [BotID]. An unknown
variable is substituted with an empty string, silently.
Variable	Description
_clientid	The ClientID of the subscriber on whose behalf the request came. Only on a webhook.
_messenger	This subscriber's messenger. Only on a webhook.
_channelid	The identifier of the channel (bot) they belong to. Only on a webhook.
Bot information
Variable	Description
_botname	The bot name.
_bottype	The bot type — the designation of the channel or bot landing page: telegram, max, wa, wabusiness, vk, viber, instagram, fb, avito, cian, wb, ozon, yandexmarket, tiktok, lp, jivo.
_botuser	The bot's username.
_botid	The bot's BotID.
_lphost	The host of the website or bot landing page, for bots launched through the website widget or a bot landing page.
Scenario state
Variable	Description
_message	The text of the incoming message — the very one the subscriber is contacting with right now. Always filled, including on the FIRST message of a chain, when [Answer] is still empty.
_isstart	The scenario launch flag: 0 — no, 1 — yes. At the chain launch the [Answer] of the first block's scenario is EMPTY if the message text does not start with "/" (this is how the website widget's "start" and the first message of a VK client that launched the chain arrive); the text itself is in #{_message}.
_sendtomessid	An object created by "Send to bot". Holds the markers, the recipients and the message IDs for deletion.
_error	Holds the error for the action "Working with groups and channels in Telegram": the messenger's answer (JSON with description). The request broke off without an answer — the string "нет ответа: <code>": this is a connection failure, not a settings error; say "try again later", not "add the bot as an administrator".
Outputting a list to the bot and paging
Filled by the operation "Output a list to the bot" (the "Data operations" action,
a list operation): it sends the selected rows as separate messages,
N per page. Available in the message template and in the buttons of this operation.
The page number is taken from the subscriber's last answer: a "Forward" button
with nextData equal to #{_nextPage} returns the number, and the next call of the operation
outputs exactly that page. A number greater than the number of pages does not break the output — the last
one is shown.
Variable	Description
_listItemNum	The ordinal number of the row in the selected list, starting from 1.
_listItem	The row marker like item_3. It is also substituted into buttons — so a button knows which row it belongs to.
_currentPage	The number of the shown page.
_nextPage	The number of the next page — for the "Forward" button.
_prevPage	The number of the previous page — for the "Back" button.
_pageItems	How many rows per page (from the operation setting, 5 by default).
_pageTotal	The total number of pages with this page size.
_listItemIds	Service. The IDs of the sent messages of the page — by them the previous page is deleted from the chat before the next one is output (Telegram only).
Contact and location
Variable	Description
_iscontact	Tracks receiving a contact by the button. Values: 0 or 1.
_contact_name	The contact's name (WhatsApp only).
_islocation	Tracks receiving a location by the button. Values: 0 or 1.
client_latitude	The latitude of the point sent by the subscriber. Without an underscore at the start. Filled both by a one-time location by the button and by EVERY update of live sharing (livegeo). Available only in an action scenario.
client_longitude	The longitude of the point sent by the subscriber. Without an underscore at the start. Filled both by a one-time location by the button and by every update of live sharing (livegeo).
_location_address	The address sent through choosing a location (WhatsApp only).
metrikauserid	The ClientID of a website visitor for Yandex Metrica goals. The name is WITHOUT an underscore. From the bot landing page and the widget it comes by itself; in Telegram and MAX it must be saved from the link's start parameter (#{start}). An empty variable — goals do not go to Metrica at all.
Media and files
Variable	Description
_ismedia	1 if the subscriber sent a media file (photo, video, video note, document, voice, audio, sticker), otherwise 0.
_media_type	The media file type: photo, video, video_note (a Telegram video note), document, voice, audio, sticker.
_media_types	An array of file types. Filled when the subscriber sent several files at once (Telegram, MAX, VK).
_fileId	The ID of the uploaded file (Telegram and MAX).
_caption	The caption the subscriber sent the file with ("UPD 451"). Empty if there is no caption or what came is not a file.
_file_name	The name of the sent document ("upd-451.pdf"), if the messenger passed it. A photo has no name.
_resendonefile	How many files are forwarded: 1 — one, 0 — several. For the "Receive and forward media" action.
_yadisklink	A temporary download link of a file after uploading to Yandex Disk.
The file link and the list of attachments
When the subscriber sends media, the link to the file goes into the variable media_url, and the full list of links to all attachments of one message — into media_urls (each link on a new line). Both variables are written without an underscore (see also the Receive and forward media action).

Variable	Description
media_url	The link to the received file (the first attachment).
media_urls	The list of links to all attachments of the message, one per line. Filled for marketplace channels (Avito, Wildberries, Ozon, Cian, TikTok), and after the "Receive and forward media" action — with links to all forwarded files.
media_count	How many files the "Receive and forward media" action forwarded. One file — 1.
media_sent	Whether the forwarding reached the recipients: 1 — all of them, 0 — someone did not get it, empty — there were no recipients. Filled by the "Receive and forward media" action. Counted over the whole incoming message: if there are two intake actions, the result of the second does not overwrite the failure of the first.
media_notsent	How many sendings failed for this message.
media_error	A short reason of the last failed sending.
sticker_id	The identifier of the sent sticker: file_id in Telegram, a code in MAX.
sticker_emoji	The sticker's emoji (Telegram).
sticker_set	The name of the set the sticker is from (Telegram).
sticker_url	A link to the sticker picture (MAX).
Where the link comes from and how long it lives
Messengers (Telegram, MAX, WhatsApp etc.) — the file is downloaded to the server, the link is temporary: Telegram — 1 minute, Meta channels (WhatsApp Business, Instagram, Facebook) — 10 minutes. If it is needed longer, save it to Yandex Disk or Google Drive at once.
Marketplaces (Avito, Wildberries, Ozon, Cian, TikTok) — media_url / media_urls receive the marketplace's own direct links (without re-hosting). For Wildberries reviews, the photos and videos attached by the buyer to the review get here.
Payments and online receipts under 54-FZ
When to fill
These variables must be assigned before the payment link is generated — otherwise the receipt does not go out.

Variable	Description
_receipt_email	The buyer's e-mail for online receipts under 54-FZ.
_receipt_phone	The buyer's phone for online receipts under 54-FZ.
ItemsID	The order contents for writing off the stock of catalog products. The name is WITHOUT an underscore. The engine fills it ITSELF when an order returns from the "Catalog" mini app (the cat_order operation) — no separate action is needed. For the stock to be written off, pass it to the payment link as a payloads element { name: "ItemsID", data: "#{ItemsID}" }: after the payment is accepted the products are written off automatically. If no write-off is needed, just do not add this element.
AI model

_aiContextReset	"1" if the conversation with the AI model was started anew: the conversation outgrew the model's window. "0" — the conversation continues.

Classified boards and marketplaces
The constructor fills these variables for incoming events from classified boards (Avito, Cian), marketplaces (Wildberries, Ozon, Yandex Market) and social networks (Instagram, TikTok). They are filled only in the corresponding channel. The text of the message/question/review comes as an ordinary incoming message — read it with the variable #{_message}, and attachments — in the variables media_url / media_urls (see the section "Media and files").

HOW A SUBSCRIBER ON A MARKETPLACE IS ARRANGED. On Wildberries a review and a question are NOT a conversation: each event comes from a separate subscriber (the code f<id> for a review, q<id> for a question, c<chatId> for a chat), and there will never be a second message from them. Hence three rules:
- the reviews/questions chain is ONE block; each block message is published as a reply to the review, so a second block would publish a second reply;
- there can be no dialog: there is nobody to ask clarifying questions, the whole parsing is done in one pass of the scenario;
- an empty answer publishes nothing — this is what to do when a person must answer: the scenario exits without exitmes and at the same time sends a notification to an employee.
The review rating comes in #{_wb_rating} (1–5), the event type — in #{_wb_kind}. On Ozon and in the WB chat it is an ordinary conversation with the buyer.

Avito
Variable	Description
_avito_chat_id	The chat ID (a dialog about an ad).
_avito_author_id	The ID of the message author (the buyer).
_avito_item_id	The ad ID.
_avito_chat_type	The chat type (for example, u2i).
_avito_message_type	The message type: text, image, link.
Cian
Variable	Description
_cian_chat_id	The ID of the chat/dialog about the ad.
_cian_user_id	The user (client) ID.
Wildberries
Variable	Description
_wb_kind	The event type: question, feedback (review), chat (chat with the buyer).
_wb_id	The ID of the question or review (for question/feedback).
_wb_product	The product name.
_wb_nmid	The WB article (nmId).
_wb_rating	The review rating (1–5).
_wb_user	The buyer's name/identifier.
_wb_chatid	The chat ID (for kind=chat).
_wb_event_id	The chat event ID (for kind=chat).
Ozon
Variable	Description
_ozon_chat_id	The chat ID.
_ozon_message_id	The message ID.
_ozon_user_id	The buyer ID.
Yandex Market
Variable	Description
_ym_kind	The event type: chat, review.
_ym_chatid	The chat ID (for kind=chat).
_ym_from	The sender of the chat message.
_ym_msg_id	The chat message ID.
_ym_id	The review ID (for kind=review).
_ym_rating	The review rating.
_ym_author	The review author.
_ym_nmid	The product identifier.
Instagram
Variable	Description
_ig_event	The event type: message, postback, reaction, referral, optin, story_reply, story_mention, deleted, comment, live_comment, mention.
_ig_reaction	The reaction type (love, like …) — for the reaction event.
_ig_reaction_action	The reaction action: react / unreact.
_ig_referral	The referral marker (an ig.me link / an ad).
_ig_referral_source	The referral source.
_ig_story_id	The story ID (a reply to a story).
_ig_reply_to_mid	The ID of the message being replied/reacted to.
_ig_comment_id	The comment ID (for comment/mention).
_ig_media_id	The post/Reels ID.
_ig_parent_id	The parent comment ID (a reply to a comment).
_ig_username	The username of the comment author.
TikTok
Variable	Description
_tiktok_conversation_id	The dialog ID.
_tiktok_user_id	The user ID.
Web App: catalog and calendar
Variable	Description
_webapp_id	The app ID.
_webapp_title	The app name.
_webapp_url	The app URL.
_webapp_type	The Web App type: cat — catalog, cal — calendar.
_webapp_order_rubrics	The categories (comma-separated) the ordered products are in.
_delrecid	The ID of a deleted Web App calendar booking.
_delrecwebappid	The ID of the Web App the booking was deleted from.

Chat bot scenarios.
Each scenario has its own set of data that fills variables and substitution templates. For each subscriber this set may be unique. When a scenario is restarted by /start all variables are reset, so it often makes sense to keep some data in lists, to be able to get it at any moment.
A scenario consists of blocks and action scenarios.

An example JSON export of a request-collecting chat bot that sends the request to the administrator. With comments after //. Study all the explanatory comments carefully so as not to make mistakes when generating the bot.
{
  "groupnull": false, // whether the export has bot elements without a group, boolean
  "groups": [ // the array of exported groups. Groups are needed to group separate projects with bots.
    {
      "id": 5239, // group id, num
      "name": "Sending requests in a chat bot", // group name, string
      "ordernumber": 1 // ordinal number in the interface, num
    }
  ],
  "chains": [ // message chains
    {
      "id": 14039, // chain id, num
      "code": "505186607020537", // chain code, string
      "name": "Data collection", // name
      "ordernumber": 93, // ordinal number in the interface, num
      "BotGroupId": 5239, // id of the group the chain is bound to, num
      "channel": "[]", // channels for sending messages (messengers, bot landing pages, widget)
      "active": true, // on/off, boolean
      "sended": 0, // messages sent, num
      "planned": 0, // waiting to be sent, num
      "canceled": 0, // cancelled, num
      "addtagstart": "null", // tags that launch the chain when added. Tags are added in message buttons or in buttons of scenario blocks/action scenarios and in action scenario settings, string
      "deltagstart": "null", // tags that launch the chain when removed. Tags are removed in message buttons or in buttons of scenario blocks/action scenarios and in action scenario settings, string
      "addtagstop": "null", // tags that stop the chain when added. Tags are added in message buttons or in buttons of scenario blocks/action scenarios and in action scenario settings, string
      "deltagstop": "null", // tags that stop the chain when removed. Tags are removed in message buttons or in buttons of scenario blocks/action scenarios and in action scenario settings, string
      "keywords": "null", // keywords from subscribers' messages that launch the chain. They work if no scenario is running for the subscriber, string
      "dialog": false, // launch the chain when the subscriber writes. Used for example in WhatsApp, Jivo, since there is no bot start button there, boolean
      "nothandled": false, // launch the chain if no chain was launched by other conditions, boolean
      "subscription": true, // launch the chain on subscription (pressing the Start button), boolean
      "rangetime": false, // sending on given days or in a time interval, boolean
      "rangedays": "[\"пн\",\"вт\",\"ср\",\"чт\",\"пт\"]", // sending days — the engine's Russian day codes, write them exactly: пн, вт, ср, чт, пт, сб, вс (Mon … Sun)
      "rangetimestart": 0, // the start of the sending time interval in milliseconds, num, the hours and minutes given in rtshour and rtsminute converted to milliseconds
      "rtshour": "00", // the start of the sending time interval — hours
      "rtsminute": "00", // the start of the sending time interval — minutes
      "rangetimeend": 0, // the end of the sending time interval in milliseconds, num, the hours and minutes given in rtehour and rteminute converted to milliseconds
      "rtehour": "00", // the end of the sending time interval — hours
      "rteminute": "00", // the end of the sending time interval — minutes
      "additional": null, // always null
      "webhook": null, // always null
      "email": "[]", // emails for sending a notification when the chain is launched
      "hidden": false, // always false
      "messenger": "[]" // sending to messengers when the chain is launched
    }
  ],

      "dimension": "now", // days, hours, minutes, immediate sending day|hour|minute|now. now — only for the first step (ordernumber:0)

Chain messages.
The message property of the messages array holds a message with buttons or a scenario of blocks. Chain messages may or may not be a scenario. Non-scenario messages are sent according to the sending interval settings. An example of a scenario message of 4 blocks (after JSON.parse()):

      [
  { // the start block (the first)
    "id": "BZJU41f5", // block id, a string a-zA-Z0-9
    "x": -320, // the block's coordinate on the board along the x axis
    "y": -39, // the block's coordinate on the board along the y axis
    "order": 0, // the block's ordinal number from 0
    "text": "Leave a request, it will be processed shortly", // the text of the block's message that is sent to the bot subscriber
    "media": [], // the array of attached images, always leave empty
    "btn": [ // the array of attached buttons
      {
        "id": 0, // button id, an ordinal number from 0
        "name": "Leave a request", // button name
        "url": "", // the link when action equals link
        "action": "nextmessage", // a mandatory parameter, the button type: link/action/stop/nextmessage/nextdata/stepback/stopscenario.  stepback — a step back in the scenario,  stopscenario — exit from the scenario
        "addtag": null, // a serialized array of tags to add, for the action button type
        "deltag": null, // a serialized array of tags to remove, for the action button type
        "nextMessage": { // an object with the data of the block to jump to, for the nextmessage button type
          "label": "Name", // block name
          "value": "0yj9iLem" // block id
        },
        "nextData": "", // the button value that is put into the subscriber's answers on press, for the nextdata button type
        "data": "", // a data string passed in the button, for the nextmessage and action button types. The data is passed separated by the | sign and is put into the scenario as the templates [Data1], [Data2] etc. in order
		"intentBtn": null // the button colour for Telegram and MAX. A string, one of the engine values ['Синий', 'Зеленый', 'Красный'] (blue, green, red) — write them exactly in Russian
      }
    ],
    "number": false, // check that the subscriber's answer is a number, boolean
    "regex": false, // check the subscriber's answer by a regular expression, boolean
    "regexval": "", // the regular expression
    "email": false, // check that the subscriber's answer is an email, boolean
    "phone": false, // check that the subscriber's answer is a phone number, boolean
    "getdata": false, // request data by url, boolean
    "getdataurl": null, // url for requesting data
    "externalbefore": { // the action scenario to launch before the block is executed
      "label": "get the request recipient for notifications", // action scenario name
      "value": 0 // the constructor fills in the id, the link goes by the name above
    },
    "acceptpayment": false, // marks that the block accepts payments. On a notification from the payment provider this block is launched, boolean
	"dontwaitanswer": false, // move to the next block without waiting for the user's answer. The maximum number of no-wait blocks in a row is 10, boolean
  "answervar": '', // the name of the variable to save the subscriber's answer to the block
	"fileid": "", // the fileId of a file. A file of any type is sent: photo, document, video, audio, voice, sticker — the engine chooses the method itself. A FileId can be obtained by uploading the file to the bot. For this, output the variable #{_fileId} in the next block. For Telegram and MAX, string
	"fileidnote": false, // send the file from fileid as a VIDEO NOTE (sendVideoNote, Telegram only). Turn it on only if fileid holds a video note identifier: Telegram does not accept an ordinary video with this flag, and a video note without the flag goes out as a rectangular video. A video note has NO caption — the block text goes as a separate message, boolean
    "waitinterval": "", // the delay before the block is launched, a numeric string
    "waitunit": null, // an object with the delay time unit. Options: { label: 'Секунд', value: 'sec' }, { label: 'Минут', value: 'minute' }, { label: 'Часов', value: 'hour' }, { label: 'Дней', value: 'day' } (the labels are the editor's, write them as they are)

    "ticklim": "", // the number of repeats of sending the block's message if the delay is less than 60 seconds, a numeric string
    "wronganswer": null, // the block to show if the answer failed the check: { "label": "Name", /* block name */ "value": "0yj9iLem" /* block id */ }. ONLY an object or null — the engine does not show a string, and the bot stays silent
    "conditionlist": [ // an array executed in the block after the answer from the subscriber is received. If the array has one element, there are no conditions. If more than one, conditions have been added. Conditions are written as a string, for example: [Answer] = 1 means that the last answer must equal 1. All comparison signs: = (equal), != (not equal), > (greater), >= (greater or equal), < (less), <= (less or equal), INCLUDE (contains), NOTINCLUDE (does not contain). There may be several logical expressions. The right and left parts of an expression must be in single quotes. If conditionlist has more than one element, the nextmessage of the first element becomes the "no condition matched" case. All properties are mandatory. If nextdata buttons are output in a block or into a block by an action scenario, they must be processed in the conditions, or on a transition to another block, in the conditions of an action scenario. ATTENTION: elements of conditionlist with an index greater than 0 (added conditions) have an extra field "name" (a string like "Условие #N") — the condition name. The first element (index 0) has no name field.
      {
        "nextmessage": { // the block to jump to
          "label": "Name", // block name
          "value": "0yj9iLem" // block id
        },
        "getdata": false, // request data by url, boolean
        "getdataurl": null, // url for requesting data
        "externalafter": null, // the action scenario to launch after the answer is received. Holds an object like the externalbefore property
        "exitwait": false, // exit from the scenario waiting for an answer. After the answer is received the scenario ends
        "exitatonce": false, // exit from the scenario without waiting for an answer
        "webhookcheck": false, // sending a webhook with the collected data on exiting the scenario
        "webhook": null, // a string with the webhook url
        "emailcheck": false, // sending the collected data by email on exiting the scenario
        "email": null, // a string of an array of emails
        "messengercheck": false, // sending the collected data to an employee on exiting the scenario, always false
        "messenger": null, // the employee to send to, always null
        "addtagcheck": false,// adding tags on exiting the scenario
        "addtag": [], // the array of tags
        "deltagcheck": false, // removing tags on exiting the scenario
        "deltag": [], // the array of tags
        "tagdata": "", // a data string passed with adding/removing tags. If a scenario is launched by this, the data is passed into the scenario as the templates [Data1], [Data2] etc. Pass the data separated by |
        "cond": "", // the condition string, filled when conditionlist has more than one element — mandatory to fill
        "metrika": false, // sending goals to Yandex Metrica
        "metrikaid": [], // an array of Yandex Metrica JS goals [{"id":"target1","sum":"0","cur":"RUB"},{"id":"target2","sum":"0","cur":"RUB"}]
        "metrikaname": null, // the Yandex account {"label":"vamtak","value":1}, where label is the name, value — the account id in the database.
        "metrikacounter": "" // the Yandex Metrica counter number
      }
    ],
    "messengercheck": false, // sending a message to an employee together with the answer to the subscriber. More — the section "SENDING TO AN EMPLOYEE"
    "messengerall": false, // true — send to ALL active employees of the account, then messenger is not needed
    "messenger": null, // specific employees: [{"label":"Name","value":123}] from the passed employees list. With messengerall true — null
    "messengermes": "", // the text of the message to the employee, substitution templates and variables work
    "messengerbtn": [], // the buttons of the message to the employee, arranged like the block's btn
    "messengermedia": [], // the attachments of the message to the employee, file names
    "messengerfileid": "" // the fileId of a file (photo, document, video…) for the message to the employee (Telegram/MAX)
  },
  {
    "id": "0yj9iLem",
    "x": -81,
    "y": -38,
    "order": 1,
    "text": "Enter your name",
    "media": [],
    "btn": [],

        "addtagcheck": false,
        "addtag": [],
        "deltagcheck": false,
        "deltag": [],
        "tagdata": "",
        "cond": "",
        "metrika": false,

        "metrikacounter": ""
      }

    "name": "Phone",
    "btnsettings": { // extra block settings
      "tg": { // for Telegram
        "inrow": [], // an array of the number of buttons per row. The last element applies to all following rows of buttons. Example: ["3","2"]
        "inline": true, // true — show buttons under the message, false — buttons in the keyboard, true by default
        "clearkeyboard": false, // clearing the keyboard, false by default

THE SUBSCRIBER'S DIRECTORY: "MY CARS", "MY ADDRESSES", "MY PETS". When a person
books not for the first time, asking the same things again is bad. A list
is created with its own fields plus ClientID (an autofield), and then the choice goes by
buttons, and input only for something new.

  List: Make and model | Plate number | ... | ClientID (filled by itself)
  The choice screen — a block with [Actions], a "before sending" scenario:
    • reads the list with the filter ClientID = [ClientID] into array variables;
    • found — an exit with the message "Choose a car" and buttons
      from the arrays: name: "#{make}", nextData: "#{plate}" (one button
      per row), plus "Add another" and "Delete" by transitions to input blocks;
    • not found — an exit "Launch block" straight to the first adding step:
      the person does not see an empty screen saying "you have nothing".
  Adding — two or three question blocks; at the end the scenario writes the row
  with the values [AnswerID(block id)]: this way the answers are taken by blocks, not by
  order, and the path can be changed without rewriting anything.
  THE OWNERSHIP CHECK IS MANDATORY: the next step looks up the chosen item
  by [Answer] AND ClientID = [ClientID]. Without the second condition a subscriber
  who sent someone else's plate number as text gets booked on someone else's car.

DO NOT WRITE NOTIFICATION RECIPIENTS AS NUMBERS. Read the triple { botid, messenger,
clientid } from the administrators list and substitute it with variables. Numbers written
by hand live until the first change of employee, and they would have to be changed in every
branch of every scenario — and a forgotten branch silently stops notifying.

CANCELLATION BY THE ADMINISTRATOR — WITH A BUTTON FROM THE NOTIFICATION. Give the message about a new
booking a "Cancel" button: action = nextmessage, nextMessage = the id of the cancellation
block, and put the booking number into data and read it in the scenario as [Data1]. Keep the cancellation
blocks in a SEPARATE chain without its own launch (subscription: false,
dialog: false, no keywords) — it can be reached only by this button,
and the client will never see it.

REVERSE SYNCHRONISATION WITH GOOGLE CALENDAR: THE ADMIN EDITS THE CALENDAR, THE BOT FINDS OUT.
It is asked like this: "so that masters can book a client right in the calendar, not
only through the bot". The bot writes to the calendar with the "Google Calendar" action,
but the reverse path — an event created or deleted BY HAND — comes from Google
as a notification, and this is a separate scheme.

  0. HOW MANY CALENDARS. One per bot is simpler: the subscription, the token and the term lie
     in one row of the settings list. A calendar per service — if the masters have
     different schedules: then the same fields are kept in the services list, and the notification
     finds the needed row by the channel number.

  1. THE SUBSCRIPTION. The "Google Calendar" action, the operation "Event: notification
     settings" (eventwebhook) subscribes the bot to calendar changes. The answer has channelId, resourceId and the lifetime
     of the subscription (usually a week). Save all of this to a list next to the calendar:
     Name | google id | channelId | resourceId | nextSyncToken | until what
     date the subscription is alive.

  2. WHERE GOOGLE KNOCKS. The subscription address is the SCENARIO WEBHOOK. Such a
     scenario starts with the action "Data exchange" → "Waiting for data",
     and it is launched not by a block but by an incoming request. The request headers lie
     in #{_request_headers}, the body — in #{_request}; parse them with a JS action.
     Google sends: x-goog-resource-state, x-goog-channel-id,
     x-goog-resource-id.

  3. SKIP THE FIRST NOTIFICATION. Right after the subscription Google sends an empty one
     with the state "sync" — it is a handshake, not a change. The condition
     '#{state}' != 'sync' in the very first branch — quotes on both sides,
     otherwise the branch always fires and the handshake goes to parsing.

  4. WHAT EXACTLY CHANGED. The notification does NOT contain the event — it only
     says "something changed in the calendar". Find your calendar in the list by channelId
     and request the changes with the operation "Event: synchronisation"
     (eventwebhooksync) with nextSyncToken: only new edits come back,
     not the whole calendar.
     Write the new token from the answer back to the list at once — with the old one
     the next request returns the same thing once more.

  5. PARSING THE EVENT. An event has status: "cancelled" — the admin deleted
     the booking, so cancel it in the bot too (free the slot in the mini app
     with the action "Calendar: delete a client's booking", mark the row
     in the list). Otherwise it is a new or changed booking: if there is no row with this
     event id yet — create one and take the slot. THE EXISTENCE CHECK
     IS MANDATORY: Google repeats notifications, and without it one admin booking
     turns into three.

     items comes AS AN ARRAY: the admin may move several
     bookings at once. Parse them all, not the last element.

  6. THE SUBSCRIPTION MUST BE RENEWED. It lives a week, and there is no need to create a separate cron
     for it: at the end of the same scenario compare the term from the list
     with today and, if it is time, renew it with the operation "Event: renewing
     notifications" (eventwebhookrenew) and overwrite channelId, resourceId
     and the new term. The bot renews the subscription itself as long as it
     is used.

  7. WHOM TO WARN. A booking the admin made in the calendar is not seen by the client:
     they did not create it. But the office master must be told —
     find the employees by the office code and send them a message in the bot.
     Put a note "booked by an administrator" on the row, to distinguish it
     from a client's booking in reports and on cancellation.

  WHAT NOT TO DO: do not parse the notification body as an event (it is not there);
  do not request the whole calendar instead of nextSyncToken; do not create a booking
  without a duplicate check; do not forget that the scenario webhook is issued in the account
  and after moving the bot to another account the address changes — the Google subscription
  will have to be created anew.

A SUPPORT SERVICE: ALL CHANNELS INTO ONE GROUP, EACH REQUEST GETS ITS OWN TOPIC.
It is asked like this: "clients write from the website, Telegram, MAX and VK, and employees
answer in one place". It is built on a Telegram forum group: a topic is created for each
request, the employee answers right in it, and the answer goes to the client
where they wrote from. There is no employee account — an employee is a member of the group.

  The design. ONE constructor bot (Telegram is mandatory, MAX,
  VK, the website widget are connected to it), ONE chain and ONE working block: the start block with [Actions],
  a "before sending" scenario and a transition TO ITSELF, dontwaitanswer off.
  Everything comes to it: a client's message from any messenger, an employee's
  reply in a topic, a button press in a topic, the client's rating. The scenario decides in the first
  step what it was:
    • _tggroup = 1 and _tggroup_id = the group ID, there is _tggroup_message_thread_id
      and _tggroup_is_topic_message = 1 → an employee's answer in a topic;
    • [Answer] — a button value (for example "pr~t~105"), _tggroup_id = the group ID,
      and _tggroup = 0 → a button press in a topic. The engine parses a press in a group
      in the PRIVATE CHAT of the one who pressed: _tggroup is 0 there, but _tggroup_id and the topic number come.
      A command typed by hand in a private chat comes with an EMPTY _tggroup_id — this is how
      a "command" from an outsider differs from a real press;
    • otherwise (a private chat of any messenger) → a client's message. /start and "start"
      from the website widget (it sends it itself on connection, without a slash) —
      a greeting, not a request. At the chain LAUNCH [Answer] is empty if
      the text does not start with "/": the widget's "start" and the first question of a VK client
      who launched the chain with their message lie in #{_message}
      (the launch flag is #{_isstart} = 1). Take the text from there, otherwise the question
      is lost, and "start" is left without a greeting.
      #{_isstart} = 1 IS NOT YET A GREETING: the first message of a new
      subscriber comes with this flag in Telegram too, if the person
      wrote at once, without /start. A greeting — only when the text is empty or
      it is /start, "start", "Начать" (Start); otherwise it is a request — parse it.
  A second block, if control of the first answer is needed, — a delayed launch on
  the CLIENT subscriber (see below). A duty robot round on an employee is not allowed here:
  an employee standing not on the start block stops being processed in the group.

  The "Requests" list: Number | Topic | Status | Source | Name | Responsible | …
  plus the autofields ClientID, Messenger, BotID — the row is created by the client themselves, so
  the reply address is already in it. Do not create your own "Contact".

  FROM THE CLIENT TO THE GROUP:
  1. select your own request: ClientID = [ClientID], Messenger = [Messenger],
     BotID = [BotID], the last row. Open — write into its topic; none or
     solved — a new one (number = the largest in the list + 1, not a global counter);
  2. a new one: the operation "topiccreate" of the "Telegram groups and channels" action
     (channel — the group ID, topicname, topicvar — where to put the topic number),
     then writing the row with the topic number;
  3. a message into the topic: the operation "message" with threadid = the topic number. WITHOUT threadid
     the message falls into the general chat, and conversations get mixed;
  4. a client's file from Telegram — with the field messagefileid: "#{_fileId}" (the same bot).
     From MAX, VK, the website Telegram does not accept a File ID: first "Receive
     and forward media" WITHOUT a recipient by types in a row (image, video, file, audio),
     then a message into the topic with messagemedia: ["#{media_url}"] — Telegram downloads
     the file at once, and it stays in the group.
  The group operations of the "Telegram groups and channels" action (a message,
  topics, editing, pinning, a poll, links) for a subscriber from MAX, VK, the website
  are performed by the Telegram bot of the same chain. Personal operations (subscription
  check, ban, rights, join requests) — only for a subscriber from Telegram.

  FROM THE GROUP TO THE CLIENT:
  1. find the request by Topic = #{_tggroup_message_thread_id};
  2. text — "Send to bot" to ClientID, Messenger, BotID from this row:
     the client may be in MAX, VK or on the website — the answer will arrive;
  3. an employee's file — "Receive and forward media" by types in a row with the recipient
     from the same row: the action itself downloads the file from Telegram and sends it
     to the client in their messenger. The file caption — in #{_caption};
  4. do not send the client a reply that starts with "//" — it is for your own people.

  BUTTONS UNDER A MESSAGE IN A TOPIC ("Take", "Solved", quick answers) are built
  AS A SET: one button whose name = "#{names}", nextData = "#{values}",
  action = "nextdata", and the variables hold JSON arrays of strings of equal length.
  The engine expands the string "#{buttons}" with a JSON array of button objects in the btn field,
  but the scenario editor does not show such a field — so use a set.
  Values — Latin and short (pr~t~105): the button data has 64 bytes.

  CONTROL OF THE FIRST ANSWER. On a new request put a delayed launch on the CLIENT
  of the "Check" block (exittype: "startmes", startmestime: true,
  startmestimedata — minutes). The block checks the status and, if there is no answer,
  writes a reminder into the topic; the client's next message leads them by a transition
  back to the start block. Send "Received, we will answer" by "Send
  to bot" to the client themselves ([ClientID], [Messenger], [BotID]) in the previous step:
  the delayed launch eats the exit message.

  WHAT NOT TO DO:
  • a separate chain for the group next to the client chain: the private chat intercepts
    everything, and the group chain is never chosen;
  • a second topic for the same client: the filter — by the three autofields and the status;
  • a group with topics in MAX: there are no forum topics there.

  QUICK ANSWERS. The "Answer templates" list (Code | Name | Text | Show):
  set buttons under the client's message; on a press a ready text goes to the client,
  and into the topic — "answered with the template "…"".

==================================================================================
2. A MEMO ON GENERATING EACH PROPERTY
==================================================================================

groups: one object { id, name, ordernumber:1 }. name — a short project name.

chains: chains. A scenario bot usually has 1 chain. subscription:true (launch by
the Start button). For the channel/tag/schedule fields see the description below. BotGroupId = groups.id.

A SCREEN THAT REDRAWS ITSELF MUST REPLACE THE MESSAGE.
A multi-list, a checklist, option toggles, a screen with a changing state —
everything where a button press returns the subscriber to THE SAME block — requires
"replace": true in btnsettings for tg and for max.

Without this mark each press adds one more copy of the screen to the chat:
after five ticks the chat has five almost identical messages, and the subscriber
ticks in the old copies — they stay working. The mark removes
the bot's previous message, and the screen looks like one live screen, not a feed
of duplicates. In VK the same setting has the same name.

The sign of such a block is simple: it is a block WITH BUTTONS whose conditionlist
has a transition to itself, and whose message is built by a "before sending" scenario.
A technical block without buttons that returns to itself to repeat input
does not count: there is nothing to replace there.

REPLACEMENT DOES NOT WORK THROUGH A DISPATCHER. The engine removes the previous answer of THIS
block. If the screen's presses go to a common parsing block (a dispatcher as the first
block), and it moves back to the screen, the previous answer turns out to be
the parsing without a message — there is nothing to delete, copies pile up, although replace is set.
Lead such a screen to itself: its scenario in the first step recognises its own
buttons in the block's answer and parses them itself, and everything else — text, a file,
buttons of other screens and notifications — passes by a transition to the dispatcher,
touching neither the block's answer nor the waiting marker. List the dispatcher's command prefixes
in this step: this shows that the screen passes them on, and the dry run
does not count the notification buttons as lost. The group day screen
in the ready bot "Private kindergarten" is built this way.

WHEN replace IS NOT NEEDED. Paged list output (send in the read operation)
sends a message per row and removes the previous page itself —
by _listItemIds. There is no need to put replace there: it is designed for ONE
screen message and with a batch of messages does not work as expected.

A STEP THAT WAITS FOR AN ANSWER MUST BE A SCENARIO ONE (scenario: true).
For a non-scenario message the engine does not look at conditionlist at all: neither answer
intake, nor conditions, nor tags work — only the text goes to the bot.
Make non-scenario only what is simply sent and waits for nothing.

ONE STEP PER CHAIN. This is not a wish but how the engine works.
messages are the STEPS of a chain, not blocks. A step has the message field, and it holds
a STRING with a JSON array of ALL the bot's blocks. A chain has EXACTLY ONE such
object: scenario:true, scenariotype:"common", BotChainId = chains.id.

Why exactly one. When the subscriber presses a button, the engine looks for the
transition block ONLY among the blocks of the step the subscriber stands on.
It never looks into a neighbouring step. So a jump to a block from
another step finds nothing, and the bot silently falls silent — forever.

Laying the bot out "by steps, one block per step" (as an automailing looks) —
means handing over a bot in which NOT A SINGLE BUTTON WORKS. Outwardly such an
assembly looks whole: the blocks are there, the links do not dangle, the checks
pass. It will not work.

Several steps of a chain exist only for an automailing: these are messages
that go out in turn after an interval, and there are no transitions between them.
If there is at least one transition between blocks — it is ONE step.

Inside message: blocks with string ids, transitions, externalbefore. The start block —
with order:0. Check: each block has a non-empty conditionlist (≥1 element); all
nextMessage/nextmessage/wronganswer lead to existing blocks of THIS SAME step;
externalbefore.label matches the action scenario name.

The interval of a chain step. Sending "immediately" (dimension:"now", dimensionlabel:"сразу",
interval:"0", intervalnum is not taken into account) can be set ONLY for the first step —
the one with ordernumber:0. The other steps do not have this option: the constructor
does not show it, there are only day|hour|minute with a non-zero interval (the interval
in milliseconds from the previous step, intervalnum — the same number in the chosen
units). If "now" is set for the second step and further, the client does not see such an
interval in the editor and at the first edit of the step chooses it again.
An instant launch of the next step is done not by an interval but by ending the
scenario with finishnow — see 3.7.

DELIVERY AFTER PAYMENT. A block with acceptpayment: true is launched not by a transition
but by a notification from the payment provider. So:
  • it must have no incoming transitions — nobody refers to it;
  • it is where the purchase is delivered: a file, a link, access, a tag;
  • the payment link is formed by the action scenario of the previous block
    (qb.cfg.payment), and this block only accepts the result;
  • until there is a payment, the subscriber stands on the block with the link — this is normal;
  • the block with the payment link has an EMPTY transition (nextmessage: null): only
    the buttons of the scenario message lead further — "Pay" and "To menu".
The sign of a violation is visible on the board: TWO transitions leave the
"Payment link" block — its own and an extra one, to payment acceptance. There must be one, from the button.
Without such a block the bot takes the money and delivers nothing.

EVERY CHAIN MUST HAVE A LAUNCH. A chain starts working only in four
ways: subscription (pressing "Start"), dialog (any request), keywords
(keywords) and addtagstart (a tag that was set). A chain with all of this
empty NEVER launches: its blocks and its scenarios are dead weight that
the client sees in the constructor without understanding why it is silent.

CHAINS HAVE NO PERIODIC LAUNCH. The constructor has no "once an hour" and no
cron: a chain is always launched by a subscriber's action or a tag set on them.
So DO NOT make a "tick" chain that looks through a list every so often
and sends reminders — there is nothing to launch it with.

What to do instead:
  • a reminder about an EVENT (a trip, a meeting, a deadline) — with the "Notification" mark
    in the branch where the event is created: backnotify + backnotifynum +
    backnotifytimedata (in detail below, in the description of a scenario branch). The branch
    answers the subscriber as usual. The subscriber's actions do not cancel the notification,
    so it lives until the deadline. The event need not be
    a calendar booking — a list row is fine too;
  • a reminder about a BOOKING in the calendar app — with the three own
    reminders of the booking (cal_record notify): they live with the booking itself,
    and a separate block is not needed for them;
  • a reminder about a FORGOTTEN step — startmesnotify. It works only while
    the subscriber is silent: any action of theirs removes the reminder;
  • a delayed TRANSITION by time — startmestime. It moves the subscriber
    to a block at the set moment, but answering "now" in the same branch
    is no longer possible: exittype is taken;
