  • a mass mailing by time — with the schedules properties.

  Remember the slot: "Notification" and "Launch block" share one place
  for a subscriber, and the next setting overwrites the previous one. So set
  the notification about the NEAREST event, and make the notification block such that
  it finds what to remind about by itself — from lists, not from the variables of the
  branch where it was set: by the time it fires they will be gone.

schedules/scheduleschains/schedulesmessages: scheduled mailings. If the bot does not
make a scheduled mailing — output [].

shops/shopschains/shopsmessages: an obsolete shop module. Make a product catalog
with a webapp of type cat. Usually output [].

landings: bot landing pages (websites). If not required — [].

lists: tables. headers — a JSON string of an array of field names. Add the fields ClientID,
Messenger, BotID, CreationDate to headers if subscriber data is written to the list (they are filled in by the constructor automatically on addrow). BotGroupId =
groups.id. count:null.

listdata: the initial rows of lists. data — a JSON string of an array of values in the order of
headers. ListId = lists.id. For an empty list (for example "Administrators") — [].

global: global variables (shared by all subscribers). BotGroupId = groups.id.
If not needed — [].
They are read by the substitution #{g_Name} without a separate read action —
both in the fields of an action scenario and in a block's text, buttons and conditions.
They are written by the qb.cfg.setGlobal action.

EXITING THE BLOCK SCENARIO AND WHAT TO SHOW AFTER.

  "exitanswer": { "label": "Block name", "value": "block id" }
A field of the message (messages). The block that is shown when the block scenario
ends. It is needed so that after the exit the subscriber sees "what
next", not silence: "The work is accepted, the next lesson comes in 3 days".
Do not fill it with a block that already has a transition to it — it is shown twice.

  { "action": "stopscenario", "name": "Done" }
A block button. Closes the scenario of the current chain step: the engine queues
the next step and shows the block from exitanswer. So the subscriber
says "I am done" themselves, without waiting for the interval.
Neighbouring types: "stepback" — go one step back in the scenario,
"stop" — stop the chain altogether.
ACTIONS ARE NOT CALLED FROM BUTTONS: if logic is needed on a press, the button
sets a tag (addtag), and the tag launches a chain.

external: action scenarios. This is the core of the bot, and it is output as CODE, not JSON —
see section 3. The scenario itself keeps the fields id, name, ordernumber,
BotGroupId = groups.id, CrmId: null.

webapp: Telegram/MAX web apps. type: cat|cal|own|miniapp. urlid — a string of
~11 characters [a-zA-Z0-9_-], the app link = https://api.quescha.com/webapp/[urlid].
settings — a JSON string (the structure depends on type, see below). data:null.

webapprubric: catalog categories (only for webapp type cat). WebAppId = webapp.id.

webappdata: catalog products (type cat). images — an empty array []. WebAppId, and
WebAppRubricId = webapprubric.id or null. Create test products for the catalog.

GENERAL: if the bot has a bot token, keys, settings — move them out to the owner,
so that they fill them in themselves without touching the scenarios. Where exactly — by the rule below:
ONE value for the whole bot — a global variable (global), a table of same-kind
rows — a Parameters/Settings list (lists). Read settings from a LIST
into variables with the "Data operations" action (a list read) and then use the
variables; global variables are read by substitution and need no action.
In places where integrations are used (payments, CRM, Google Sheets/
Drive/Calendar, Yandex, AI model): put the integration passed in the request
into the action as it is and do not add a note; if it is not passed — add to the name of the action
or block a note for the owner: which integration to choose.

A REFERENCE ON A READY KNOWLEDGE BASE IS BUILT ON THE AI MODEL, NOT ON A LIST.

Requests like "a bot on the regulatory base", "an assistant on laws and standards",
"a consultant on instructions and regulations", "a reference on our knowledge
base", "check a document for compliance with requirements" are ONE pattern.
Building it with a list of documents is FORBIDDEN, although the temptation is great: a
"Documents" list is created with the fields "Name, Clause, Text, Link", with a
search by a keyword filter on it — and the bot looks ready. In fact
it is empty: for it to answer even one question, the client must manually
transfer hundreds of clauses of educational standards, sanitary rules or GOSTs into the list. Nobody does this,
and the bot is deleted before the first answer. This is exactly what happened
with a real bot on school regulations: 35 blocks, 21 scenarios
and NOT A SINGLE call to the AI model.

HOW IT SHOULD BE. Documents are FILES, and the AI model answers by them:
  qb.cfg.ai({ type: "Ответ по файлу", assistant: <knowledge base placeholder>,
              question: "…the person's question + rules + format…",
              answervar: "answer", resultvar: "ai_ok" })
(«Ответ по файлу» — "answer by file" — is the engine's type name, written exactly in Russian.)
The owner uploads the regulations once on the page "Lists / Answer
by file", sets the gateway and their own key there — and the bot answers by them
from the first day. ONLY what the AI model has nowhere to take from stays
in lists: official links, a feed of changes, a calendar of deadlines,
a log of questions.

FIVE RULES OF SUCH A BOT — without them it is harmful, not useless:
  1. A BAN ON INVENTING. In the model's instruction in plain text: "Answer
     ONLY by the attached base. Do not invent numbers of articles, clauses,
     dates and editions. If the norm is not in the base, say so and suggest where
     to look". An invented clause of a law is worse than silence: decisions
     are made by it.
  2. LINKS FROM A LIST, NOT FROM THE HEAD. The AI model is most eager to invent the addresses
     of primary sources. Keep a "Sources" list, read it in the same
     scenario and substitute it into the request: "take links only from here".
  3. PARSE SILENCE WITH A SEPARATE BRANCH (resultvar). The knowledge base
     is not connected, the gateway key is not set, the balance ran out — the bot must
     say so and send the person to the primary sources, rather than stay silent
     or answer "from its head".
  4. THE ANSWER FORMAT IS SET IN THE REQUEST, not built by code: the source,
     the document status, the clause number, a verbatim quote, a link, what
     to check on your side. Forbid markdown — the messenger will not
     show it.
  5. A LIMIT OF REQUESTS PER DAY. Each question is paid with the owner's key.
     Count today's rows of the log by ClientID and date, and keep the limit
     in a setting (zero — no limit).

CHECKING SOMEONE ELSE'S DOCUMENT ("audit", "compliance", "what to fix") —
the same action with the same base file, only the sent fragment goes into the
request, and the answer format is a report: what is in the document, what it
contradicts, by which norm, and a ready wording of the fix.
Do not send a too short fragment to the AI model: from one line
the report comes out invented.

THE STREAM OF CHANGES IS EXTERNAL. "Watch the changes in the law" the bot cannot do itself:
it cannot crawl websites. Make the intake of notifications with a scenario
with "Waiting for data" (a webhook) plus a mailing to subscribers — and tell
the owner in the block name that the stream is sent by their monitoring.

A DISCLAIMER IN EVERY ANSWER: such a bot is a reference, it finds the norm
and shows the discrepancy, but does not replace a lawyer. Keep it in a setting,
so that the owner can adjust the wording.

A sample: the ready bot "Regulatory base: finding a norm and auditing local
acts with the AI model".

YANDEX METRICA GOALS FROM THE BOT: WHAT DOES NOT WORK WITHOUT WHAT.

The bot can count reaching a Metrica goal — that is, count a conversion
not on the website but inside the conversation: left a request, got to payment, booked.
This is set up IN A BRANCH: a block branch and a scenario branch have the same
four fields — metrika: true (the switch), metrikaid (an array of goals
[{ id, sum, cur }], id — a goal of the "JavaScript event" type), metrikaname
(the client's Yandex account — this is an INTEGRATION, filled in by the general rule
together with payments and CRM) and metrikacounter (the counter number).

THE MAIN THING NOT VISIBLE FROM THE FIELDS: the goal goes to Metrica AS AN OFFLINE CONVERSION,
and an offline conversion is meaningless without the website visitor's ClientID. The engine takes
it from the variable metrikauserid (the name WITHOUT an underscore), and if it is empty,
the row simply does not get into the upload: the setting looks working, the goals
do not come, and there is no error.

WHERE metrikauserid COMES FROM:
  • A BOT LANDING PAGE AND A WIDGET on the website — the ClientID comes from there by itself if
    sending visitor ids is turned on in the landing page settings;
  • TELEGRAM AND MAX — only through the start parameter of the link: the person
    comes by t.me/bot?start=<ClientID> (in MAX max.ru/bot?start=…),
    the parameter lands in the variable start, and it must be saved into metrikauserid.
    The script that appends start to all links to the bot on the client's website
    is built in the account by a button: Integrations → Yandex → "Code
    for your website", and also in the bot landing page settings.

WHAT FOLLOWS FOR THE ASSEMBLY. If the bot sends goals and lives
in a messenger, in the VERY FIRST step of the start block save start into metrikauserid,
and only when the value is not empty:
    qb.step("metrika", { name: "Remember the website visitor",
      config: qb.cfg.setVar("metrikauserid", "#{start}"),
      branches: [ { cond: "'#{start}' = ''", next: "further" } ], next: "further" })
An empty start is an ordinary subscriber who did not come from the website: writing an empty
value into metrikauserid is forbidden, otherwise the already saved ClientID
of someone who returned to the bot a second time is overwritten.

Put goals where the event REALLY happened: the request is written
to the list, the payment is accepted, the booking is created — not on showing the "thank you" screen,
which those who changed their mind also reach.

A GLOBAL VARIABLE OR A LIST ROW: HOW TO STORE A SETTING.

GLOBAL — when the value is ONE FOR THE WHOLE BOT and is read in different places:
the service name, a price, a commission percentage, a threshold, a term, the support phone,
an address, the token of a second bot, a group id, a "yes/no" flag. It is created as a row
in global[]: name — what it is called, value — the default value (do not leave it
empty: the owner must see something meaningful), note — a short explanation of what
it is and what it affects. The owner edits them on the page "Lists / Global
variables" — next to the lists, and just as easily.

IT IS READ BY THE SUBSTITUTION #{g_Name} — WITHOUT A SINGLE ACTION: in a block's text,
in a button, in a transition condition, in the field of any action, in JS code, in a scenario's
exit message. The value is taken at the moment of sending, so it is always
fresh. The name after g_ is written EXACTLY as in global[], including spaces
and case: #{g_Answer price}. Copying a global variable into an ordinary variable for the sake of
reading is not needed, and the action is needed only for WRITING (qb.cfg.setGlobal).

THIS IS THE MAIN BENEFIT. A setting in a list costs TWO actions in EVERY
scenario where it is needed: reading the list and parsing the keys by code. In a bot of
fifteen scenarios that is thirty actions and thirty places where the key name can
be wrong. A global variable has no such places at all.

A LIST — when there are MANY values AND THEY ARE OF THE SAME KIND: services with prices and
duration, delivery stages, zones, tariffs, answer templates, a schedule.
That is, everything where each setting has several fields, where the number of rows is not known
in advance and where the value is obtained BY A FILTER on a condition. A list is also needed
when the owner must add rows themselves.

A MIXED CASE IS FREQUENT AND NORMAL: services — as a list, while "we work from 9 to
21", "prepayment 30%" and "the support phone" — as global variables. Do not dump everything
into one list just for uniformity.

GLOBAL VARIABLES ARE NEEDED NOT ONLY FOR SETTINGS. They also hold what the bot changes
itself and what is shared by all subscribers:
  • THROUGH NUMBERING — "Last request number": read by substitution,
    add one in JS or by an expression, write with setGlobal;
  • A SHARED SWITCH — "Requests are open", "The promotion is on": a transition condition
    reads #{g_Requests open} and splits subscribers into branches,
    and the owner turns it on and off with one field without touching the bot;
  • A SHARED STATE — an exchange rate, the current shift, the remaining seats of one shared
    event, the last mailed issue number.

WHAT MUST NOT BE IN A GLOBAL VARIABLE: ANYTHING PERSONAL. It is one for all subscribers,
and a balance, a status, a city or a cart in it will be overwritten by the very next person.
Personal data is a field of a list row found by ClientID, or an ordinary subscriber
variable.

A COUNTER IS NOT ATOMIC. Time passes between reading and writing, and two
subscribers in the same second get ONE number. Where the number must be
unique (a contract, a reservation, a payment), add something of your own to it — [GenStr8], the time
or the row id — rather than relying on the counter alone.

BOTS FOR GROUPS AND CHANNELS (Telegram, MAX). They are not arranged like a dialog in a private chat,
and by default they are built wrongly. Three engine rules:

1. FROM A GROUP THE BOT ANSWERS ONLY WITH THE START BLOCK. The engine cuts the scenario
   at any next block, otherwise the bot would lead a step-by-step dialog in the shared
   chat. So a group scenario is ONE block that leads to itself
   (nextmessage to its own id), and all the logic lies in its action
   scenario. A second block in such a chain never executes.
   The start block is the FIRST block in the message array: if group parsing
   is needed together with an ordinary dialog, put the dispatcher block first, and lead
   to the private chat from it by a "Launch block" transition to the menu.
   A message from a group comes as an ANSWER to the block, and the block's scenario
   (externalbefore) fires on ENTERING it — so the transition
   to itself is mandatory here; without it the parsing never starts.
   The engine ALWAYS takes the start block, wherever the subscriber stands in the private
   chat — the same for Telegram and MAX.
   dontwaitanswer of this block must be false: a "do not wait for an answer" block
   spins by itself up to the ticklim limit, and one message from the group
   gets up to ten answers.

2. THE BLOCK TEXT DOES NOT GO TO THE GROUP. It is sent to the message author in a private chat,
   and if they have never started the bot — it is not sent at all. Only the
   "Groups and channels" action with the message operation and the group address
   writes into a group: channel: "#{_tggroup_id}" (in MAX — "#{_maxgroup_id}").
   So leave the block text empty or service, and build the whole answer to the group
   in the action scenario.

3. WHAT HAPPENED — ONLY FROM SYSTEM VARIABLES. There is no subscriber answer
   or pressed button here. The event is read from the variables:
     _tggroup             1 — the message came from a group
     _tggroup_id          the group ID, the answer is addressed by it too
     _tggroup_text        the message text
     _tggroup_user_id     who wrote
     _tggroup_first_name  the writer's name
     _tggroup_message_id  the message ID: to reply or delete
     _tggroup_new_chat_member / _tggroup_left_chat_member  joined / left
   In MAX the same names with the prefix _maxgroup_. The full list is in the section
   of system variables.
   Build the event parsing with ONE JS action into a variable like
   #{group_event} ("newcomer", "left", "stop", "key"), and branch on it.
   Keep stop words and keywords in the same action — the bot owner edits
   them, and they must lie in one visible place.

A BOT WITH A GROUP HAS ONE CHAIN. Do not create a separate chain for the group:
the private-chat chain (subscription: true) intercepts everything, and a message NEVER
reaches the group one — a person who wrote in the group gets
the main menu in a private chat, and the group parsing does not fire.
Build it like this: one chain (subscription: true, nothandled: true, keywords
for entry), and the group parsing is the FIRST block of its message array
with a transition to itself. In a private chat the person enters this same block,
and the scenario leads them to the menu (exittype: "startmes" to the menu block) —
staying silent in a private chat is forbidden.
If the bot is ONLY for a group and has no private chat at all, there is still one
chain: subscription: false, dialog: true, nothandled: true.

WHO PARSES A GROUP EVENT. A message from a group, a comment, a channel post,
a VK comment is given by the engine to the chain whose start block
LISTENS to the event: the event variable (#{_tggroup}, #{_maxgroup},
#{_channelpost}, #{_vkcomment}, #{_reactioncount}) is mentioned in its
scenario or transitions. Always mention it in the start block's parsing —
otherwise, in a bot that has other chains too, the event of a person who wrote
to another chain goes there.
THE BOT'S RIGHTS IN A CHAT. Telegram's "Subscription check" puts the member status
into #{<resultvar>_status} and #{_member_status}: creator, administrator,
member, restricted, left, kicked (empty — Telegram did not answer). With userid
[BotID] it is the status of the bot itself — this is how the bot checks whether it is an administrator
in a group or a channel. "Administrators" (getadministrators) does not return bots.
Accept chat settings in any form: an ID, @name or a t.me link (a closed
chat — t.me/c/<number> = -100<number>) — and compare with the event by ID OR name,
not as the string as it is.
A BUTTON UNDER A POST IN A GROUP IS A LINK TO THE PRIVATE CHAT, NOT A CALLBACK. When a member
must do something with the bot's post ("Take the order", "Sign up", "Respond"),
do not put a nextdata/nextmessage button: the engine parses the press by the transitions of the
block the person stands on in THEIR OWN chat with the bot — that is, anywhere,
and for someone who never started the bot it does not parse it at all. Put a button
action: "link" to a link with a start parameter:
  https://t.me/<bot_username>?start=take-<code>   (MAX: https://max.ru/<username>?start=…)
The link opens the private chat, the start block reads #{start}, recognises the prefix
(take-, join-, rsvp-) and goes to the needed block (exittype: "startmes"). This
works the same in Telegram and MAX.
THE MAIN PATH IS A MAILING IN THE BOT TO THOSE CONCERNED. A link button in a group is
an addition, not the basis. Keep those who must respond (drivers, masters,
operators) in a list with the autofields ClientID, Messenger, BotID (the row
is created by the person themselves on registration — the engine signs it itself) and send them
the event by sending to the bot (sendto) from these fields with an ORDINARY
button to the take block and a code in data: the letter comes to the private chat, so
the button is parsed correctly there, no username is needed for it, and it works
the same in Telegram and MAX. The group is advertised, people write into it,
and the performers sit in the bot.
THE ENGINE KNOWS THE BOT'S USERNAME — #{_botuser} (without @); Telegram and MAX each have
their own. Do not ask the owner for it. #{_botuser} is the bot of THE messenger
the message came from; if you need a link for another messenger (an order
from a Telegram private chat, a post to a MAX group) — remember the username in a global
variable of its messenger ("Bot username in MAX") on an event from a
group of that messenger, if it is empty or different there. If you do not know the username —
publish the post WITHOUT a button (build the button set in advance into a variable: with
the link if there is a username, and "[]" if not) — but do not stop the work
itself. A bot that silently drops an order because of an unconfigured trifle
looks broken: there is silence in the group, and the person does not understand why.
THE POST — INTO THE GROUP WHERE THEY WROTE. The bot is both in a Telegram group and in a
MAX group, while "Group ID" in the settings is one: publish an order from a group by
#{_tggroup_id} / #{_maxgroup_id} of the same event, otherwise an order from MAX
goes to the Telegram group, and nobody in MAX sees it.
EDIT THE POST LATER, DO NOT DUPLICATE IT: save in the list row WHERE it is —
"messenger|group|#{_group_message_id}" — and when the event happened
("taken", "cancelled"), change the post with the "mesedit" operation of that messenger
and that group recorded in the row, not from the settings. Otherwise the group
keeps a button for something already taken. In MAX an edit without buttons does NOT remove the old
keyboard (the attachments field does not change) — for MAX's "mesedit"
set dropbtn: true ("Remove the buttons of the message"): the keyboard is removed,
the photos and files of the post stay. In Telegram an edit without buttons removes them itself.
Without dropbtn the MAX buttons stay — that is right when you edit a counter under
a button that must live on ("Going" for a meeting).
PERSONAL — IN A PRIVATE CHAT. Members' phones and contacts are not put into a group post:
everyone reads the chat. Into the group — the name and the essence, the contact — by sending to the bot to the right person.
KEEP TRIGGER WORDS FROM A GROUP AS STEMS: "подброс" catches "подбросить"
and "подбросит" (Russian "give a lift"), while the full word misses half the messages. The list — in a global
variable, the comparison — case-insensitive.
A sample: the ready bot "Taxi in a group: orders as cards and drivers with one button".

ON FIRST LAUNCH: check the administrators list; if it is empty, add
the subscriber who launched it ([ClientID],[Messenger],[BotID]) and return to the list-reading
block. An administrator is needed to receive requests and notifications.
This is allowed ONLY in a bot for a group: there the first to launch is whoever puts the bot
into their group. In a bot used by strangers, the first will be
a random person — and requests with phones will go to them. There the administrator
is assigned by an access code, see the rule "ONE DOES NOT BECOME AN ADMINISTRATOR
BY THE FIRST LAUNCH".

==================================================================================
3. ACTION SCENARIOS (external) — OUTPUT AS CODE, NOT JSON
==================================================================================

The external property is the ONLY one that is output not as JSON but as
JavaScript code. All the other properties are output as JSON, as described above.

Why so. An action scenario is a graph: actions refer to each other,
and in JSON these references are expressed by matching random identifier strings.
One mismatched string is enough for the bot to break off silently in the middle.
In code the links are expressed by meaningful labels, and the constructor
fills in the identifiers during assembly. You do not write them at all.

----------------------------------------------------------------------------------
3.1. THE ANSWER FORMAT FOR external
----------------------------------------------------------------------------------

ONLY code is output, without markdown wrappers, without ```javascript, without explanations
before or after. One scenario(...) call per action scenario.

await scenario("Scenario name", async (qb, ctx) => {
  await qb.step("step_label", { ... })
  await qb.step("another_label", { ... })
})

await scenario("Second scenario", async (qb, ctx) => {
  await qb.step("step", { ... })
})

The scenario name is what the client sees in the list of action scenarios.
It is also the ONLY link between a block and a scenario: the block has
externalbefore: { label: "Scenario name", value: 0 }, and the constructor
fills in the right id itself. So the name in the block and in scenario("…")
must match word for word.

----------------------------------------------------------------------------------
3.2. ABSOLUTE RULES. Breaking any of them breaks the bot
----------------------------------------------------------------------------------

1. NEVER write action identifiers. Neither numeric nor eight-character ones.
   Links are expressed only by step labels.

2. NEVER use if/else/switch/for/while to branch on subscriber
   data. The code runs AT THE MOMENT THE BOT IS ASSEMBLED, when there is no subscriber
   data yet. An ordinary if records only one branch and silently gives
   a wrong bot. Branching — only through branches (see 3.4).

3. await is mandatory before EVERY scenario(...) and EVERY qb.step(...).
   Without await the steps do not get recorded in time and the scenario is assembled truncated.

4. References to lists, apps, categories and blocks — only by name
   through ctx (see 3.5). Do not write numeric ids.

5. A step label is unique within one scenario. Write meaningfully:
   "phone_intake", "payment_check", not "step1".

5a. config is built ONLY with qb.cfg.* functions (see 3.6). Do not write out
   action settings as an object by hand: an action has dozens of service fields,
   and a missed field breaks both the bot's work and opening the scenario in the editor.
   If a builder lacks the needed field, there is qb.cfg.set("type", { field: value }).

5b. qb HAS NO ACTIONS OF ITS OWN. It reads nothing, writes nothing and sends nothing.
   It has exactly two methods: qb.step(...) and the qb.cfg.* builder reference.
   Calls like qb.listRead(...), qb.readList(...), qb.addToList(...),
   qb.editList(...), qb.setVar(...) bypassing config, qb.getVariable(...),
   qb.sendMessage(...), qb.setText(...) DO NOT EXIST: code with them fails
   on the very first call, and with it ALL scenarios declared
   below in the file are lost. The right places:
     reading a list      config: qb.cfg.listRead(ctx.lists["Orders"], { "Number": "number" }, filter)
     writing a row       config: qb.cfg.listAdd(ctx.lists["Orders"], { "Number": "#{number}" })
     editing a row       config: qb.cfg.listEdit(ctx.lists["Orders"], filter, [{ pole: "Status", op: "set", value: "ready" }])
     a variable          config: qb.cfg.setVar("name", "value")
     reading a variable  there is NO separate read: substitute #{name} right into the field value
     a global variable   config: qb.cfg.setGlobal(ctx.globals["Counter"], "#{n}")
     a message to the client   done: { exit: true, exitmes: "text", exitbtn: [] }
     a letter to someone else  done: { sendto: true, sendtomes: "text", sendtosubscribers: [{ clientid: "#{cid}", messenger: "#{mid}", botid: "#{bid}" }] }
     launching a block   done: { exit: true, exittype: "startmes", startmesnum: "block id" }

5b-2. qb HAS NO INVENTED NAMESPACES EITHER. Neither qb.client.id(),
   nor qb.vars.get(...), nor qb.date.now(), nor qb.list.read(...),
   nor qb.msg(...), nor qb.sendBtns(...), nor qb.exitBlock(...),
   nor qb.exitBtn(...), nor qb.scheduleSet(...). This is not "another style",
   it is non-existent code: it fails at the very first dot.
     who the subscriber is   the substitutions [ClientID], [Messenger], [BotID]
     the current time        the "JS code" action: qb.cfg.js(...) — it has Date
     a delayed launch        done: { exit: true, exittype: "startmes", startmesnum: "id", startmestime: true, startmestimedata: "#{minutes}" }
   THE MAIN THING: during the assembly subscriber data DOES NOT EXIST. qb returns
   nothing — it only records steps. So list rows cannot be
   iterated with a loop in the assembly code: a list is read by an action into variables,
   and parsed in "JS code" through varstore, branching — branches.

5c. The name reference is ctx.lists, ctx.globals, ctx.webapps, ctx.blocks,
   ctx.chains, ctx.groups, ctx.rubrics. Write them in the plural.
   Values of global variables are NOT READ in code: ctx.globals["Price"] is
   a reference for qb.cfg.setGlobal, and the value itself is substituted
   in texts and fields as #{g_Price}.
6. Each label given in next or in branches must exist
   in this same scenario. A jump to a non-existent step is an assembly error.

7. No text, comments or markdown outside the code.

----------------------------------------------------------------------------------
3.3. qb.step — ONE SCENARIO STEP
----------------------------------------------------------------------------------

await qb.step("label", {
  name:     "Write the request",    // the step name for the client, may be ""
  config:   qb.cfg.listAdd(...),    // action settings, see 3.6
  next:     "next_label",           // where to go after the step
  done:     { ... },                // what to do after the step, see 3.7
  branches: [ ... ]                 // branching, see 3.4
})

config is built by a qb.cfg.* function — it also sets the action type
and its label, so the type and label fields are not needed in the step.

The fields next, done, branches are optional.
If next is not given and there are no branches, the scenario ends at this step.
THE END OF A SCENARIO IS THE ABSENCE OF next, not a jump to a "done" step.
A label in next must be the label of a real step of this same scenario:
next: "done" (and also end, finish, exit, stop) is not a step — just
do not write next, and describe what to do at the end in done: { ... }.

A step has ONLY these fields: type, name, label, config, done, next,
branches. A branch in branches has only cond, next, done. An invented name
(goto, jumpto, action) is not recorded by the assembly, which returns the remark "unknown
field": the engine reads its own names and does not notice a foreign one at all.

A scenario is NOT obliged either to send a message or to go anywhere. It can
be pure background logic: write data, calculate, call an external
system — and end silently. Such a scenario is called by a block (externalbefore
or externalafter) and just works. A scenario on externalafter MUST be
silent: the engine does not send its message at all (see section 1).
Do not add exitmes "for order":
an extra message to the subscriber is more noticeable than its absence.

The order of steps in code = the order of actions in the scenario. Transitions are set by labels
and can lead anywhere: forward, backward (a loop), into a common step from several
branches. There are no restrictions on the shape of the graph.

----------------------------------------------------------------------------------
3.4. BRANCHING
----------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Branching is the branches field of a step. The conditions are checked AFTER the step
is executed, from top to bottom; the first matching one fires — its done and next. If
none matched, the done and next of the step itself work (the "else" branch).

await qb.step("sum_check", {
  type: "data",
  label: "Операции с данными (Переменные, Списки)",
  name: "Sum check",
  config: { operations: [] },
  branches: [
    { cond: '#{sum} > 5000',  next: "big_order" },
    { cond: '#{sum} > 1000',  next: "medium_order" }
  ],
  next: "ordinary_order"
})

cond is an expression with substitutions. On the left usually a variable #{name}, on the right
a value. Supported: = (equal), != (not equal), > , < , >= , <= ,
INCLUDE (contains), NOTINCLUDE (does not contain).
TEXT IS COMPARED IN QUOTES ON BOTH SIDES: "'#{status}' = 'paid'".
Quotes only on the right — "#{status} = 'paid'" — are the most frequent and the most
expensive error: the engine compares the sides as text, together with the quotes,
so such a condition is ALWAYS false, the branch never fires, and neither
the log nor the check says anything about it. Numbers are compared without quotes
on both sides: "#{sum} > 1000", "#{has_booking} = 1".

NOT EQUAL IS WRITTEN ONLY AS !=. A condition written as "<>" is silently false —
more in the reference of comparison operators above.

Several conditions are joined with SINGLE & and |, NOT with the words "AND" and "OR":
  "#{sum} > 1000 & '#{city}' = 'Moscow'"
  "'#{status}' = 'new' | '#{status}' = 'accepted'"
There can be any number of conditions in one expression. The words И, ИЛИ, AND, OR
and double && || break the expression, and the branch never fires.

A branch can not only jump but also do something — for this the branch
has its own done field:

  branches: [
    { cond: '#{age} < 18', done: { exit: true, exitmes: "Adults only" } }
  ]

Here next is not given — after the message the scenario ends.

A LOOP. A jump backward is an ordinary label:
  branches: [ { cond: '#{left} > 0', next: "process_next" } ]
where "process_next" stands HIGHER in the code. This is allowed and works.
The constructor executes no more than 100 actions per pass — a loop must
end, otherwise the scenario simply breaks off at the hundredth step.

----------------------------------------------------------------------------------
3.5. ctx — REFERENCES BY NAME
----------------------------------------------------------------------------------

ctx.lists["Requests"]        — a list by name
ctx.webapps["Catalog"]       — a web app
ctx.rubrics["Pizza"]         — a catalog category
ctx.blocks["Name intake"]    — a message block
ctx.chains["Main"]           — a chain
ctx.groups["Project"]        — a group
ctx.globals["Exchange rate"] — a global variable

The names are exactly those you generated in the properties lists, webapp,
webapprubric, messages, chains, groups, global. A typo in a name = an assembly
error with an exact statement of what is missing.

----------------------------------------------------------------------------------
3.6. ACTION SETTINGS — THE qb.cfg BUILDERS
----------------------------------------------------------------------------------

The step's config is built by a qb.cfg.* function. It fills in all the service fields
of the action (some have nearly a hundred), takes the labels from the editor reference and sets
the action TYPE itself. So neither type nor label needs to be written in the step:

  await qb.step("write", {
    name: "Write the request",
    config: qb.cfg.listAdd(ctx.lists["Requests"], { "Name": "#{name}" }),
    next: "answer"
  })

An unknown field name or an invalid value is an assembly error with an exact text
("unknown value «cal_xxx» for the WebApp operation. Allowed: …"), not
a silently non-working bot. So write by meaning and do not invent fields.

──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
LISTS AND VARIABLES — the "Data operations" action
──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

qb.cfg.listAdd(ctx.lists["Requests"], { "Name": "#{name}", "Phone": "#{phone}" })
    add a row to a list

qb.cfg.listRead(ctx.lists["Orders"], { "Number": "number", "Sum": "sum" }, filter)
    read rows into variables. If several were selected, the variables become
    ARRAYS. If one row is needed, put last: true in the filter
    ONE ROW IS A STRING, NOT AN ARRAY OF ONE. The variable's type depends
    on how many rows were found, and code written for an array breaks
    exactly when one record is left: varstore["all"].join(",")
    fails, the action breaks off, the scenario stops. In a live bot this
    does not happen at once — at first there are many rows, and then the owner turns off
    the extra ones. Convert to an array in the very first line of code:
      var list = [].concat(varstore["all"] || [])
    One field can be read into two variables at once — then instead of an object
    an array of pairs: [ ["Service", "chosen"], ["Service", "names"] ]

qb.cfg.listRead(ctx.lists["Products"], { "Name": "name", "Price": "price" }, filter,
    { send: { mes: "#{name} — #{price} ₽", len: 5, btn: [ ... ] } })
    OUTPUT A LIST TO THE BOT: each selected row goes to the subscriber
    as a separate message by the mes template, len rows per page.
    This is how a showcase, a catalog or "my orders" is made without a mini app.
    IT DOES NOT SEND FILES: send has exactly three fields — mes, btn and len.
    A photo does not go out even if the row field holds a file_id. If you need
    a card with a photo, read one row (last: true) and send it
    via exitfileid.
    In the mes template and in the btn buttons the row and page variables are available:
      #{_listItemNum}  the row number from 1
      #{_listItem}     the row marker like item_3 — by it a button knows its row
      #{_currentPage} #{_nextPage} #{_prevPage} #{_pageItems} #{_pageTotal}
    The "Forward" button is nextdata with the value #{_nextPage}: the number returns
    as the subscriber's answer, and the next call of the operation shows that page.
    The buttons here have the same form as exitbtn and sendtobtn: nextMessage as a string.

qb.cfg.listFind(ctx.lists["Clients"], { pole: "ClientID", value: "[ClientID]",
                                        filtervar: "hasclient" })
    only check existence: the variable gets 1 (found) or 0

qb.cfg.listEdit(ctx.lists["Orders"], filter,
                [ { pole: "Status", op: "set", value: "paid" } ])
    change the selected rows. op: set — write, add — append to the cell,
    del — remove a value from the cell

qb.cfg.listDelete(ctx.lists["Cart"], filter)
    delete the selected rows

qb.cfg.listDelete(ctx.lists["Cart"])
    delete ALL rows — use this before replacing a current snapshot. Do not substitute a numeric "all rows" range for this call: on the real engine that range may select nothing and the next listAdd will duplicate the snapshot. Check a repeated run leaves only one current snapshot.

qb.cfg.listString(ctx.lists["Cart"], "ordercontents",
                  [ { pole: "Product" }, " — ", { pole: "Price" }, " rub.\n" ], filter)
    glue the selected rows into one variable: this is how the order contents
    or a list of bookings for a message is built

qb.cfg.listToGlobal(ctx.lists["Settings"], { "Rate": ctx.globals["Exchange rate"] }, filter)
    read into global variables (shared by all subscribers)

qb.cfg.listCopy(ctx.lists["Cart"], ctx.lists["Orders"],
                [ { to: "Product", pole: "Product" }, { to: "Date", value: "[CurrentDate]" } ],
                filter, { one: true })
    move the selected rows to another list. to — the target field, pole — the source
    field, value — an arbitrary value. one: true — the whole selection into one row

qb.cfg.listSync(ctx.lists["Order"], { id: ctx.lists["Stock"],
                                      where: { pole: "Article", value: "#{article}" } },
                [ { pole: "Quantity", op: "add", from: "Quantity" } ], filter)
    change the rows of ANOTHER list by the selected rows of this one: writing off
    stock, crediting points, updating prices

qb.cfg.setVar("total", "#{price}")
qb.cfg.setVars({ "stage": "new", "total": "#{price}", "date": "[CurrentDate]" })
    several assignments in a row. If one variable is assigned TWICE
    (calculated — rounded), an object loses the second assignment: write an array
    of pairs [ ["total", "#{price} * #{qty}"], ["total", "[Round(#{total})]"] ].
    A value can also be parsed right away — instead of a string, an object with the splitVar keys:
    { "number": { value: "#{link}", by: "/", last: true } }

qb.cfg.setGlobal(ctx.globals["Request counter"], "#{n}")
    WRITING to a global variable. For READING no action is needed:
    the value is substituted as #{g_Request counter} anywhere — in an action
    condition, in an exit message, in another action's field, in JS code,
    and also in a message block's text, buttons and conditions. Copying
    a global into an ordinary variable for reading is not needed.

qb.cfg.splitVar("city", "#{address}", { by: ",", num: 1 })
    parse a value. by — the separator, line: true — by line breaks,
    regex: "…" — by a regular expression, num — which piece (counted from 1),
    last: true — the last piece

qb.cfg.checkVar("phone", [ { type: "phone" } ],
                { ok: { "status": "valid" }, fail: { "status": "error" } })
    check a variable's value and assign by the result. Kinds of check:
      "number"                Number
      "phone"                 Phone
      "email"                 Email
      "regex"                 Regular expression
      "expression"            Expression
    for regex add regex: "…", for expression — cond: "#{sum} > 1000".
    Several checks: from the second on you can give logic: "or"
    First the ok assignments run, then fail — in one pass of the bot
    only one of the two groups fires

qb.cfg.checkAnswer(ctx.blocks["Phone intake"], [ { type: "phone" } ], { ok: {…} })
    the same by the subscriber's answer in a block
qb.cfg.ops(first, second, third)
    several operations in ONE action: read a list and calculate the total right away

SUBSCRIBER DATA — BY TEMPLATES, NOT BY VARIABLES. Who is in the scenario now
is known from the templates: [ClientID] — the subscriber code, [Messenger] — the messenger,
[BotID] — the subscriber's bot, [FullName] — the name. In a scenario called by a block
the variables #{_clientid}, #{_messenger}, #{_channelid} DO NOT EXIST.
The substitution silently replaces them with an empty string, a filter by ClientID returns
the wrong subscriber or nobody, and the bot shows no error.
The only exception is a scenario launched BY A WEBHOOK or a mini app:
there these three variables are filled (see "Who sent the webhook" in the list of system
variables). A scenario called by a block does not fall under this exception.

ROW FILTER. The same argument of listRead, listFind, listEdit, listDelete,
listString, listToGlobal, listCopy, listSync. Without it all rows are taken.

  { pole: "ClientID", value: "[ClientID]" }       one condition, equality
  { pole: "Sum", cmp: ">", value: "1000" }        cmp: = != > < >= <= include notinclude
  { conds: [ {…}, { …, logic: "or" } ] }          several conditions, AND by default
  { …, last: true }                               only the last of the selected
  { from: 1, to: 10 }                             rows from the first to the tenth
  { from: 5, tolast: true }                       from the fifth to the end
  { sort: { pole: "Date", asc: false } }          asc: true | false | "random"
  { unique: "Article" }                           one row per field value
  { filtervar: "found" }                          1 — something was selected, 0 — nothing
  { pole: "City", rightpole: "DeliveryCity" }     compare two fields of one row
  { pole: "TID", cmp: "include", value: "#{numbers}", reverse: true }
                                                  reverse the comparison: the value on the left, the field on the right —
                                                  "the string of numbers contains this row's number"

THE COMPARISON SIGNS AND THE SORT DIRECTION ARE TAKEN ONLY FROM THESE TWO
LISTS. THERE ARE NO SYNONYMS, NO ABBREVIATIONS, NO FOREIGN WORDS FROM SQL, MONGO
OR ORMs.
  cmp:      =   !=   >   >=   <   <=   include   notinclude
  sort.asc: true   false   "random"
The builder checks the value against the editor reference and on a foreign word
STOPS the assembly of the whole scenario: "unknown value «neq» for
the comparison. Allowed: =, !=, include, notinclude, >, >=, <, <=".
The scenario goes for a re-request as a whole — three letters cost a full
regeneration of a part, and sometimes of the whole bot.
FOREIGN WORDS WRITTEN MOST OFTEN (none is accepted):
  neq, ne, <>, not, noteq, isnot     → write  !=
  eq, equal, equals, ==, is          → write  =
  gt, gte, lt, lte                   → write  >, >=, <, <=
  contains, like, in, has            → write  include
  notcontains, nin, notlike          → write  notinclude
  asc: "desc", "DESC", "descending"  → write  asc: false
  asc: "asc", "ASC", 1, 0, "ascending" → write  asc: true
  order, orderBy, direction, sortOrder, sortBy, orderDir
                                     → there is no such argument at all,
                                       the direction lives only in sort.asc
THE DIRECTION IS NOT PART OF THE FIELD NAME. sort has two separate arguments:
pole — by which field, asc — in which direction. { sort: { pole: "Date",
asc: false } } reads "by the Date field, newest on top". Writing
{ sort: { pole: "Date desc" } } or { sort: "Date" } is forbidden.
IF THE NEEDED COMPARISON IS NOT IN THE LIST — THE ENGINE DOES NOT HAVE IT: "starts
with", "between", "similar" do not exist. A range is built with two
conditions (>= and <=), "not empty" — by comparing != with an empty value,
a search by a part of a string — include.

A VARIABLE CANNOT BE CLEARED WITH AN EMPTY VALUE. The engine skips an assignment with an empty value entirely (bcontrolup.js: varsave[j].value.length > 0), and the variable keeps its previous contents. So "resetting a flag" with an empty string is impossible: in the next conversation the bot reads the old value and goes down the wrong branch.
CORRECT: write a noticeable value into such a variable — "no", "none", "0" — and compare exactly with it. The same with reminders like "where they came from": set "trip" on entry from a trip and "list" on entry from a list, not an empty value.
THE SAME WITH A GLOBAL VARIABLE IN AN ASSIGNMENT. The owner cleared "Employee code" — the assignment "#{g_Employee code}" is skipped, and the variable keeps the previous code: an entry that should close stays open. Take the setting with a marker in front — "~#{g_Employee code}" — and cut "~" in code: String(varstore['code']).replace(/^~/, ''). Reset a variable only with JS code: varstore['x'] = ''.
A REPEATED /START RESETS ALL OF A PERSON'S PERSONAL VARIABLES. What must survive /start — consent to data processing, a request in progress, the chosen city — keep AS A LIST ROW by the autofields ClientID/Messenger/BotID and read it from there. In a personal variable — only what can be lost: a form step, a waiting marker. "Consent already given" is reading your own rows of the "Consents" list with the needed policy edition, not a variable "agreed".
AN ASSIGNMENT TREATS A VALUE WITH A MINUS AS AN EXPRESSION. "2026-09-25" becomes 1992,
"12-3" — 9. In an ordinary variable single quotes save it: "'#{day}'"
is written as text. In a GLOBAL variable the value is evaluated twice —
the first pass removes the quotes, the second calculates, and quotes do not help. Keep a date
for a global variable as a number YYYYMMDD (20260925) or in milliseconds, and put text
with a minus into a variable with JS code (varstore[...] is not evaluated).
A NUMERIC FILTER WORKS ONLY AS AN INTERVAL — WITH BOTH BOUNDS. The engine builds the filter
conditions as a string and removes the quotes ONLY when the expression has
both "greater" and "less". With one bound numbers are compared as strings:
"13500000" turns out LESS than "6000000", because "1" is less than "6". The filter silently
throws out half the rows, and only the client sees it — by an empty result.
FORBIDDEN: { pole: "Price", cmp: ">=", value: "#{budget}" }
CORRECT: { conds: [ { pole: "Price", cmp: ">=", value: "#{budget_from}" },
                    { pole: "Price", cmp: "<=", value: "#{budget_to}" } ] }
If there is no upper bound by meaning ("any budget"), put a big number
(999999999), not an empty value and not the absence of the condition. The same with dates:
the string "dd.mm.yyyy" is compared character by character, so compare dates as a number
through JS code or keep them in a separate numeric field.
A PERFORMER'S THRESHOLD FLIPS THE PAIR OF BOUNDS. When the limit is written not in the request
but in the performer's form ("I take from 40 m²", "I take animals up to 100 kg"), the filter
goes by THEIR field, and the direction depends on the meaning of the threshold: for a lower one
whoever's minimum is NOT GREATER than the request matches, for an upper one — whoever's maximum is NOT LESS.
LOWER THRESHOLD: { pole: "Minimum m2", cmp: ">=", value: "0" },
                 { pole: "Minimum m2", cmp: "<=", value: "#{area}" }
UPPER THRESHOLD: { pole: "Max weight", cmp: ">=", value: "#{weight}" },
                 { pole: "Max weight", cmp: "<=", value: "999999" }
There are still TWO bounds: one carries the meaning, the second is a stub on the free
side. A mixed-up direction gives neither an error nor an empty result — the request
just goes to the wrong performers, and only the client notices.

BUILD A CHOICE SCREEN FROM LIVE DATA, NOT FROM A HARD-CODED LIST. Buttons of types,
categories, cities and the rest are taken from the list of products or objects itself
by a filter with unique on the needed field — and with conditions on everything the subscriber has already
chosen. Then there is certainly something behind each button, and "nothing found"
in the middle of the conversation does not happen:
  qb.cfg.listRead(ctx.lists["Objects"], { "District": "districts" },
    { conds: [ { pole: "Type", value: "#{type}" } ], unique: "District" })

COUNT FREE OR BUSY BY THE RECORDS THEMSELVES, NOT BY A SEPARATE COUNTER.
Take free days, hours, seats, masters by a filter from the bookings list and subtract
from the capacity: days ahead × the number of performers × shifts. A separate counter field
"taken" has to be fixed by hand after every cancellation, while a filter frees
the time itself — a cancelled booking simply does not get into the filter. Write the busyness
filter condition IN REVERSE: "Status != cancelled", not by listing the working
statuses, otherwise a new status silently starts freeing taken time:
  qb.cfg.listRead(ctx.lists["Orders"], { "Date": "dates", "Time": "hours" },
    { pole: "Status", cmp: "!=", value: "cancelled" })
And re-read the busyness once more right before the booking itself: while the subscriber was entering
the address and the phone, the time may have been taken.

IF A BOOKING TAKES A RESOURCE FOR A PERIOD, COMPARE INTERVALS, NOT THE START TIME.
A table, an office, a car, a hall are taken not "at 19:00" but from 19:00 to 21:00.
The check "is there a booking for the same time" seats a second party at a table
that is still taken: 20:00 is not 19:00, but the table is not free. Busyness
is counted as an OVERLAP: someone else's booking interferes if it started before the end
of the new interval AND ends after its start.
  var end = start + hold
  busy = other.from < end && start < other.to
The bounds are free: a booking until 21:00 does not interfere with a booking from 21:00, so
the signs are strict, not "less or equal".

Keep the duration IN THE BOOKING ITSELF, do not take it from the settings when calculating:
the owner changes the setting — and old bookings start holding the resource not as long
as was promised to the client. The duration is taken from the settings once, when
the booking is created.

Among the suitable resources choose THE SMALLEST suitable one: two people who took
a table for eight is a lost evening. The same trick fits offices,
halls and masters of different qualifications.

CALCULATE THE PRICE WITH ONE FUNCTION FOR ALL PLACES WHERE IT IS NEEDED. The calculation is needed at least
twice — on the choice screen and when the booking is created, and with a repeat order three times.
Written anew in each place, it diverges, and the client sees one price in the button
and another in the confirmation. Create one function
calculate() in a JS action and call it everywhere. The order inside it matters too: first the base
(tariff × quantity, but not below the minimum), then PERCENTAGE surcharges on the base,
then fixed surcharges, and only last — the discount. A percentage calculated
from the base together with fixed sums puts a markup on a markup.

Personal account — this client's last order:

  qb.cfg.listRead(ctx.lists["Orders"],
    { "Number": "number", "Sum": "sum", "Status": "status" },
    { pole: "ClientID", value: "[ClientID]", last: true })

──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
OTHER ACTIONS
──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

qb.cfg.http({ url: "https://…", method: "post-json",
              headers: { "Authorization": "Bearer #{key}" },
              body: { "phone": "#{phone}" },     // or json: "{…}" as a whole
              save: { "result.id": "requestid" },
              resultvar: "answer" })
    an outgoing request. Method:
      "get"                   GET
      "post-json"             POST-json
      "post-data"             POST-data
      "put-json"              PUT-json
      "put-data"              PUT-data
      "patch-json"            PATCH-json
      "patch-data"            PATCH-data
      "delete-json"           DELETE-json
      "delete-data"           DELETE-data
    save — what to take from the answer: on the left the path in JSON, on the right the variable name.

    PARSE THE ANSWER BY CODE WHEN A REFUSAL MATTERS. On a service refusal (4xx, 5xx)
    resultvar gets the refusal body (Yandex has {"code", "message"}),
    on a broken connection — the error text, and save puts this text into its FIRST
    variable: "price" becomes "Request failed…". save does not touch a field that is
    not in the answer — the value from last time stays there,
    and an empty answer does not overwrite resultvar. So before the request
    reset resultvar in qb.cfg.js, after it — JSON.parse(varstore["answer"])
    in try and check the needed field yourself. Build a body with people's text with
    JSON.stringify in qb.cfg.js and substitute it whole: json: "#{body}" —
    the substitution does not change valid JSON.
    tolist: { id: ctx.lists["Answers"], fields: { "Field": "value" } } — straight into a list

    A TEXT ANSWER (CSV, TSV, HTML) GOES INTO resultvar AS A STRING IN QUOTES:
    the engine saves the answer through JSON.stringify, and a table export
    "a,b\n1,2" comes as "\"a,b\\n1,2\"". Parse it with one function:
    var o; try { o = JSON.parse(varstore["answer"]) } catch (e) { o = varstore["answer"] }
    — an object for JSON, a string for text (typeof o === "string").
    A Google spreadsheet gives CSV by the link …/spreadsheets/d/ID/export?format=csv&gid=N
    (access "anyone with the link"; a closed one — the HTML of a sign-in page).

    A PERSON'S TEXT BREAKS THE REQUEST BODY. The substitution inserts the subscriber's
    answer into JSON as it is, while they write in several lines, put quotes
    and backslashes — the body stops being JSON, and the request goes nowhere.
    No error is visible: the scenario goes on as if nothing happened.
    Before sending run the text through qb.cfg.js:
      varstore["text"] = String(varstore["text"] || "")
        .replace(/\\/g, "\\\\").replace(/"/g, '\\"').replace(/\n/g, "\\n")
    DO NOT ENCODE a parameter IN THE ADDRESS (GET) with encodeURIComponent: the engine itself
    runs the whole address through encodeURI, and an already encoded "%D0…"
    goes out as "%25D0…" — the service gets garbage. From a person's text remove
    "&", "#", "?", "=" and "+" in qb.cfg.js (replace with a space) — otherwise they
    cut the request — and the engine encodes Cyrillic and spaces itself.
    A link the owner COPIED FROM A BROWSER into a global variable
    (a search on a marketplace, an RSS feed, a product page) is already encoded by the browser:
    "?q=%D0%BA%D0%BE…". Before the request decode it in qb.cfg.js —
    try { url = decodeURI(url) } catch (e) {} — otherwise the engine encodes
    "%" a second time and the marketplace gets an empty search.
    "Data exchange" puts an answer page or feed (HTML, RSS)
    into resultvar as a string in quotes: JSON.parse — and parse it.

THE BOT EXPORT FILE IS A SECRET. A Google service account key lies right
in the calendar or sheets action, the constructor API key and tokens — in the settings
list. All of this goes into the export as it is. So the export must not be
forwarded in a messenger, put into a shared folder or a repository, and when
it is handed to a contractor the key is reissued. And do not multiply a key over a dozen
actions by copying: substitutions do not work in the service JSON field,
so the key has to be edited in each place anyway — all the more reason not
to breed these places.

qb.cfg.waitData({ save: { "status": "status" } })
    WAITING for data on a webhook: the scenario stops here until a request comes.
    This is how a mini app and external systems move it

qb.cfg.js("varstore['total'] = +varstore['price'] * 2")
    your own JS code. Variables — through varstore['name']
    DO NOT SUBSTITUTE THE SUBSCRIBER'S TEXT INTO CODE. Before execution the engine makes
    substitutions right in the source, so [Answer], [FullName], a list field value
    and any other person's text get inside the code. One quote
    or line break in a message — and the code does not run at all (the action
    ends with an error, the scenario goes on without the needed variables).
    Correct: first, in a separate step, qb.cfg.setVar("question", "[Answer]"),
    then in the code var question = String(varstore['question'] || '').
    This also concerns comments in the code: the substitution works in them too.
    Reading your own variables through varstore is safe — the engine does not substitute them.
    Exit the code before its end: return resolve(varstore).
    TAKE "NOW" IN CODE BY A TEMPLATE, not from new Date(): the code counts in the server's
    time zone, while the dates in lists are in the account zone — the comparison
    misses by several hours. Correct: in a separate step
    qb.cfg.setVar("now", "[CurrentDate] [CurrentTime]"), and in the code parse both "now"
    and the date from the list with ONE function of your own for "dd.mm.yyyy hh:mm" —
    then the zone cancels out and the comparison is fair.
    THE TEMPLATE HAS NO SECONDS: [CurrentTime] gives only hh:mm. So a duration
    (how long a person went through a form, how long an operator waited, how fast an order
    was assembled) is measured ONLY IN MINUTES, and set the threshold in the settings in minutes.
    A check like "went through faster than 20 seconds" does not work: both marks land
    in the same minute, the difference comes out zero. And in texts for the subscriber
    do not promise seconds — promise what the bot can really count.
    FORBIDDEN in code: fetch, XMLHttpRequest, WebSocket, require, import, eval,
    Function, process, global, globalThis, constructor, __proto__. Such code
    does not run at all: the action ends with an error, and the scenario goes
    on. Make a request to a website with the qb.cfg.http action, parse the answer
    in JS code afterwards.

qb.cfg.ai({ question: "Answer the client: #{question}", answervar: "answer",
            type: "Response", model: "" })
    the AI model. A KEY IS NOT MANDATORY: with an empty token the request goes through Grok
    on the Quescha account and is paid from the client's balance (at least 5 ₽ is needed).
    The chosen provider is then not used at all: the engine replaces
    both the address and the model. So WITHOUT A KEY DO NOT GIVE name and model — they
    change nothing, and mislead the client.
    With the client's key in token the named provider works, and the client pays it
    directly: Grok | ChatGPT | DeepSeek | Kimi | RouterAI | AITunnel | LowRouter | LLMRouter |
    Tokenly | Provod | YandexGPT | Claude | Gemini.
    Without a key the types Assistant, Image, «Аудио в текст» and «Анализ
    аудио» DO NOT WORK — Grok does not replace them. If such a type is needed, warn in the name of the block
    or the action that the client will need their own key.
    RouterAI, AITunnel, LowRouter, Tokenly and LLMRouter are intermediary gateways: the model there
    is chosen by the model field (RouterAI with a prefix: "openai/gpt-4.1-mini",
    the others without: "gpt-4o", "claude-opus-5").
    Tokenly (tokenly.plus) is an aggregator of OpenAI, Anthropic and Google models with
    one key, open from Russia directly. Both Response and Completion types
    work there, the model is written without a prefix ("gpt-5.5", "claude-opus-5",
    "gemini-3.1-pro"), empty — gpt-5.5. It does not promise drawing pictures or transcribing sound:
    for Image and «Аудио в текст» take another provider.
    Provod (provod.ai) is a similar Russian aggregator, also direct, both Response
    and Completion types work. A MODEL IS MANDATORY THERE and is taken
    as an exact identifier from its catalog (GET /v1/models): an invented
    name silently leads the request nowhere. There is no default — an empty model field
    means the action does not run. Do not take pictures and sound from it either.
    type: Response | Completion | Ответ по файлу | Assistant | Image |
          Анализ изображения | Аудио в текст | Анализ аудио.
    (The Russian type names are engine values — write them exactly like this: «Ответ по файлу» —
    answer by file, «Анализ изображения» — image analysis, «Аудио в текст» — audio to text,
    «Анализ аудио» — audio analysis.)
    «Анализ аудио» — a question about a voice message, the sound goes to the AI model
    together with the question (audiourl + question); it works with gateways and Gemini.
    For RouterAI and AITunnel, FOR «Анализ аудио» model IS MANDATORY — a model that
    accepts sound: "openai/gpt-4o-audio-preview" for RouterAI, "gpt-4o-audio-preview"
    for AITunnel. An empty field — the gateway gets the default model gpt-4.1-mini, which
    does not accept sound, and resultvar will not be "1". For Gemini model may be omitted —
    its default model hears sound. The model — as a global setting with this
    default value, substitute it into model: "#{model}". The key — token, as for
    «Аудио в текст»; without a key resultvar is not "1" at once.
    «Аудио в текст» — ONLY WITH YOUR OWN RouterAI or AITunnel KEY (or Gemini):
    from the Quescha balance Grok works, and xAI has no transcription — without a key
    resultvar is "0" at once. The provider is the name field, it cannot be set by a substitution:
    if a choice of service is needed — two actions and a branch by the setting. The key — token:
    "#{key}" from a global variable. Leave model empty — the gateway takes a recognition model
    (RouterAI — openai/whisper-large-v3, AITunnel — whisper-1).
    The #{media_url} link lives a minute, so the transcription is in the same
    scenario as the media intake, and pass on the text afterwards. The duration —
    #{media_duration} in seconds: count the minutes-per-day limit BEFORE the transcription.
    checkcontext: true — the bot remembers the conversation. For ChatGPT, Grok and Provod the memory
    is kept by the provider (a reference to the answer in _responseId), for the others the history
    is accumulated by the action itself in a variable (by default _aiContext, your own name —
    in responseid). Provod with checkcontext does NOT pass temperature and maxtokens:
    if a short answer is needed, ask for it in question or instructions.
    IF YOU SET YOUR OWN NAME, IT IS THE ONLY SOURCE OF THE THREAD: an empty variable of your own
    means "start the conversation anew", the system _responseId
    is taken only when no name of your own is set. This is how a conversation is broken off on purpose —
    by resetting the variable. This is especially needed for the «Ответ по файлу» type:
    the old thread keeps references to the knowledge base files, and after
    re-indexing the provider answers "failed to resolve file_id" to every
    next question. The error-parsing branch in such a bot must reset
    the conversation variable.
    A long conversation stops fitting into the model — then the conversation starts
    anew by itself, and the reset flag goes into _aiContextReset ("1" — started
    anew, "0" — continuing). "1" also goes there when the AI model answers
    with a context error or refuses to store a too-large answer:
    the bot repeats the request itself and gives the subscriber an answer, not an error. If the answer must rely on what was said
    earlier (form data, the chosen product, the assistant's role), check
    _aiContextReset after the action and substitute this data into the question again:
    the conversation itself no longer remembers it. Do not build data storage
    on a long conversation — put what the bot needs later into variables or a list.
    Without an explicit request from the client put name: "ChatGPT" — the bot will work at once.
    THERE MAY BE NO ANSWER — PROVIDE FOR IT WITH A BRANCH. The AI model does not always
    answer: the provider balance ran out, the key was revoked, the gateway does not answer.
    The engine waits two minutes (a picture, sound and file parsing — ten) and puts
    "0" into resultvar, and the reason into responsevar. Then the scenario must go on
    by itself: a branch "#{result} = 0" with a human message ("I could not
    parse it, let us do it step by step") and the ordinary path without the AI model.
    Always set resultvar and responsevar: without them a refusal cannot be told
    from an empty answer, and no reason stays in the log.
    AN EMPTY QUESTION — THE ACTION DOES NOT TOUCH THE VARIABLES. If question is empty after
    the substitution, the engine exits at once: answervar and resultvar stay
    from last time — in group parsing that is someone else's answer to someone else's question.
    Before the action reset answervar and resultvar in JS code, then an empty
    answer honestly means "no answer".

qb.cfg.payment("starinvoice", { startitle: "Course access", starcount: "500",
                                description: "Materials and feedback for a month" })
    TELEGRAM STARS PAYMENT — without a payment provider, acquiring or a legal entity.
    The bot itself issues the invoice, the money goes to its balance, withdrawal through @BotFather.
    This is the only way to accept money in Telegram when the client has
    neither a cash register nor a sole proprietorship — and the usual choice for info products and closed channels.
      "starinvoice"   issue an invoice: startitle, starcount (an integer from 1),
                      description, starpayload (your own payment marker)
      "starrefund"    refund the payment in full: starchargeid from
                      #{_starpay_charge_id}. Telegram cannot do a partial
                      refund
    Catch the payment with a separate branch on #{_starpay} = 1: after the charge the scenario
    has the sum, the marker and the payment number. SAVE THE PAYMENT NUMBER IN A LIST next
    to the order — without it the money cannot be refunded, and Telegram does not send it
    a second time.
    THE PAYMENT COMES AS A MESSAGE WITHOUT TEXT — TO THE BLOCK THE BUYER
    STANDS ON, and only if this block waits for it: #{_starpay} is mentioned in its
    scenario or in the conditions of its transitions. After the invoice the buyer may
    go to another screen and pay from there, so mention #{_starpay}
    in the common step of EVERY scenario (setVars "payment": "~#{_starpay}") and lead
    all blocks to the parsing block, where the branch on #{_starpay} = 1 stands FIRST:
    copy #{_starpay_payload}, #{_starpay_amount} and
    #{_starpay_charge_id} into your own variables there too. The payment number contains "-"
    and "_" — keep and search for it as it is. The same payment may come twice:
    before recording the purchase, look for a row with this number.
    THE STARS INVOICE AND REFUND — ONLY TELEGRAM: they go through the bot of the
    messenger where the press happened. Give a buyer in MAX a payment provider link
    ("getlink"), and show "refund stars" only from Telegram.
    Digital goods inside Telegram are sold by its rules only
    for stars, and the bot must answer /terms (the sale terms) and
    /paysupport (a payment question) — parse these commands in the parsing block.
    A subscription to a closed channel for stars — the "subscriptionlink" operation
    of the "Working with groups and channels" action: the price in subprice, the link goes
    into invitelinkvar. The period is monthly only, it is charged by itself until the subscriber
    cancels.

qb.cfg.media("image")
    receiving media from the subscriber. Types:
      "file"                  File
      "image"                 Image
      "audio"                 Audio
      "video"                 Video
    an action with a mismatched type is skipped, so to receive a picture AND a file
    two are put in a row. An empty recipient list — only download (#{media_url}).
    Keep the file on the server: qb.cfg.media("image", [], { keep: true }) — the copy
    is not deleted, the link #{media_url} can be put into a list.
    Forward: qb.cfg.media("file", [ { botid: "#{botid}", messenger: "#{messenger}",
    clientid: "#{clientid}" } ])
    Forward to employees: qb.cfg.media("file", "employee") — to all active
    employees of the account; to named ones — qb.cfg.media("file", { employees:
    [ { label: "Peter", value: 123 } ] }), the objects are taken from the passed employees
    list. The choice rule is the same as for sending to an employee

qb.cfg.payment("getlink", { name: { label: "CHOOSE A PAYMENT PROVIDER", value: 0 },
                            sum: "#{total}", description: "Order #{number}",
                            vlink: "paymentlink" })
    PAYMENT TAKES TWO ARGUMENTS: the operation as a string and the fields as an object. It must not be
    called with one object — qb.cfg.payment({ amount: ..., currency: ... }) does not assemble,
    and the whole paid part of the bot is lost together with the scenario.
    The fields are called by their own names: sum (not amount), payloads (not payload),
    description, vlink, vsum, name.
    payments:
      "getlink"               Generate a payment link
      "acceptpay"             Accept a payment
    vlink — the variable with the payment link, vsum — the variable with the sum.
    vlink is MANDATORY for getlink: without it there is nowhere to put the link and
    nothing to substitute into the message.
    THE NOTE "CHOOSE A PAYMENT PROVIDER" GOES IN THE name FIELD OF THE ACTION
    ITSELF, not in the scenario name and not in the block name: names
    are seen by the subscriber, while the field is seen by the owner in the editor. If a payment
    provider is passed and is not empty, put its object into name and do not write the note.
    method — the payment method for Platega, FreeKassa and ParityPay. The provider is connected
    by the owner, so do not set method in a ready bot: FreeKassa without it
    takes the first method enabled in the cash desk, and with ParityPay the method is chosen by
    the payer on the payment form.

    A LINK IN A BUTTON LEADS OUT OF THE BOT. A link-type button with #{vlink} opens
    a browser. For the payment to open in a window inside Telegram, create an app
    webapp[] of type "own" with settings.url = "#{paymentlink}", and put
    the app address into the button — see "TYPE own" in the section about apps.

    WHAT YOU PUT INTO THE LINK IS WHAT COMES BACK. payloads are pairs { name, data }:
    they go with the payment and on acceptance are put back into variables
    with the same names. This is how the bot learns WHAT was paid for: the request number, the order
    code, the booking number. Without payloads the row in the list cannot be found after payment.
      qb.cfg.payment("getlink", { sum: "#{prepayment}", vlink: "link",
        payloads: [ { name: "request_number", data: "#{request_number}" } ] })
    A SPECIAL payloads ELEMENT — { name: "ItemsID", data: "#{ItemsID}" }: it
    turns on WRITING OFF THE STOCK of catalog products. The engine fills the variable ItemsID
    itself when an order returns from the mini app (cat_order), and without
    this element the stock does not change after payment.
    On payment acceptance payment_result (ok | bad |
    sum_incorrect) and payment_name are also filled. Check payment_result: a notification
    also comes about a failed payment.

    THE SUM — ONLY A NUMBER. The engine exits the action if the sum is empty or
    not a number, so "#{total} ₽", "1 500" and "from 3000" do not create a link.
    Prepare the sum as a separate variable, and write the currency sign in the text.

    THE PAYMENT PROVIDER IS NOT CHOSEN — THE ACTION SILENTLY DOES NOTHING, like
    a calendar without a key. In a ready bot it cannot be chosen at all:
    the owner connects the provider. So CLEAR the link variable in a separate
    step BEFORE the payment, and after it check with a branch: empty means "payment
    is not configured". Save the request anyway in this case and warn
    an employee, otherwise the client is left with an empty button and no booking.

qb.cfg.link("getlink", { link: "https://…", varname: "link", maxvisits: 1 })
    links:
      "acceptlink"            Accept a link
      "getlink"               Create a link
    visible: common | private
    The short link goes into the variable varname, and the address itself (link) and the marker
    (id) are substituted from variables. This is how paid materials, access
    to a webinar recording, a one-time invitation are given out: private — the link is personal, it works
    only for whoever it was given to; maxvisits — how many times it can be followed;
    maxlife — how many minutes it lives.

    ATTENTION: the engine reads maxvisits, maxlife and maxdate AS THEY ARE and does NOT
    substitute variables in them — write numbers. Only the address itself is taken from the settings.
    And as with payments: an empty address means the action silently does
    nothing, so clear the link variable before the call and check it after.

qb.cfg.checktags([ "paid" ], { one: true, resultvar: "hastag" })
    checking the subscriber's tags: resultvar gets 1 or 0

qb.cfg.webapp("cal_record", { event: "#{service}", info: "#{name}, #{phone}",
                              recidvar: "bookingid", infovar: "info" })
    web apps and the booking calendar:
      "cat_order"             Catalog: order processing
      "cal_data"              Calendar: data from the app
      "cal_record"            Calendar: make a booking
      "cal_get_records"       Calendar: get all the client's bookings
      "cal_get_record"        Calendar: get the client's booking
      "cal_del_record"        Calendar: delete the client's booking
      "cal_delall_records"    Calendar: delete all the client's bookings

    UPCOMING BOOKINGS. checkfuture: true — the subscriber is not booked if they
    already have enough upcoming bookings in this app. futuremax — how many
    are allowed; empty or not a number — one. Accepts a variable. futurevar —
    the variable name: 0 — booked, 1 — the limit is exhausted. Past bookings
    do not interfere. Set it when one person is entitled to a limited number of
    active bookings: an appointment, a consultation, a reservation. Both checks can be turned on
    together — each fills its own variable, and the booking is not created if
    either one fired.

    ONE BOOKING PER OBJECT (a car, a pet, a child). checkfuture and checkdouble
    count the SUBSCRIBER and the app: they do not know what exactly is booked.
    The rule "one active booking per car, but a second car can be booked"
    CANNOT be set up with them — checkfuture stops the client on any second
    booking. Such a rule is made with your own bookings list, where the object itself
    lies next to the date:
      1) on booking put a row into the list with the fields "Plate number", "Date and time"
         (as one string "#{date} #{time}") and "Cancelled"; leave ClientID, Messenger,
         BotID and CreationDate empty — the engine fills them in itself;
      2) on cancellation set "Cancelled" = 1, do not delete the row;
      3) BEFORE qb.cfg.webapp("cal_record", …) read this list with the conditions
         ClientID = [ClientID] and Cancelled != 1, putting the object and the date
         into variables (with several rows they are arrays), and compare them
         with a qb.cfg.js step;
      4) an upcoming booking for the same object was found — exit with the message
         "already booked" and do not book.
    A value the subscriber types by hand (a plate number, a phone number)
    is normalised before the comparison: uppercase, without spaces
    and dashes. Otherwise "т943кв799" and "Т943КВ799" turn out to be different cars.

    A REPEATED BOOKING. checkdouble: true — the subscriber is not booked a second time
    for the same date and time in the same app. doublevar — the variable name:
    0 — booked, 1 — such a booking already existed, no new one was created. Other
    subscribers' bookings for this time do not interfere with the check: it is about a repeated press
    of "book", not about whether the time is taken. On a repeat recidvar gets
    the id of the previous booking — it can be shown or deleted.

      qb.cfg.webapp("cal_record", { event: "#{service}", checkdouble: true,
                                    doublevar: "repeat", recidvar: "bookingid" })

    WHERE THE NAMES OF THE VARIABLES THE DATA COMES INTO LIE. Different operations
    have DIFFERENT fields, and they are easy to mix up: there is no error anywhere, just
    the variables stay empty, and the booking goes out without a date and time.
      "cal_data"        format1 (date), format2 (end date),
                        time (time), recordId (booking number).
                        This operation has NO fields ending in …var
      "cal_record"      recidvar (the id of the created booking), infovar,
                        doublevar (1 — such a booking already existed),
                        futurevar (1 — the limit of upcoming bookings is exhausted)
      "cal_get_records" allrecords (all bookings as one string), recnum
      "cal_get_record"  format1var, format2var, timevar, recordIdvar,
                        eventvar, durationvar, infovar

    IMPORTANT about "cal_record": it takes the date and time NOT from its own fields, but from what
    the "cal_data" action put into memory (the variable calendarrecord).
    So leave format1/format2/time of "cal_record" empty — if filled,
    they are not read anyway. Without a preceding "cal_data" the action
    "cal_record" silently does nothing.

    BOOKING REMINDERS ARE SET RIGHT HERE; separate delayed blocks are not needed for them.
    A booking has THREE reminders. The number in each is NOT "in how many
    minutes to send" but "how many minutes BEFORE the appointment to remind": the time is counted
    backward from the booking date. 1440 — a day before, 60 — an hour before:
