    So the action creating the link must run BEFORE the message with the button
    is sent and in the same chain.
  • AN EMPTY VARIABLE = A BROKEN WINDOW. The link was not created — the subscriber sees
    an error instead of the payment. Check the result of getlink with a condition and show
    the button only with a non-empty link.

  TELEGRAM ONLY. In MAX the app button goes out without the subscriber marker,
  there is nothing to substitute the variable into the app from. For MAX give an ordinary
  link button with the payment link itself.

TYPE miniapp (Telegram/MAX mini app):
- Put your own HTML/JavaScript code into settings.miniappcode.
- IMPORTANT: for the bot scenario to continue, the app must send data
  back to the URL https://api.quescha.com/miniapp/callback by POST or GET.
  The quescha object MUST be sent together with the data (it is created outside
  the app, it must not be overridden — send it as it is).
- After the data is sent, the bot gets the answer webapp:miniapp. If you do not use
  buttons under messages — process this answer on every block with the condition
  '[Answer]' = 'webapp:miniapp'.
- All the data passed by the app becomes scenario variables by their keys —
  process them in an action scenario.
- ONE APP FOR TELEGRAM AND MAX. MAX has its own library. Connect both
  and work with the one that has launch data:
    <script src="https://telegram.org/js/telegram-web-app.js"></script>
    <script src="https://st.max.ru/js/max-web-app.js"></script>
    var tg = (window.Telegram && window.Telegram.WebApp && window.Telegram.WebApp.initData) ? window.Telegram.WebApp : null;
    var app = tg || window.WebApp || null;   // ready(), close() — both have them
  The quescha object identifies the client in both messengers itself. Remember that
  in MAX a bot has one app (see above): such an app must be it.
- WHAT IS HIDDEN MUST STAY HIDDEN. The hidden attribute hides an element only
  by the browser's default rule, and any display in your own CSS overrides it:
  a cart or card window with a class "display: flex" is visible right on
  opening, covers the whole app, and the close cross (hidden = true) changes
  nothing. If you hide elements with the hidden attribute, put as the first
  style rule: [hidden] { display: none !important; }
- An alternative: the app can exchange data through a webhook — then
  continue the scenario through the action "Data exchange"->"Waiting for data",
  build the answer (for example, through JS code) into the variable _response.

WHERE A MINI APP KEEPS ITS DATA. It has no storage of its own. localStorage,
sessionStorage and any external databases MUST NOT be used: the app code lives
in the messenger, and everything it saves on its side disappears at the next opening
and is visible neither to the bot nor to the client's other devices.
The app's data is kept in the constructor's LISTS. The app does not read
or write them directly — it calls an action scenario, and the operations with
the list are done by the scenario.
So a bot with an app of type miniapp that shows or
saves something MUST have a list for this data and an action scenario that
works with it.

The list for the app must carry the columns ClientID, Messenger, BotID —
a record is bound to the client by them. Read records only with the filter
{ pole: "ClientID", value: "[ClientID]" }, otherwise the client sees someone else's data.

HOW A SCENARIO READS THE APP'S REQUEST. The request body comes as a JSON string
in the variable _request. Parse it with the "JS code" action and lay it out
into variables, for example the operation (show/add/change/delete)
and the row fields. Then — ordinary list operations, and build the answer to the app
in _response.

The minimal set of actions of a data scenario:
  1) JS code: parse _request into variables;
  2) a list operation: a read with a filter by ClientID — or adding,
     changing, deleting by the same logic;
  3) JS code: build the answer and put it into _response.

The description of the settings object for a web app (webapp elements).
App type: cat (Shop catalog)

{
  settings: {
    token: string, // The bot token for launching from the menu button (maxlength: 256, optional), mandatory for the MAX messenger
    theme: {
      value: string, // The theme value
      label: string // The theme name
    }, // A choice from the list (by default { value: 'cafeux', label: 'Дизайн 1' }):
    // { value: 'cafeux', label: 'Дизайн 1' }   (Design 1)
    // { value: 'shopux', label: 'Дизайн 2' }   (Design 2)
    // { value: 'cafe', label: 'Кафе' }       — an obsolete design
    // { value: 'shop', label: 'Магазин' }    — an obsolete design
    available: null, // A list with product stock (object|null). Leave null when generating
    availopt: { // The field names of the stock list (used if available is set)
      itemid: string, // The name of the "Product ID" field (by default 'ID Товара')
      sizeid: string, // The name of the "Size ID" field (by default 'ID Размера')
      colorid: string, // The name of the "Colour ID" field (by default 'ID Цвета')
      quantid: string // The name of the "Stock" field (by default 'Остаток')
    },
    themecolors: {
      bgr: string, // Background colour (HEX, for example '#FFAA2C' for cafe, '#111111' for shop)
      text: string, // Text colour (HEX, for example '#FFFFFF')
      header: string, // Header colour (HEX, for example '#FFAA2C')
      headertext: string, // Header text colour (HEX, for example '#FFFFFF')
      price: string, // Price colour (HEX, for example '#FFAA2C' for cafe, '#111111' for shop)
      oldprice: string, // Old price colour (HEX, for example 'grey')
      title: string, // Product name colour (HEX, for example 'black')
      descr: string, // Description colour (HEX, for example '#666')
      code: string, // Article colour (HEX, for example '#999')
      catname: string // Catalog name colour (HEX, for example '#111111', only for theme: 'shop'/'shopux')
    },
    btn: {
      bgr: string, // Button background colour (HEX, for example '#FFAA2C' for cafe, '#222222' for shop)
      text: string // Button text colour (HEX, for example '#FFFFFF')
    },
    backbtn: {
      bgr: string, // Back button background colour (HEX, for example '#3A3B43')
      text: string // Back button text colour (HEX, for example '#FFFFFF')
    },
    text: {
      finish: string, // The text of the "Done", "OK", "Back", "Return to the bot" button (maxlength: 256)
      code: string, // The text "Article" (maxlength: 256)
      order: string, // The text "Cart" (maxlength: 256)
      total: string, // The text "Total" (maxlength: 256)
      emptyOrder: string, // The text "The cart is empty" (maxlength: 256)
      closeOrder: string, // The text "Close" in the cart (maxlength: 256)
      deleteOrder: string, // The text "Delete" in the cart (maxlength: 256)
      catadd: string, // The text "Add" in the catalog (maxlength: 256)
      catshowitem: string, // The text "View" in the catalog (maxlength: 256)
      empty: string, // The text "No products" (maxlength: 256)
      catname: string, // The text "Catalog name" (maxlength: 256, only for theme: 'shop')
      description: string, // The text "Description" in the card (maxlength: 256)
      sizes: string, // The text "Size" or any product parameter, for example "Volume" (maxlength: 256)
      colors: string, // The text "Colour" or any product parameter, for example "Volume" (maxlength: 256)
      closeFull: string, // The text "Close" in the card (maxlength: 256)
      addOrder: string, // The text "Add to cart" (maxlength: 256)
      noSize: string, // The text "Choose a size" (maxlength: 256)
      noColor: string, // The text "Choose a colour" (maxlength: 256)
      noPieces: string, // The text "The maximum quantity of the product available for order: " (maxlength: 256)
      search: string, // The text "Search" (maxlength: 256)
      searchplaceholder: string, // The text "Search" in the field (maxlength: 256)
      searchempty: string, // The text "Nothing found" (maxlength: 256)
      filter: string, // The text "Filter" (maxlength: 256)
      sort: string, // The text "Sort" (maxlength: 256)
      sortasc: string, // The text "Price ascending" (maxlength: 256)
      sortdesc: string, // The text "Price descending" (maxlength: 256)
      nosort: string, // The text "No sorting" (maxlength: 256)
      filtercancel: string, // The text "Reset" in the filter (maxlength: 256)
      filtersave: string, // The text "Apply" in the filter (maxlength: 256)
      pricefilter: string, // The text "Price range" (maxlength: 256)
      currency: string, // The currency text (maxlength: 256, for example '₽')
      quantity: string // The product unit text (maxlength: 256, for example 'pcs')
    },
    search: boolean, // Turning on search in the app (true/false)
    filters: boolean, // Turning on the filter and sorting (true/false)
    itemsizes: [
      {
        id: string, // A unique size identifier (generated as a randomstring)
        title: string // The size name (maxlength: 256, for example "S", "M", "L")
      }
    ], // ATTENTION: the elements have the fields id and title (not value/label)
    itemcolors: [
      {
        id: string, // A unique colour identifier (generated as a randomstring)
        title: string // The colour name (maxlength: 256, for example "Red", "Blue")
      }
    ], // ATTENTION: the elements have the fields id and title (not value/label)
    description: string, // A general description for all products (maxlength: 4000)
    descrlim: number // A limit on the description length in the product list (for example, 100)
  }
}

App type: cal (Booking calendar).

{
  settings: {
    token: string, // The bot token for launching from the menu button (maxlength: 256, optional), mandatory for the MAX messenger
    theme: { value: string, label: string }, // The theme. By default { value: 'calux', label: 'Дизайн 1' }
      // Options: { value: 'calux', label: 'Дизайн 1' }, { value: 'cal', label: 'Дизайн 2' }
    singledate: boolean, // Choosing one date (true) or an interval (false)
    settime: boolean, // Turning on the time choice (true/false, only if singledate: true)
    setdateinfinite: boolean, // Unlimited bookings for one date (true/false)
    settimeinfinite: boolean, // Unlimited bookings for one time (true/false, only if settime: true)
    maxperslot: string, // The maximum bookings for one time (a string, for example '2', if settimeinfinite: true)
    endbuffer: string, // The time until the end of the working day in minutes (a string, for example '60')
    text: {
      finish: string, // The text of the "Done", "OK", "Back", "Return to the bot" button (maxlength: 256)
      headersingle: string, // The text "Choosing a single date" (maxlength: 256)
      headertime: string, // The text "Choosing a time" (maxlength: 256)
      headerintfirst: string, // The text "Choosing the start date" (maxlength: 256)
      headerintsecond: string, // The text "Choosing the end date" (maxlength: 256)
      btnsingle: string, // The text of the single date confirmation button (maxlength: 256)
      btnintfirst: string, // The text of the start date confirmation button (maxlength: 256)
      btnintsecond: string, // The text of the end date confirmation button (maxlength: 256)
      dateerror: string // The text "The date is unavailable" (maxlength: 256)
    },
    btn: {
      bgr: string, // Button background colour (HEX, for example '#1677FF')
      text: string // Button text colour (HEX, for example '#FFFFFF')
    },
    backbtn: {
      bgr: string, // Back button background colour (HEX, for example '#3A3B43')
      text: string // Back button text colour (HEX, for example '#FFFFFF')
    },
    themecolors: {
      bgr: string, // Background colour (HEX, for example '#3A3B43')
      text: string, // Text colour (HEX, for example '#FFFFFF')
      elem: string, // Main element colour (HEX, for example '#1677FF')
      elemtext: string, // Header text colour (HEX, for example '#444')
      date: string, // Colour of the selected date and time (HEX, for example '#1677FF')
      dateaccepted: string, // Colour of the confirmed date (HEX, for example '#FFFFFF')
      time: string // Colour of the time in the schedule (HEX, for example '#FFFFFF')
    },
    workingtime: [
      {
        days: [string], // An array of weekdays
        // Possible values (the engine's Russian day codes, write them exactly): 'пн', 'вт', 'ср', 'чт', 'пт', 'сб', 'вс'
        time: [
          {
            hour: string, // Hours (for example, '09', '14')
            // Possible values: '00' - '23'
            minute: string // Minutes (for example, '00', '30')
            // Possible values: '00' - '59'
          }
        ]
      }
    ]
  }
}

A comment on the cal app type:
If you want to take the duration of services into account, pass the GET parameter duration in the app link, meaning the required time in minutes to provide the services (duration=60)
If you need to limit the number of booking days from the current date, pass the GET parameter freedays with the number of days in the app link (freedays=30)
If you need to make only today's date available for booking, pass the GET parameter todayonly with the value true in the app link (todayonly=true)
If you need to make (even/odd) hours available, use the GET parameter freetime. Possible values: even — even hours are available, odd — odd hours are available.
Also for freetime. Give a value in the format "workers_n_m_k". n — the number of workers, m — the interval in hours, k — the worker's number
Also for freetime. Give a value in the format "x_y". x — the interval of busy hours, y — the interval of free hours, in hours
Also for freetime. Give a number of hours x that will be busy. The next hour will be free. Equivalent to "x_1"

App type: own (Open a website inside Telegram).

{
  settings: {
    url: string, // The URL of the external app (mandatory, must not contain 'quescha.com/webapp'). It can also contain a variable holding a link from a scenario, for example the variable for a payment link #{paylink}
    token: string // The bot token for launching from the menu button (maxlength: 256, optional)
  }
}

App type: miniapp (Telegram mini app).

{
  settings: {
    miniappcode: string, // The HTML/JavaScript code of the Telegram mini app
    devtech: string, // A service field for development, '' (an empty string) by default
    token: string // The bot token for launching from the menu button (maxlength: 256, optional), mandatory for the MAX messenger
  }
}

A comment on the miniapp app type:
You can use your own mini app in Quescha constructor bots. Put the HTML/Javascript code into the field below, and specify the app link in a "link" type button or in the bot settings in @BotFather.
IMPORTANT: For the bot scenario to continue, data must be sent back to the bot at the URL https://api.quescha.com/miniapp/callback by the POST or GET method. Besides the data from your app, the quescha object MUST be sent. The quescha object is created outside the mini app and must not be overridden; send it as it is — quescha or window.quescha.
After the data is sent, your bot gets the string webapp:miniapp as the answer. If you do not use buttons under messages, take care of processing this answer in every block, for example by adding the condition '[Answer]' = 'webapp:miniapp'.
All the app data becomes variables in the bot scenario by the passed keys, and you can process them as you wish.
The app can also exchange data with the constructor through a webhook. In this case you can continue the bot scenario through an action scenario by sending messages to the bot. To set up a webhook, add an action scenario and the action "Data exchange"->"Waiting for data". Then build the needed data format, for example JSON with the "JS code" action, and put it into the variable _response, which is returned as the answer to the app's webhook call.
TWO COMMUNICATION CHANNELS OF AN APP. The difference is NOT that one reads and the other writes —
both can do both. The difference is whether the exchange touches the dialog with the bot:
  1) /miniapp/callback — MOVES the bot scenario: the data becomes variables,
     and the bot continues the dialog. It fits where "filled in a form → the bot answered".
  2) an action scenario webhook — works with the data SILENTLY, the bot writes nothing.
     It fits a personal account: the client browses, changes, saves, and at
     the next opening the app reads the current data through the webhook again.
Running account actions through /miniapp/callback is forbidden — the bot would poke
the client with a message on every click.

THE quescha OBJECT IS PASSED TO THE WEBHOOK TOO, not only to /miniapp/callback.
It is created by the constructor and contains webappid, channelid and clientid.
The app must send it in every request to the webhook.

WHO MADE THE REQUEST — FROM THE quescha OBJECT. The constructor checks it and raises
the subscriber, so in a scenario on a webhook the ordinary templates work:
[ClientID], [Messenger], [BotID], [FullName], [Self], [Balance]. There are no separate
variables for this — the templates are the same in all scenarios, and
parsing the request JSON for the sake of the ClientID is not needed.
If the app did not send the quescha object or the webhook is called by a third-party
service, there is no subscriber at all and the templates give an empty string.

A FILTER BY ClientID IS MANDATORY. The lists a personal account works with
must carry the ClientID column, and reading them must go with the filter
{ pole: "ClientID", value: "[ClientID]" }. Without this filter one account
shows the data of all clients at once. This is a correctness condition, not a wish.

THE APP'S REQUEST LIES IN _request. The body of the call comes as a JSON string
in the variable _request — parse it with the "JS code" action. Neither the operation itself
(show, add, change, delete) nor the row fields get into variables by
themselves: this is the difference from /miniapp/callback, where the data becomes
variables by its keys.

PUT THE WEBHOOK ADDRESS AS A MARKER. The real address is issued to an action scenario only
after the bot is installed, so in the app code write a marker with the scenario's NAME,
and the constructor replaces it with the address on installation:

  {{webhook:App data}}

The name inside the marker must match the name of the action scenario exactly
— the same one that stands as the first argument of scenario(...). The marker can be
put anywhere in the code, usually into a variable at the very top:

  const API = "{{webhook:App data}}";

Do not invent the address yourself, do not leave an empty string and do not ask the client
to enter the address by hand: with the marker everything is substituted by itself. If the names
do not match, the marker stays in the code as it is — this is visible at once.

BEFORE THE CALL CHECK THAT THE MARKER WAS REPLACED. An unreplaced marker is
not an address, and a request by it goes to the app page, not to the webhook:
the server answers with markup, the app cannot parse it and shows
the client an obscure error. One line at the start of the request function removes
this whole path:

  if (!API || String(API).indexOf("http") !== 0) {
    return { ok: false, error: "the data scenario is not connected to the app" };
  }

THE ANSWER ON WRITING. If the app saves something through the webhook, return
the result in _response — "saved", a new id, an updated set. Without
_response the webhook returns an empty object with code 200, and the app cannot tell
success from silence.

SHOW A REQUEST ERROR WITH DETAILS. The answer comes as text, and parsing
it as JSON does not always work: the address may not have been found, the server may have answered
with an error page. A short word like "bad_json" is useless in such a case —
it does not tell what happened. Show the answer code and the beginning
of the body together with it:

  var res = await fetch(API, { method: "POST", headers: { "Content-Type": "application/json" }, body: JSON.stringify(payload) });
  var text = ((await res.text()) || "").trim();
  try { return JSON.parse(text); }
  catch (e) { return { ok: false, error: "the answer was not parsed (code " + res.status + "): " + text.slice(0, 120) }; }

The same for a network failure: write the error text, not the single word "network".

IMPORTANT: The whole work of the app depends on you. You can also create the app with the help of AI models yourself, by correctly formulating the requirements for the app and its sending and receiving of data.

==================================================================================
THE CONNECTEDNESS OF THE SUBSCRIBER'S PATH
==================================================================================

A bot is built not from blocks but from a path. A meaningful ending is legitimate:
"the request is accepted, a manager will contact you" is a normal end of a conversation. What breaks
is not the end but a gap: the subscriber answered, and nobody listened; a check
needs data that was not collected on this path; a branch answered and left
the person without a single button. There is no error anywhere: the bot just
stays silent, and the client leaves.

Before outputting the bot, walk the path with the subscriber's eyes from every entry
(the chain launch, every menu button, the return from a mini app, a notification
to an employee) and check six things.

1. EVERY COLLECTED ANSWER LEADS SOMEWHERE.
   A block that asks — asks a question, saves the answer into answervar
   or checks it (number, phone, email, regex) — must have
   a continuation: a transition in conditionlist, a button to a block or a block launch
   from a scenario (exit + exittype startmes + startmesnum). Without it
   the subscriber answers into the void and stays on the same step.

2. THE "NO DATA" BRANCH LEADS TO WHERE THE DATA APPEARS.
   A check like "the client is not in the database", "the booking was not found", "no services
   chosen" must not just end the scenario. It must lead
   to the step that collects this data: a form, a choice of services, entering a number.
   Otherwise you get a loop: check → refusal → menu → the same check.
   When leading to such a step, do not attach YOUR OWN message to the transition: it
   overrides the target block's answer together with its buttons. Write the reason
   into a variable, and the target block's scenario shows it.
   And conversely: if there is nowhere in the bot to fill the variable, such
   a check loops the subscriber forever.

3. THE DATA IS THERE ON EVERY PATH, NOT ON THE MAIN ONE.
   If several roads lead into a block, the scenario on it gets the variables
   only from the road it came by. This especially concerns the return
   from a mini app: every block catches it, so one can get into checkout
   bypassing the choice. Either lead into such a block by one path, or
   put an emptiness check with a return to the needed step at the start of the scenario.

4. THE SCENARIO'S ANSWER CARRIES NAVIGATION.
   An exit message without buttons fits only where the block itself
   has buttons: the scenario's buttons are added to the block's buttons. If
   the block has none, each branch with a message must give exitbtn or
   a transition to a block. A frequent error: the "not found" branch has buttons,
   while the "done" branch does not, and the successful path breaks off.

5. NO DEAD SCENARIOS AND NO EMPTY VARIABLES.
   Every scenario must be called by someone: externalbefore of a block,
   externalafter of a transition or a mini app webhook. A helper scenario
   that "will come in handy" and that nobody calls does not get into the bot —
   it only confuses the owner.
   And conversely: if a scenario reads a #{variable}, find with your eyes the step
   that fills it — on EVERY path to this scenario. A helper
   waiting for variables from a caller but called by nobody is a double
   error: both dead and empty. It is simpler to write its work right where
   it is needed.

6. FROM ANY STEP THERE IS A RETURN TO THE BEGINNING.
   To the beginning of one's own chain: to the client — the main menu, to an employee — their
   account. A button to the start block or a nextdata button
   with the value /start fits: the engine resets the queue and launches the bot anew.
   An employee's account is home too, but if the employee is also a client,
   give them an exit from the account to the ordinary menu.

Channels without buttons (Avito, WhatsApp, marketplaces) are excluded from items 4 and 6:
the conversation goes by text there, there is no navigation at all.

==================================================================================
THE FINAL CHECKLIST BEFORE OUTPUTTING EACH PROPERTY
==================================================================================
1. Format: only the array is output (or false for groupnull), without a wrapper, without
   comments, without markdown, as one minified string.
2. All numeric ids are unique; the string ids of blocks are 8 characters [a-zA-Z0-9].
   In action scenarios (external) identifiers are NOT written at all —
   only labels and references by name through ctx.
3. All link fields (BotGroupId, BotChainId, ListId, WebAppId, WebAppRubricId)
   point to existing ids from the previously generated properties.
4. All nextMessage/nextmessage/wronganswer lead to existing blocks;
   the label in them matches the target's name.
4a. wronganswer is an OBJECT { label, value } or null. A string in this field
   does not work: the engine does not show it, and the bot stays silent on a wrong answer.
   Each check (number, phone, email, regex) has its own
   "not recognised" block with the buttons "Enter again" and "Main menu".
5. All externalbefore/externalafter point to the NAME of an existing
   action scenario — the one that stands as the first argument of scenario(...).
6. Each block's conditionlist contains ≥1 element.
   For external the checklist 3.9 applies instead.
7. Each branch of a block's conditionlist ends with a transition or an exit —
   no "hanging" branches. In action scenarios this is NOT required: a scenario
   can be a background one and end silently, without a message and a transition.
8. If a block's text has [Actions] — externalbefore is set and the scenario returns
   a message (exit:true, exittype:'back', exitmes filled in).
9. nextdata buttons are processed by conditions or by a transition to a handler block;
   for a transition after nextdata, nextmessage is set.
10. No blocks without incoming transitions. The exceptions — the start block, the payment
    acceptance block (acceptpayment), the block from exitanswer and a block that a
    button from an action scenario leads to (sendtobtn with nextmessage): such
    blocks are called not by a transition, so they do not count as "hanging".
11. Initialise every counter and accumulator to zero before the first addition:
    in listdata, in global or by the very first action of the scenario. An unknown
    variable is substituted with an empty string, and an empty string is added
    as text, not as a number: '' + 1 gives "1", then "11", then "111".
    Comparing such a counter is silent too: '' > 0 is false.
12. Each block that asks has a continuation: a transition, a button
    to a block or a block launch from a scenario. An answer that nobody accepts
    is a bot stop without an error.
13. The "no data" branch leads to the step where this data is collected, rather than just
    ending the scenario. There must be somewhere to fill the variable.
14. If different roads lead into a block, the scenario on it does not rely
    on the variables from one of them: either one path, or an emptiness check
    with a return to the needed step.
15. Each scenario branch that answers the subscriber has navigation —
    exitbtn or a transition to a block, if the owner block has no buttons of its own.
16. From any step one can return to the beginning of one's own chain: by a button to the start
    block or a nextdata button with the value /start. Channels without buttons
    are excluded from this item.
17. The first branch of a block's conditionlist is without a condition. The engine does not check
    a condition in the first branch: the subscriber would stand on this block forever.
18. JS code has not a single template [ ]: neither subscriber answers, nor dates,
    nor generators. Everything the code needs is put into variables a step earlier.
19. Each list read operation has the "save to variables" fields filled in,
    not only the counter of found rows.
20. A block with [Actions] that asks nothing is marked dontwaitanswer
    and leads to the next block.
21. If a scenario reads #{media_url}, the action "Receive
    and forward media" stands before it — without it the link is empty. And the file stays somewhere:
    either the action has a recipient (an employee or the administrator's ClientID/Messenger/BotID),
    or the same scenario sends the file to Yandex or Google
    Drive, and the link from there is written to the list. The copy on the server lives a minute.
22. Messages contain real line breaks, not the pair of characters "\n".
    Check the messages of actions: the exit, sending to the bot, to an employee, an email,
    the information for a calendar booking and a reminder.
23. In a document from a template the placeholder name is written without braces, each
    element has a value, the link is saved in doclinkvar, and the action name
    has a note for the owner to upload the template file or set its public link when doc.url is empty.
24. If there is more than one screen with value buttons, each parsing
    scenario also looks for the incoming value in another screen's list — and leads to
    where the subscriber aimed. Buttons of past messages stay working.
25. The number of rows is counted by the rows read (the array length),
    not by the filter counter: the engine puts "1" or "0" into it — only
    the flag "found", not a number.
26. A filter goes by the key of its own entity in its list: a class code — in the Code field
    of the schedule, a tariff name — in the Name field of the tariffs. Comparing keys
    of different entities is forbidden.
27. If the functionality goes beyond the constructor — output «Невозможно создать».
    If the topic is forbidden — output «Запрещено».
28. Each action has its own button value. Two buttons with the same
    value are one action: "Accept the request" and "Pass to the next one"
    with the value z~12 both just open card 12. The prefix says
    WHAT we do, not only with what: a~ accept, s~ pass, c~ close.
    And the parsing of the value in the scenario must know all the prefixes the
    bot sends — otherwise the number is not extracted.
29. Not a single scenario on a block transition (externalafter) if it must
    show something or lead to a screen with a scenario. Answer parsing is
    a separate technical block with [Actions] and a "before sending" scenario.
30. Each block has btnsettings for the bot's channels — not only those
    where buttons are set by hand: buttons also come from a scenario. Without the settings
    the keyboard in the messenger may not appear at all.
31. The scenario's buttons do not repeat the block's buttons: they are ADDED to them,
    and an identical "To menu" is shown twice.
32. A comparison with emptiness is written in quotes on both sides:
    '#{variable}' != ''. Without quotes on the left the branch works only while
    the variable holds one word of letters, and on an empty value it never
    works. And in quotes the comparison breaks if the value itself contains
    an apostrophe ('): then both "=" and "!=" give "false" — the branch is silently dead.
    Do not put people's text or the AI model's answer into a condition: write
    a flag '1'/'0' in a JS step and compare it.
33. JS code has no real line breaks inside quotes — only "\n",
    and variables are read through varstore['name'], not by the substitution #{name}.
    Either one cancels the execution of the WHOLE action, silently.
34. If the AI model assembles an order, its answer is JSON, and the items and prices
    are checked against the catalog by code. The sum is calculated by the bot. Order readiness
    is decided by the bot, not by the AI model.
35. If the bot answers with templates by the words of a question, the filter has BOTH
    checkboxes on — "Search by word stem" and "Reverse comparison" — and the list has
    one word per row. Synonyms and verb forms — as separate
    rows with the same answer.
36. Not a single key, token or password right in an action: they are substituted
    by a variable from the settings list. A personal account address in a URL is also
    a setting. Do not leave debug messages ("json: #{json}") in a ready bot.
37. If by the specification attempts go in a circle ("did not answer — to the next one, and so
    on"), the round is built with a delay FROM A MINUTE (it goes to the background), it
    has an exit condition that will certainly come, and the round's screen is marked
    "Replace the previous message".
38. In every filter the comparison sign is one of eight: =, !=, >, >=, <, <=,
    include, notinclude; in every sorting asc is true, false or
    "random". Neither neq, nor gt, nor desc, nor orderBy: the builder does not accept such a value
    and stops the whole scenario.
39. A JS code line that starts with "(" or "[" starts
    with ";": without it, it is glued to the previous line, and the action fails
    at run time.
